2026-11-05

Seeing Beyond the Next Step: World-Model-Guided Human-Like Navigation in Multi-Agent Scenes

Seeing Beyond the Next Step: World-Model-Guided Human-Like Navigation in Multi-Agent Scenes

Seong-Eun Hong、JuYeong Hwang、Jinhyun Kim、Hanyoung Jang、HyeongYeop Kang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Selecting a next motion in a populated scene is not just a matter of avoiding what is currently in the way: an action can be locally feasible yet drift onto the road instead of the crosswalk, cut into a stream of crossing pedestrians, or bottleneck a narrow passage. These failure modes are invisible from the current state alone, and acting on them requires reasoning about what each candidate action will cause. World models offer exactly this, namely action-conditioned predictions of the future, but rolling them out at every decision step is a cost deployable navigation systems cannot pay, and the field has largely traded foresight for speed. We argue this is a false dichotomy. The natural place for a world model in human-aware navigation is at training time, as a preference oracle that distills foresight into a fast policy, rather than at inference time, as an online planner. We pair a discrete visual action prior with two task-aligned world models, one for nearby social dynamics and one for future scene semantics, that score candidate actions by their predicted consequences and refine the policy through group-relative preference distillation. At deployment, both world models are removed and the policy acts in a single forward pass. Across five held-out evaluation scenarios, this yields navigation that is simultaneously safer, more socially and group-coherent, and more goal-directed than strong reactive and learned baselines, with naive observers rating the resulting motion as more human-like. Taken together, our findings point to a different approach for bringing world-model foresight into deployable agents: not accelerating rollouts, but amortizing them into the policy so that the cost of looking ahead is confined to training. Project page: https://seoneun.github.io/-NeurIPS-2026-Seeing-Beyond-the-Next-Step/

Across five held-out evaluation scenarios, this yields navigation that is simultaneously safer, more socially and group-coherent, and more goal-directed than strong reactive and learned baselines, with naive observers rating the resulting motion as more human-like. Taken together, our findings point to a different approach for bringing world-model foresight into deployable agents: not accelerating rollouts, but amortizing them into the policy so that the cost of looking ahead is confined to training. Project page: https://seoneun.github.io/-NeurIPS-2026-Seeing-Beyond-the-Next-Step/

Multimodal Information Interactions Bottleneck for Multimodal Self-Supervised Learning

Multimodal Information Interactions Bottleneck for Multimodal Self-Supervised Learning

Liangjian Wen、Jiangtao Zheng、Jiang Duan、Qun Dai、Yong Dai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multimodal self-supervised learning is challenged by heterogeneity, unequal informativeness, and complex information interactions across multimodal data sources such as images, text, audio, and tabular data. While contrastive methods effectively align shared semantics, they typically rely on strong multi-view redundancy assumptions, overlooking modality-specific and synergistic information, thus leading to suboptimal representations. We propose the Multimodal Information Interactions Bottleneck (MIIB), a principled framework grounded in Partial Information Decomposition that explicitly encodes redundant, unique, and synergistic information within multimodal interactions. MIIB employs a dual-path model architecture for modality-specific encoding and multimodal fusion, optimized using surrogate tractable entropy-based objectives that enforce minimal sufficient representations without requiring explicit mutual information estimation. By leveraging variational bounds and score-based entropy estimation, MIIB efficiently captures complex cross-modal interactions while suppressing superfluous information. Experiments across diverse benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over state-of-the-art multimodal self-supervised methods. Code is available at https://github.com/wanglingzaishi/MIIB.

Fast and Accurate Hand Estimation in Pixels, Interaction and World Space

Fast and Accurate Hand Estimation in Pixels, Interaction and World Space

Jinkun Cao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Monocular hand mesh estimators commonly predict rig parameters and project the mesh to obtain 2D keypoints. We find that directly predicted 3D joints agree better with 2D predictions, and fitting a mesh to predicted points reduces mesh error. We present FAH (Fast and Accurate Hand Estimation), a family of models built on SAM 3D Body's hand branch through fine-tuning and feature distillation. The models predict corresponding 2D and 3D joints and surface points, fit MANO or MHR-Hand, and optionally estimate anatomical parts and contact without object geometry at inference. Causal refinement reduces acceleration error, and camera estimation places the hands in world space. Experiments show improvements in image-space alignment, hand geometry, contact estimation and world-space tracking over the evaluated baselines. The default ViT-L model runs at 65 fps with mesh fitting on an RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, or 49 fps with parts and contact enabled. The smallest ViT-S model reaches 125 fps without mesh fitting, parts or contact. Code: https://github.com/noahcao/FAH.

ReferClaw: Disambiguating User Intent for Egocentric Assistance

ReferClaw: Disambiguating User Intent for Egocentric Assistance

Hangyu Qin、Junbin Xiao、Angela Yao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Users of egocentric AI assistants often ask short, ambiguous questions whose intended referents cannot be uniquely resolved from video and dialogue history. For example, "Where did I leave it?" may refer to several previously handled objects. Existing VideoQA systems typically commit to a single interpretation, risking plausible but intention-misaligned answers. We introduce ReferClaw, an agentic framework that formulates egocentric streaming QA as an interactive process: the assistant asks clarifying questions to resolve user intent before answering. Three cooperating agents identify candidate interpretations, request clarification, and generate answers grounded in the resolved intent and visual evidence. A bounded, cost-aware policy balances expected information gain against user effort while limiting clarification turns and question complexity. Referclaw learns from interaction through intent-specific skill updates and process-reward-guided policy optimization with agent-level credit assignment. Evaluations on augmented egocentric QA benchmarks demonstrate improved answer accuracy and joint answer-and-grounding performance over existing baselines, alongside zero-shot transfer across datasets. These findings support interactive clarification as a practical approach to resolving online user intent in egocentric streaming assistance.

These findings support interactive clarification as a practical approach to resolving online user intent in egocentric streaming assistance.

Neural Image Compression with Internal-External Prior Fusion and Alignment

Neural Image Compression with Internal-External Prior Fusion and Alignment

Tiancheng Si、Wenlong Xu、Dahua Gao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Existing context models for learned image com- pression rely primarily on internal priors from the current image, overlooking common patterns and typical textures shared across natural images. To address this limitation, we propose an Internal-External Prior Fusion-Alignment (IE-PFA) context model built upon quadtree spatial-channel joint modeling, where a learnable dictionary shared by the encoder and decoder serves as an external prior. Specifically, an RWKV-based Spatial- Channel Prior Fusion (RSCPF) module efficiently fuses inter- nal and external priors along spatial and channel dimensions with linear complexity, while a training-only Self-Distilled Prior Alignment (SDPA) mechanism aligns them without inference overhead. Moreover, our model supports variable-rate coding over 64 quality levels within a single network and is accelerated by custom CUDA operators. Experiments on seven benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art rate-distortion performance, achiev- ing an average BD-Rate saving of 21.65% over VTM-22.0-Intra, with only 29.9M parameters and the fastest coding speed among advanced neural codecs (61 ms encoding and 52 ms decoding). Code is available at https://github.com/stcnjtech/DCVC-X-Intra.

Subspace Learning with Outlier Exposure for Out-of-Distribution Detection

Subspace Learning with Outlier Exposure for Out-of-Distribution Detection

Hongxu Shen、Kun Fang、Jiahui Zheng、Jian Sun、Qinghua Tao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Out-of-Distribution (OoD) detection is pivotal for reliable deployments of Deep Neural Networks (DNNs), aiming to distinguish anomalous samples from the In-Distribution (InD) data in training. By introducing outliers as surrogate OoD data to finetune DNNs, Outlier Exposure (OE) has greatly advanced OoD detection in recent years. Existing OE-based research remains predominantly logit-centric, either by generating diverse outliers or by regularizing them with objectives operating at the prediction-output level. Nonetheless, these approaches leave much of the rich information in feature spaces untapped. In this work, we propose an OE-based detection framework, named RecOE, that learns appropriate feature subspaces from InD data and auxiliary outliers, such that the InD-OoD discrepancy can be effectively characterized through the learned feature subspaces. Specifically, the feature subspaces are optimized to capture the dominant variations of InD and outliers by minimizing reconstruction errors therein. Besides, a sample-level objective discourages reconstruction in the opposing subspace, while an orthogonality regularizer reduces subspace overlap, further promoting discriminative subspaces. At inference, the difference in reconstruction errors between the learned subspaces serves as the OoD detection score. Extensive experiments show the superiority of RecOE on multiple benchmarks, in which significant performance gains are achieved over recent strong baselines with only slight extra overhead from two small-size matrices of subspace bases. The source code is available at https://github.com/shenhongxu123/RecOE.

The source code is available at https://github.com/shenhongxu123/RecOE.

Improving Long-Tail Detection via Layout-Instruction Conditioned Diffusion Synthesis

Improving Long-Tail Detection via Layout-Instruction Conditioned Diffusion Synthesis

Xinyu Zhang、Xiaodi Wang、Yanpeng Sun、Lingfeng Yang、Jingdong Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Synthetic data from diffusion models can improve object detection, but long-tail performance is still limited by scarce rare classes and inflexible conditioning (e.g., text-only or layout-only inputs). In this paper, we propose Tail-SD, a layout-instruction conditioned stable diffusion framework that supports text, layouts, or their hybrid, enabling controllable synthesis from boxes, masks, and/or prompts to better cover the long-tail objects. To train a single model across heterogeneous conditions, we introduce a multi-task cross-attention module and multi-task embedding tokens, improving generation quality and diversity. Extensive experiments on COCO and LVIS demonstrate that diffusion synthesis with hybrid conditioning consistently benefits detection, e.g., on LVIS val, it achieves +4.89% / +5.68% rare Box/Mask AP, highlighting strong potential for addressing long-tail challenges.

2026-11-05

OptIMerge: Optimization-Based Merging of Diffusion Models

OptIMerge: Optimization-Based Merging of Diffusion Models

Viraj Shah、Ashwini Pokle、Peyman Milanfar、Mauricio Delbracio、Hossein Talebi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The arithmetic merging of weights from multiple task-specific models fine-tuned from a shared base model, commonly known as model souping, has emerged as a popular strategy for multi-task learning without the computational overhead of joint retraining. However, naive scalar weight averaging frequently leads to severe performance degradation when the constituent models learn structurally divergent feature representations, leading to destructive parameter interference. We study this problem in a largely unexplored setting: merging image-to-image (I2I) diffusion models fine-tuned for dense, pixel-accurate tasks such as image restoration. Unlike the large language models (LLMs) and text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models for which most merging methods were developed, I2I experts encode conflicting spatial priors (e.g., anisotropic motion-blur vs. isotropic defocus-blur) in their convolutional filters, where both naive soup and LLM-style element-wise merging introduce visible artifacts. We introduce OptIMerge, a gradient-based optimization framework: instead of relying on heuristic scalars, OptIMerge learns highly expressive, yet parameter-efficient merger coefficients by minimizing a multi-task diffusion loss over a small set of 100-500 images. OptIMerge parameterizes the merge with architecture-aware transformations: for attention layers, a structured low-rank feature alignment with explicit channel biases; for convolutional layers, per-kernel merger coefficients, and a low-rank Cayley transform that aligns channels by orthogonal rotation without distorting the filters. By keeping the experts frozen and optimizing only these lightweight merger parameters (equivalent to <0.4% of the network footprint), OptIMerge achieves simultaneous multi-task capabilities that rival computationally expensive joint training and decisively outperform zero-shot model soups.

2026-11-05

Reviving the Discarded High-Resolution Feature for Transformer-Based Tiny Object Detection

Reviving the Discarded High-Resolution Feature for Transformer-Based Tiny Object Detection

Zehao Wang、Si Wu、Guiping Cao、Xin Li、Yaowei Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Tiny object detection remains challenging because tiny instances occupy only a few pixels, and their fine-grained details are easily lost during feature downsampling. Although the highest-resolution feature preserves rich local details for tiny objects, DETR-like detectors often discard it due to its large computational cost, while directly reintroducing this feature also brings dense background noise. To address this problem, we propose ReDHF, a novel framework that Revives the Discarded High-resolution Feature for tiny object detection. ReDHF has three cooperative components. Cross-Scale Deformable Fusion (CSDF) first constructs a semantically enhanced highest-resolution feature and injects its fine-grained details into the shallow feature under a moderate cost. Ellipse-Guided Shallow Supervision (EGSS) then guides the enhanced feature to learn more reliable classification and localization priors by applying geometry-adaptive auxiliary supervision. Full-Scale Query Interaction (FSQI) further enables decoder queries to access both low-level details and high-level semantic information, allowing them to aggregate richer cues for tiny objects. Extensive experiments show that ReDHF consistently improves multiple baselines across different DETR variants and achieves state-of-the-art performance on the evaluated benchmarks. The best ReDHF variants surpass their baselines by +1.4 AP on AI-TOD-v2, +1.6 AP on VisDrone, and +1.5 AP on SODA-D, with especially notable gains on tiny and small objects.

2026-11-05

Flux as a Codec: Zero-Shot Lossy Compression with Flux

Flux as a Codec: Zero-Shot Lossy Compression with Flux

Fuma Kimishima、Jinjia Zhou

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We propose FluxC, a zero-shot codec that uses Flux as a compression prior. Although DiffC has shown that pretrained diffusion models can serve as zero-shot codecs, we ask how the choice of coding path and reconstruction method affects rate--distortion--perception behavior. We analyze FluxC along three axes: coding cost and bit allocation, reconstruction method, and low rate distortion. At matched signal-to-noise ratio (SNR), we show that the path choice does not affect the ideal information rate, but changes how bits are allocated across time steps. For reconstruction, we compare ancestral sampling, one-step ODE reconstruction, and many-step ODE reconstruction. Our analysis shows that ODE-based reconstruction can achieve lower distortion than ancestral sampling, especially in the low rate regime. Experiments on Gaussian sources and natural images support this prediction. Moreover, FluxC performs competitively with, and in some cases outperforms, recent neural image compression methods, suggesting that Flux can serve as a principled zero-shot codec.

StitchEdit: Stitching Depth Priors into Image Editors via Per-Layer Gradient Probing

StitchEdit: Stitching Depth Priors into Image Editors via Per-Layer Gradient Probing

Qin Guo、Dan Xu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Text-guided image editing with diffusion models struggles with spatial edits (e.g. manipulating objects, changing viewpoints, manipulating occlusions) that depend on the 3D structure understanding of a scene. Marigold and follow-up work demonstrate that a pretrained diffusion backbone already harbors strong geometric priors that can be activated for depth estimation, yet how to redirect these priors toward editing remains unresolved. Through per-layer gradient probing, we discover that the editing and depth objectives are compatible in early transformer layers but actively conflict in late layers, explaining why naive depth-conditioned editing either ignores geometry or degrades visual quality. We present StitchEdit, which first activates the backbone's geometric capability via a lightweight depth-denoising adapter, then selectively fine-tunes this adapter for editing using a probe-guided per-layer learning rate schedule that preserves depth knowledge where it helps and prunes it where it conflicts. On the proposed StitchEditBench, a benchmark of 150 geometry-dependent edits spanning spatial transformation, occlusion manipulation, and viewpoint change, StitchEdit consistently surpasses both instruction-based and depth-conditioned baselines on perceptual quality and geometric fidelity.

2026-11-05

PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State

PlayGen2: Learning Real-Time Playable Worlds with Explicit 3D State

Jihai Zhang、Yuhan Zhou、Junyou Li、Deheng Ye、Qi Dou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Building a playable world requires more than generating plausible video: it demands responsive control, persistent spatial structure, and coherent interactions with dynamic entities. Using generated frames as the sole recurrent world representation ties these requirements to view-dependent appearance synthesis, making it difficult to maintain long-term spatial and temporal consistency in generation and interaction. We present PlayGen2, a real-time interactive world model that separates learned 3D state evolution from neural rendering. PlayGen2 maintains a persistent world memory comprising static geometry represented by a volumetric occupancy grid and identity-indexed 3D states for the player and other entities. Within a fixed-size local region, its world-state model jointly learns action-conditioned entity dynamics, geometry completion, and entity discovery, committing updates to a persistent world memory. A separate neural renderer synthesizes first-person observations from depth and semantic projections of the world state, without feeding generated images back into state prediction. Using Doom as a testbed, we demonstrate that the model successfully learns world dynamics including geometry-aware player motion and player--NPC interactions. The framework is computationally efficient, enabling the complete simulation and rendering pipeline to achieve approximately 160ms backend latency on a MacBook Pro with an M4 Pro chip. Beyond single-player interaction, we demonstrate zero-shot deployment on unseen Doom maps, multiplayer interaction through the composition of single-player models, and programmable gameplay through rule-based state interventions. These capabilities demonstrate the transferability and extensibility of the learned game-world model. Code and pretrained model weights are publicly available.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-11-05

STAG-Net: Structure-Aware Attention over Landmark Groups for Lightweight Head Pose Estimation

STAG-Net: Structure-Aware Attention over Landmark Groups for Lightweight Head Pose Estimation

Asiri Gawesha Lindamulage、Sanka Mohottala、Dharshana Kasthurirathna、Nuwan Kodagoda

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Head pose estimation is important for human computer interaction and driver monitoring, but many image based methods remain too costly for resource constrained deployment. We propose STAG-Net, a structure-aware attention over landmark groups for lightweight head Pose estimation that estimates head pose from facial landmarks using movement aware selection, spatial clustering, compact DeepSet encoders, self attention, and a global geometry descriptor. We develop variants using FaceMesh and Face Alignment Network landmarks and evaluate them under cross dataset and subject independent protocols on 300W-LP, AFLW2000, and BIWI. STAG-Net achieves the lowest mean absolute error among all compared landmark based methods in every evaluated setting. On BIWI frames with valid detections, the FAN variant also records the lowest overall error among all compared methods, reaching $3.51^\circ$ under cross dataset evaluation and $2.39^\circ$ under subject independent evaluation. The regressors use only 0.012 to 0.043 million parameters and 24.1 to 83.6 thousand FLOPs, about three orders of magnitude fewer FLOPs than FSA-Net. Despite including landmark extraction and regression, the FaceMesh pipeline reaches 322.9 frames per second on a Tesla T4 GPU, nearly twice the 164.5 frames per second of the fastest measured image based baseline, and 111.0 frames per second on CPU. After normalization by dataset size, STAG-Net processes training samples seven to eight times faster than FSA-Net and more than one hundred times faster than HopeNet and TokenHPE, while complete training finishes within 28 minutes. These results show that STAG-Net combines leading landmark based accuracy with substantially lower training and inference cost.

STAG-Net achieves the lowest mean absolute error among all compared landmark based methods in every evaluated setting. On BIWI frames with valid detections, the FAN variant also records the lowest overall error among all compared methods, reaching $3.51^\circ$ under cross dataset evaluation and $2.39^\circ$ under subject independent evaluation. The regressors use only 0.012 to 0.043 million parameters and 24.1 to 83.6 thousand FLOPs, about three orders of magnitude fewer FLOPs than FSA-Net. Despite including landmark extraction and regression, the FaceMesh pipeline reaches 322.9 frames per second on a Tesla T4 GPU, nearly twice the 164.5 frames per second of the fastest measured image based baseline, and 111.0 frames per second on CPU. After normalization by dataset size, STAG-Net processes training samples seven to eight times faster than FSA-Net and more than one hundred times faster than HopeNet and TokenHPE, while complete training finishes within 28 minutes. These results show that STAG-Net combines leading landmark based accuracy with substantially lower training and inference cost.

目标检测/分割生成模型/扩散医学图像
2026-11-05

CMDiff: Left Atrial Scar Segmentation from 3D LGE MRI Using Conditional Mask Diffusion Model: A Feasibility Study

CMDiff: Left Atrial Scar Segmentation from 3D LGE MRI Using Conditional Mask Diffusion Model: A Feasibility Study

Jing Zhang、Bastien Bergere、Jonas Leite、Emilie Bollache、Mikaël Laredo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Late gadolinium enhancement (LGE) cardiac MRI enables non-invasive assessment of left atrial (LA) scar, an imaging marker associated with atrial fibrillation ablation outcome. However, automatic scar segmentation is hindered by low contrast, the thin atrial wall, scarce labels, and extreme class imbalance. Conditional diffusion offers iterative mask generation, but its suitability for sparse 3D LA scar segmentation remains unclear. To address this gap, we propose Conditional Mask Diffusion (CMDiff), which predicts clean masks using a dynamic-encoder-conditioned 3D sinusoidal UNet. We compared CMDiff with a deterministic baseline on identical cases in five-fold cross-validation of 60 scar-labelled public LASCARQS subjects, with 48 training and 12 held-out cases per fold. CMDiff achieved a scar DSC of 0.47 $\pm$ 0.08, HD95 of 12.89 $\pm$ 6.39 mm, and ASD of 1.91 $\pm$ 0.96 mm, compared with deterministic baseline values of 0.51 $\pm$ 0.08, 10.86 $\pm$ 5.39 mm, and 1.58 $\pm$ 1.03 mm, respectively. Under the same implementation, clean-mask prediction performed substantially better than the noise-prediction configuration evaluated in this study. Thus, CMDiff was technically feasible but did not improve segmentation performance: DSC was lower, and both HD95 and ASD were higher. Direct mask diffusion therefore provides an analytical alternative, but not a competitive performance advance for this task. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/jizhang02/CMDiff.

TraCE: Trajectory-Aware Concepts for Explainable Video Understanding

TraCE: Trajectory-Aware Concepts for Explainable Video Understanding

Wooil Lee、Junpyo Hong、Jongseo Lee、Tuomas Oikarinen、Tsui-Wei Weng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video understanding models often rely on motion, yet their explanations can obscure which motion sources support a prediction. We identify motion-source under-specification as a limitation of existing video XAI: region-centric explanations can be source-ambiguous, while motion vocabularies restricted to single-person pose sequences are source-incomplete. We propose TraCE (Trajectory-Aware Concepts for Explainable Video Understanding), a concept-based framework that discovers source-flexible motion concepts by clustering optical-flow trajectories from training videos. These concepts represent dynamic evidence from people, objects, and interactions, while object and scene concepts account for static context. The resulting typed trajectory/object/scene interface supports class prediction for action recognition and question-conditioned concept matching for video question answering (VQA). We evaluate TraCE across five random seeds on six benchmarks: NTU RGB+D Mutual, Chiral SSv2, UCF-101, KTH, TOMATO, and STAR. Relative to non-interpretable baselines, TraCE maintains competitive task performance, with an average accuracy drop of 1.0 points for action recognition and a nominal gain of 0.4 points for VQA. Motion perturbation and concept-removal tests provide complementary evidence of directional semantic alignment and decision faithfulness, while sample-level interventions illustrate targeted model debugging. Our project page is available at https://leewooil.github.io/TraCE_project_page/.

Category differences in similarity between fixation density maps and psychological potential fields for sketches

Category differences in similarity between fixation density maps and psychological potential fields for sketches

Naoyuki Awano、Yuki Hayashi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

When an object comes into view, humans can instantaneously recognize it if known to them. However, assessing how this recognition takes place remains challenging. A few studies using simple sketches have reported varying degrees of image similarity between the fixation density map (FDM), which visualizes eye movements and durations, and the psychological potential field (PPF), which visualizes part of the visual cognition process, depending on the object category. This study reports how the similarity behaves across three categories---animal, non-animal, and meaningless shape---and under rotated presentation, which is known to increase cognitive load during object recognition. The similarity was quantified through area under the curve (AUC) measures, including a baseline-adjusted AUC that isolates the early rise in similarity from stimulus-dependent baseline levels. A variability score was introduced to assess individual differences, and linear mixed models were used to evaluate category and orientation effects. Results indicated that the baseline-adjusted AUC yielded significant differences among all three categories with large effect sizes, whereas the raw AUC did not distinguish between the animal and non-animal categories due to the influence of sketch representation. No significant Category $\times$ Orientation interaction was observed, indicating that rotated presentation did not produce a category-specific effect. The meaningless shape stimuli yielded consistently low similarity and variability scores, clearly separating them from the two meaningful object categories. These results characterize the behavior of the FDM--PPF similarity under free viewing and indicate that baseline adjustment is necessary to separate category-related variation from stimulus-dependent baseline levels.

2026-11-04

VINO: Revisiting 3D Representation Learning with Volume Transformers

VINO: Revisiting 3D Representation Learning with Volume Transformers

Adrian Kruse、Kadir Yilmaz、Daan de Geus、Francis Engelmann、Bastian Leibe 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Self-supervised learning has given rise to vision foundation models that learn general-purpose image representations transferable across downstream tasks, yet representation learning for 3D scenes has not achieved comparable success. Motivated by this, we identify key limitations of current 3D representation learning approaches and revisit their core design choices. We first replace the commonly used hierarchical PTv3 backbone with the Volume Transformer, a ViT-like architecture for 3D scenes. We then redesign two core objectives of modern approaches built around student-teacher networks: matching representations across views and predicting masked content from visible context. For the first objective, we separate the roles of teacher and student: the teacher provides clean, consistent targets while the student learns invariance to stronger perturbations. For the second objective, we prevent the student from accessing the masked geometry and instead require it to infer missing representations from visible context only. Finally, we redesign 2D-to-3D knowledge distillation, replacing direct regression of view-dependent image features with view-consistent supervision. Together, these changes form VINO, a framework that learns semantic representations of 3D scenes without labels. VINO substantially improves dense linear probing across indoor and outdoor benchmarks and transfers strongly to out-of-domain settings.

Gaussians Can Subtract: Neural Dissolve Fields for Hybrid Scene Rendering

Gaussians Can Subtract: Neural Dissolve Fields for Hybrid Scene Rendering

Yiyan Wang、Haohan Liu、Tetsuo Shibuya

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Hybrid Mesh-Gaussian rendering combines compact textured surfaces with flexible 3D Gaussian primitives, offering an efficient alternative to purely Gaussian scene representations for structured environments. Existing scene-reconstruction hybrids commonly follow an additive formulation, where Gaussian residuals compensate for errors left by the mesh. However, not every Mesh-Gaussian conflict is caused by missing content: residual geometry errors, occlusion mismatch, texture-projection artifacts, and view-dependent appearance can make the same mesh content consistent in one view but inconsistent in another. We therefore formulate hybrid rendering as a multi-view inconsistency problem and present Gaussolve, a subtract-and-refill framework that selectively dissolves inconsistent mesh content before residual Gaussians refill the revealed regions. We introduce a lightweight Neural Dissolve Field, parameterized by scalar spherical-harmonic Gaussians, to learn where and from which viewing directions mesh content should be dissolved. A continuous transmittance fusion rule progressively exposes residual Gaussians behind the dissolved mesh, preserving their reconstruction gradients and avoiding hard visibility discontinuities. Experiments on structured and semi-structured scenes demonstrate higher visual fidelity with fewer Gaussian primitives and faster rendering. Project page: https://ooosn.github.io/gaussians-can-subtract/

T$^2$-Splat: Adaptive Topology Mesh Splatting with Texture Residuals

T$^2$-Splat: Adaptive Topology Mesh Splatting with Texture Residuals

Zhihao Tang、Youjia Zhang、Mingbo Zhao、Wei Yang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Triangle-based splatting connects novel view synthesis with conventional graphics pipelines, but often requires excessive tessellation to preserve appearance details. We present T$^2$-Splat, which decouples topology adaptation from appearance refinement. Safe Restricted Delaunay Triangulation (Safe RDT) initializes mesh connectivity. Dynamic Topology Evolution (DTE) then adapts triangle allocation through edge collapse and vertex split operations. Residual per-face Mip-textures then refine appearance on the fixed mesh using scale-adaptive filtering. Experiments on Mip-NeRF 360, Tanks & Temples, and DTU demonstrate competitive results with compact meshes. T$^2$-Splat improves PSNR by 0.71dB over MeshSplatting on Mip-NeRF 360. Additional compression experiments demonstrate flexible control of mesh complexity, supporting face budgets as low as 10% of the original face count. Project page: https://t2-splat.github.io/.

Comparing Classification and Feature Learning by Analyzing Their Loss Functions in Normalized Spaces with Different Scale Factors

Comparing Classification and Feature Learning by Analyzing Their Loss Functions in Normalized Spaces with Different Scale Factors

Qiufu Li、Huibin Xiao、Linlin Shen、Weijia Fan、Xi Jia 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Classification and feature learning are two closely interrelated fundamental tasks. Feature learning in supervised settings is typically achieved by training classification models, under the common assumption that higher classification accuracy implies better feature quality. In this paper, we first point out that classification accuracy reflects the sample-wise local properties of a model on a dataset, whereas feature learning aims to learn features with good dataset-level global properties, such as intra-class compactness and inter-class separability. From the first principle of feature learning, we redesign the binary cross-entropy (BCE) loss, a counterpart of the cross-entropy (CE) loss used in classification. Then, in normalized feature space, we analyze the minima of the BCE and CE losses and show that both can lead to neural collapse, where sample features from different classes become maximally compact and maximally separated. More importantly, by investigating the convergence behaviors of the two losses as the scale factor $γ$ varies in the normalized space, we reveal how classification accuracy and feature quality change differently across spaces of different sizes. Under a fixed training strategy, a very large $γ$ achieves perfect classification accuracy but slows convergence toward the ideal feature geometry, whereas a very small $γ$ makes classification highly sensitive to small perturbations. A moderate $γ$ is therefore necessary for simultaneously achieving reliable classification and well-structured features. Extensive experiments validate these findings and demonstrate that BCE loss exhibits advantages in long-tail and open-set recognitions, as it better aligns with the global requirements of feature learning.

UniGADD: Universal Generator-Agnostic Deepfake Detector

UniGADD: Universal Generator-Agnostic Deepfake Detector

Bernard Tomczyk、Agil Aghasanli、Plamen Angelov

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In this paper, we introduce Universal Generator-Agnostic Deepfake Detector (UniGADD), a novel method that employs supervised learning to achieve high accuracy on known deepfake generators, while maintaining robust performance on previously unseen ones. The proposed approach follows a two-stage optimisation process. In the first stage, a contrastive loss encourages the model to learn discriminative feature embeddings from real and known fake images, resulting in strong performance within the training domain. In the second stage, the embedding space is refined by promoting inter-cluster separation and intra-cluster compactness, applied exclusively to real samples. This refinement enhances generalisability, enabling the method to exhibit improved robustness against unseen deepfake generation techniques. UniGADD achieves accuracy on par with state-of-the-art methods for known generators, while significantly outperforming them on unseen cases, demonstrating its scalability and practical applicability for adversarial content detection.

TACT-KV: Tri-Axis Cosine Transform for Compressing Volumetric KV Caches in Medical VLMs

TACT-KV: Tri-Axis Cosine Transform for Compressing Volumetric KV Caches in Medical VLMs

Chongyu Qu、Ritchie Zhao、Yufan He、Dong Yang、Zhengyi Lu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language models (VLMs) applied to 3D medical scans face a structural memory wall. A single CT or MRI volume produces tens of thousands of vision tokens, and the resulting key-value (KV) cache scales linearly with both batch and context. Existing KV compression methods operate on the cache's feature or token statistics, or apply 1D and 2D transforms, leaving the three-axis spatial structure in the cache unexploited. We propose TACT-KV (Tri-Axis Cosine Transform), a calibration-free KV compression method that exploits strong spatial correlations in visual KV states along all three anatomical axes of 3D scans. This correlation concentrates the KV cache energy under a frequency-domain transform. TACT-KV applies a separable 3D discrete cosine transform to the KV cache, allocates a per-layer bit budget across frequency bins via reverse water-filling (more bits to high-energy components), and quantizes each bin with a fixed scalar codebook. We derive a close-form relation: the distortion reduction from adaptive bit allocation is dominated by the spectral concentration of the transformed cache. Across six medical VLMs and two 3D CT benchmarks, TACT-KV achieves the lowest 1-bit prediction-flip rate on eleven of twelve model and dataset pairs in our main comparison. The same advantage reproduces on four general-purpose VLMs evaluated on video tasks, indicating that the benefit of exploiting three-axis structure generalizes beyond medical imaging. At equal high-bandwidth memory (HBM), TACT-KV scales the decoding batch size on a single GPU by up to $8\times$.

2026-11-05

Guidance Is a Field, Not a Scalar: Interval-Integrated Classifier-Free Guidance for One-Step Video Generation

Guidance Is a Field, Not a Scalar: Interval-Integrated Classifier-Free Guidance for One-Step Video Generation

Zijie Meng、Xixin Cao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

One-step generators built on MeanFlow synthesize a video in a single function evaluation, yet inherit a rigid treatment of classifier-free guidance (CFG): the guidance scale is frozen during training or, at best, exposed as a single scalar. This conflicts with mounting evidence that the optimal guidance is not a constant but a field that varies with the noise level, with spatial content, and, for video, with frame position. Every existing remedy presupposes a multi-step sampler along which guidance can be re-scheduled--an assumption one-step models violate by construction. We resolve this tension through a single observation: because the MeanFlow target is an integral over a noise interval, an arbitrary guidance field can be absorbed into the target itself and compiled into one evaluation. We formalize this as the interval-integrated CFG identity, prove that the compiled guidance carries no schedule-discretization error and can thus match or exceed any finite multi-step sampler, and instantiate it as AGAVE, which equips the average-velocity field with a learnable temporal schedule, a free motion-saliency spatial modulation, and a frame-position asymmetry. Adding fewer than ten scalars at zero inference cost, AGAVE surpasses all one-step baselines on VBench, UCF-101 and MSR-VTT while matching multi-step guided sampling. Project repository: https://github.com/YMlinfeng/AGAVE.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散机器人视觉
2026-11-05

Action-Conditioned VLM Monitoring for Autonomous Vehicle Semantic Anomalies

Action-Conditioned VLM Monitoring for Autonomous Vehicle Semantic Anomalies

Daniel Adejumo、Omkar Ankalkope、Kunal Runwal、Aliasghar Arab

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We study action-conditioned monitoring of autonomous-vehicle behavior in semantically unusual driving scenes. The target is whether the executed ego action is appropriate for the scene: the same scene can admit both normal and anomalous behavior, so visual unusualness alone does not determine the monitoring label. A Vision-Language Model (VLM) first predicts the scene-required action from an outcome-hidden video window; the monitor then compares that expectation with the realized ego-motion trajectory under an explicit compatibility rule. To study this monitoring task, we contribute a six-field taxonomy of 112 paired scenarios spanning both failure polarities (42 derived from documented incidents), a generated video corpus with estimated trajectories, and an exploratory two-stage monitor on that corpus. On 235 clips from 15 scenario families, the two-stage protocol with a fixed T-2.5 s window obtains 83.0% accuracy and anomaly recall 0.773, versus 74.5% and 0.536 for a single-stage video-only baseline; specificity is 0.880 versus 0.928.

ArtiVidRM: Verifiable Artifact-as-Rubric Reward Modeling for Explicit Quality Articulation in Video Generation

ArtiVidRM: Verifiable Artifact-as-Rubric Reward Modeling for Explicit Quality Articulation in Video Generation

Caorui Li、Yangguang Ji、Shuxia Lin、Yu Chen、YiCheng Xiao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reinforcement learning has become an effective approach for improving video generation, but its success depends heavily on reliable reward models. Existing methods either map videos directly to opaque scalar scores or generate free-form rationales that are not explicitly optimized as the basis of the final judgment. We propose ArtiVidRM, a verifiable, artifact-as-rubrics video reward model that follows an observe-then-score paradigm. It first identifies visible generation artifacts and organizes them into a structured artifact list, then uses these artifacts as explicit rubrics for quality assessment. This intermediate representation grounds each score in concrete video-level evidence, making the reward more interpretable and informative for optimization. We further introduce a multi-reward reinforcement learning strategy to jointly optimize artifact prediction and quality assessment. The reward decomposes into complementary objectives that evaluate artifact prediction quality, including semantic coverage and prediction efficiency, together with a pairwise preference objective that supervises quality ranking through score differences. By jointly optimizing the intermediate artifact representation and the downstream preference prediction, the model learns to produce informative artifact lists while improving the accuracy of video quality assessment. Experiments show that ArtiVidRM achieves state-of-the-art performance on video preference benchmarks, provides effective signals for video diffusion optimization, and enables artifact-guided refinement of closed-source video generators.

Reinforcement learning has become an effective approach for improving video generation, but its success depends heavily on reliable reward models. Existing methods either map videos directly to opaque scalar scores or generate free-form rationales that are not explicitly optimized as the basis of the final judgment. We propose ArtiVidRM, a verifiable, artifact-as-rubrics video reward model that follows an observe-then-score paradigm. It first identifies visible generation artifacts and organizes them into a structured artifact list, then uses these artifacts as explicit rubrics for quality assessment. This intermediate representation grounds each score in concrete video-level evidence, making the reward more interpretable and informative for optimization. We further introduce a multi-reward reinforcement learning strategy to jointly optimize artifact prediction and quality assessment. By jointly optimizing the intermediate artifact representation and the downstream preference prediction, the model learns to produce informative artifact lists while improving the accuracy of video quality assessment. Experiments show that ArtiVidRM achieves state-of-the-art performance on video preference benchmarks, provides effective signals for video diffusion optimization, and enables artifact-guided refinement of closed-source video generators.

Householder Motion Embedding: Noise Modulation for Motion-Controllable Video Generation

Householder Motion Embedding: Noise Modulation for Motion-Controllable Video Generation

Tao Jun Lin、Jinhong Ni、Ruyi Zha、Yifu Wang、Xibin Song 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Diffusion models for video generation have achieved remarkable success, yet precise motion control under explicit user guidance remains challenging. Recent noise-warping methods introduce temporal correlations by warping the noise according to the user-provided motion signal. However, standard spatial warping generally disrupts the noise's i.i.d. Gaussian structure, requiring distribution correction, such as Jacobian-based correction, but at a substantial computational overhead. We propose Householder Motion Embedding (HME), which encodes the motion signal into the noise through a sequence of Householder reflections that together form an orthogonal transformation applied to each location's channel vector. HME exactly preserves the spatial Gaussian structure within each frame while introducing cross-frame correlations that promote temporally consistent video. HME is agnostic to the network architecture and integrates directly into standard video diffusion fine-tuning pipelines. Experiments on different scenarios show that HME matches or outperforms the state of the art, while achieving a 2.7x speedup over noise warping. These results demonstrate that channel-space Householder transformations offer a principled and efficient alternative to noise warping for motion-controllable video generation.

Gaussian structure, requiring distribution correction, such as Jacobian-based correction, but at a substantial computational overhead. VREgo: Consumer VR Headsets for Learning Robot Manipulation from Egocentric Demonstrations

VREgo: Consumer VR Headsets for Learning Robot Manipulation from Egocentric Demonstrations

Liuchuan Yu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

VREgo trains manipulation policies from demonstrations recorded on consumer VR headsets; we instantiate it with EgoKit on one such headset, the PICO 4 Ultra. On-device head and hand tracking and stereo object localisation allow the operator to begin a demonstration at the table. We adapt HumanEgo's preprocessing to estimate grasp thresholds per recording, update object positions after release, and propagate segmentation masks between keyframes. We evaluate policies in simulation on two tabletop tasks recorded by one operator. For cube placement, we compare PICO and Project Aria recordings of the same 41 layouts. With 40 training demonstrations, the PICO policies placed the cube on the tray in 67, 74, and 60 of 100 evaluation layouts across three training seeds. The corresponding Aria results were 73, 78, and 0. For a two-stage stacking task recorded with the PICO alone, the baseline placed the block on the tray in 94 of 100 layouts and met the end-of-episode criterion for both placements in two. In one diagnostic episode, repeated replanning deferred release beyond the eight executed steps. Demonstration replays also showed that the open gripper could displace the cube before grasping it. After adapting the training trajectories and adding task-specific execution rules that use simulator object positions, the system met both placement criteria in 93.8% of episodes across four evaluation noise seeds. The same 100 layouts informed rule development and evaluation. VREgo is available at https://vrego.chuange.org/.

CRISP: Constraint-Resolved Interactions between Subsystems for Predictive Whole-Body Cooperation

CRISP: Constraint-Resolved Interactions between Subsystems for Predictive Whole-Body Cooperation

Dario Valenzuela、Jeeseop Kim

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents a layered control framework that recasts high-dimensional system dynamics as a composition of dynamically interconnected subsystems, with dynamic dual-arm manipulation on the humanoid serving as a representative application. The humanoid is decomposed into a single rigid body (SRB) representing the torso and floating-base dynamic models representing the articulated arms and lower-body. The rigid connections between arm and torso and the closed kinematic chain formed between the end-effectors during cooperative manipulation are represented as holonomic constraints. The corresponding Lagrange multipliers parametrize the interaction wrenches transmitted at the shoulders and between the end-effectors. The high-level nonlinear model predictive control (NMPC) jointly optimizes the subsystem state trajectories, arm torques, ground reaction forces (GRFs), and interaction wrenches, enabling online wrench design and optimization while preserving the dynamic coupling among the independently modeled subsystems. The optimized arm torques are applied directly for cooperative dual-arm manipulation, whereas the prescribed GRFs are mapped to lower-body joint torque for whole-body coordination. We validate the proposed framework through numerical simulations and hardware experiments on a G1 humanoid robot. The results demonstrate optimal interaction wrenches consistent with simulated contact measurement, cooperative dual-arm trajectory tracking, and stable whole-body coordination while executing the NMPC in online manner.

The results demonstrate optimal interaction wrenches consistent with simulated contact measurement, cooperative dual-arm trajectory tracking, and stable whole-body coordination while executing the NMPC in online manner.

An Empirical Study of Reinforcement Learning for Keypoint Positioning of Deformable Linear Objects

An Empirical Study of Reinforcement Learning for Keypoint Positioning of Deformable Linear Objects

Vinay Rodrigues、Sara Zeynalpour、Gokhan Solak、Sebastian Hjorth、Arash Ajoudani 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Deformable linear object (DLO) manipulation is challenging because high-dimensional configurations, nonlinear contact dynamics, and diverse material and geometric properties produce a broad range of object responses that are both difficult to model and data-intensive to learn. This work studies reinforcement learning for planar manipulation of a one-end-grasped DLO, focusing on training efficiency, observation design, robustness, and zero-shot sim-to-real transfer. We compare three Proximal Policy Optimization training stacks and find that the GPU-parallel MuJoCo Playground stack enables faster convergence and more consistent performance than CPU-based alternatives under the same wall-clock budget. Relative keypoint observations and an asymmetric actor-critic formulation further improve learning efficiency. Policies trained with observation noise, delay, domain randomization, and disturbances achieve simulation success rates of 96.4% for single-point tracking, 98.0% for disturbance recovery, and 88.8% for two-point tracking. Using markerless RGB-D keypoint estimation, the policies transfer without real-world fine-tuning, succeeding in 9/10, 10/10, and 8/10 physical trials, respectively.

Dynamic Watermarking and Action-Conditioned Latent Dynamics for Secure Vision-Based Controllers

Dynamic Watermarking and Action-Conditioned Latent Dynamics for Secure Vision-Based Controllers

Aditya Parameshwaran、Navid Aftabi、Abhishek Hanchate、Satish T. S. Bukkapatnam、Dan Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Replay attacks are difficult to detect in vision-based control systems because previously recorded observations can remain plausible while the physical system follows a different trajectory. This paper develops an adaptive dynamic-watermarking framework for detecting replay attacks against vision-based controllers. A private Gaussian watermark is added to the nominal control input, and a control input-conditioned latent dynamics network (LDN) predicts the corresponding evolution in a compact latent space learned from image observations. Attack evidence is obtained from the discrepancy between the predicted latent response and the latent representation of the received image observation. A reinforcement learning policy adjusts the watermark covariance using the latent state and detector confidence, thereby balancing attack detectability, watermark energy, and control distortion. A Lagrangian penalty on task-specific safety costs is included during training to limit the adverse effect of the excitation. The framework is evaluated on a vision-guided UR5 manipulation task in which the robot reaches a target while avoiding a moving human arm under nominal and replay-attacked observations.

Vis2State: Visual Reasoning through Executable State Abstractions

Vis2State: Visual Reasoning through Executable State Abstractions

Sheng Jin、Wentao Liu、Chen Qian

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Solving visual puzzles requires both accurate interpretation of visual evidence and reliable computation under task-specific rules. Embedding visual assumptions and task constraints directly in solving code makes the represented problem difficult to inspect and revise independently. We introduce Vis2State, a training-free agentic framework that abstracts visual puzzles into explicit, revisable problem states linking visual inspection and execution. Visual inspection guides state construction and refinement, while generated programs solve the represented problem. The model constructs both the state and the program without predefined puzzle schemas or solving procedures. Inspection and execution feedback allow it to revise the problem representation and solving procedure independently. Vis2State achieves the state-of-the-art performance on four benchmarks. Cross-model evaluation further demonstrates consistent gains. Ablations, error analyses, and fixed-program state interventions further support the effectiveness of the state-centered design. The code will be made publicly available to facilitate future research.

WeLM-Image: A Unified Image Generation Model towards Visual Intelligence

WeLM-Image: A Unified Image Generation Model towards Visual Intelligence

Canmiao Fu、Chen Li、Guohui Zhang、Hao Lu、Hongyang Wei 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present \textbf{WeLM-Image}, a unified image foundation model that integrates multimodal understanding into text-to-image generation, instruction-guided editing, and multi-reference synthesis. Designed for single-GPU deployment in WeChat's large-scale consumer applications, the model processes text, reference images, and target latents as one interleaved sequence. A Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) backbone combines shared attention with independent understanding and generation parameters to limit optimization interference. Equipping each path with Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) modules provides sparse capacity, with 31.7B stored parameters and approximately 5.4B generation parameters activated per target latent token. A generation-oriented data engine combines a knowledge-grounded visual vocabulary with large-scale visual clustering to balance semantic coverage and editing tasks, while training-time evaluation guides iterative data refinement. Bilingual annotations and verified editing supervision support progressive training up to native 1.5K resolution. We optimize generation and editing separately with rubric- and checklist-based rewards, then consolidate the specialized policies through noise-aware on-policy distillation. In the reported overall comparisons, the 1536-resolution model ranks first among the evaluated open-source models on PRISM-Bench, Qwen-Image-Bench, and UnicBench. MeanFlow-based few-step distillation yields WeLM-Image-Turbo, which generates a $1024\times1024$ image in 4.98\,s on a single NVIDIA H20 GPU using eight guidance-free steps.

MeanFlow-based few-step distillation yields WeLM-Image-Turbo, which generates a $1024\times1024$ image in 4.98\,s on a single NVIDIA H20 GPU using eight guidance-free steps.

Beyond Quadratic Attention: Separable Gaussian Operators for ODE Vision Transformers

Beyond Quadratic Attention: Separable Gaussian Operators for ODE Vision Transformers

Carlos Boned Riera、David Romero Sanchez、Oriol Ramos Terrades

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Self-attention carries a quadratic cost in token count, which limits how far Vision Transformers can scale with input resolution. We introduce SSoG, a separable sum-of-Gaussians spatial operator that replaces dense self-attention with a learned mixture of axis-factorized Gaussian kernels, reducing the per-layer cost from $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$ to subquadratic in $N$. Unlike dot-product self-attention, whose Lipschitz constant is unbounded outside a compact input domain, SSoG with fixed kernels is globally Lipschitz continuous, which, via the Picard--Lindelöf theorem, guarantees that the continuous-depth variant has a unique solution on the whole integration interval, a global guarantee that dense-attention counterparts lack in general. On CIFAR-100 at $224\times224$, in a matched discrete-Transformer setting and in the continuous setting, SSoG improves Top-1 accuracy by 2.2--4.1 points across three model widths while using 8.8--16.6\% fewer parameters, with depth and training recipe held fixed. In a looped-Transformer setting, SSoG improves Top-1 accuracy by 5.4--8.1 points. We further probe inference-time scaling by evaluating on inputs up to $1024\times1024$ at matched batch size: in the continuous-depth (Neural ODE) architecture, SSoG reaches up to 154\% higher throughput and over 60\% lower peak memory than dense attention, with the gap widening as resolution grows, consistent with the difference in asymptotic cost. In the discrete and looped settings, SSoG also achieves over 30\% higher throughput than dense attention at this resolution. Together, these results establish SSoG as a practical, parameter-efficient, and theoretically grounded alternative to dense attention across discrete, weight-shared, and continuous-depth Transformer variants.

2026-11-05

Interpretable Multimodal MRI-Clinical Fusion for Functional Outcome Prediction After Acute Ischemic Stroke

Interpretable Multimodal MRI-Clinical Fusion for Functional Outcome Prediction After Acute Ischemic Stroke

Sina Raeisadigh、Henning Müller、Abderrahmane Hedjoudje

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This study evaluates the added prognostic value of day-1 multimodal MRI for predicting three-month functional outcome after acute ischemic stroke (AIS). Sixty-six AIS patients with ADC, DWI, FLAIR, and clinical data were analyzed. Three-dimensional pretrained ResNet embeddings were extracted from each MRI modality, reduced using fold-isolated principal component analysis, and combined with structured clinical variables and ADC-derived lesion biomarkers. Linear support vector machines were evaluated using eight-fold stratified group cross-validation across MRI modality combinations. Clinical-only modeling achieved strong performance (AUC = $0.899 \pm 0.151$), while MRI-only models showed limited discrimination (AUC $\approx$ $0.67$-$0.73$). Among multimodal configurations, FLAIR-clinical fusion achieved the highest mean cross-validated performance (AUC = $0.948 \pm 0.061$), followed by ADC-clinical fusion (AUC = $0.941 \pm 0.094$). Full ADC-DWI-FLAIR fusion did not consistently outperform single-modality clinical fusion. Feature-importance analysis identified age, NIHSS subscores, ADC lesion burden, and learned FLAIR representations as key contributors, while qualitative FLAIR activation maps showed responses over lesion-like regions. These findings suggest that compact day-1 FLAIR and ADC representations provide complementary prognostic information beyond clinical variables and support interpretable, data-efficient outcome prediction in AIS.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM视频理解
2026-11-05

Fault-Guided Cross-Layer Prediction for Unsupervised Anomaly Detection

Fault-Guided Cross-Layer Prediction for Unsupervised Anomaly Detection

Tao Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feature reconstruction is widely used for unsupervised industrial anomaly detection in the single-class setting, where only normal images from one product category are available for training. We propose a single-class detector that asks what information one block can pass to another. We treat the depth of a frozen vision transformer as a sequence of discrete transitions. A directed predictor learns a block-to-block hidden-state transition pointwise over tokens. Adjacent-layer cosine similarity exposes four sharp fault lines in DINOv2 ViT-L, separating stable depth segments. The deepest blocks retain semantic context but lose local detail; a prediction from deep or shallow sources to sensitive targets therefore exposes the information that cannot be transported. The fault structure fixes the source and target roles, and the same rule transfers to ViT-B and RADIO-B. We use a bounded foreground prior calibrated solely on training images: it attenuates background evidence without also erasing defects outside the estimated foreground. Using one predictor architecture and a common training-only calibration protocol, image AUROC is 99.75 on MVTec-AD, 99.42 on VisA and 94.27 on Real-IAD. On Real-IAD the object-level AUROC is 96.26. At 256x256 pixel resolution, P-AUROC/P-AUPRO are 98.46/95.97 on MVTec-AD, 99.21/96.12 on VisA and 99.26/96.59 on Real-IAD.

多模态/VLM遥感
2026-11-05

National-scale mapping of tree species composition in Switzerland from high-resolution aerial imagery and multimodal data

National-scale mapping of tree species composition in Switzerland from high-resolution aerial imagery and multimodal data

Yixin Zhou、Luca Ferrari、Verena C. Griess、Nataliia Rehush、Konrad Schindler 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Tree-species composition shapes forest productivity and how forests respond to climate disturbances such as drought, windthrow and insect outbreaks. Yet most national forest maps assign each pixel a single dominant species and cannot show the share of accompanying species. We mapped the basal-area shares of ten main tree species and a residual Other class across Switzerland at 30 m resolution. A deep-learning model combining 0.1 m aerial RGB imagery, airborne laser scanning (ALS), annual AlphaEarth satellite embeddings, and climate variables was trained on basal-area observations from 3,992 Swiss National Forest Inventory (NFI) sites. In spatial cross-validation, the model reached a macro-$R^2$ of 0.485 across the 11 classes. On an independent forest-reserve dataset, the map reached a macro-$R^2$ of 0.552 for individual 30 m pixels and 0.733 for plots, and identified the dominant class in 73.2% of pixels and 81.1% of plots. The map shows maple and ash as widespread minor components that dominant-class maps omit. It also reproduces the elevational sequence from broadleaved lowland stands through spruce to larch and Arolla pine near the treeline. By resolving the species mixture within each 30 m pixel, the map complements field inventory statistics with spatially continuous information that can support forest monitoring, the management of mixed stands, and climate-adaptation planning across Switzerland.

By resolving the species mixture within each 30 m pixel, the map complements field inventory statistics with spatially continuous information that can support forest monitoring, the management of mixed stands, and climate-adaptation planning across Switzerland.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM遥感
2026-11-05

When GeoFMs Meet VHR: Benchmarking Foundation Models on Very High Resolution Earth Observation Imagery

When GeoFMs Meet VHR: Benchmarking Foundation Models on Very High Resolution Earth Observation Imagery

Kshitij Raj Sharma、Pierre Adorni、Minh-Tan Pham、Dirk Tiede、Sébastien Lefèvre 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Geospatial foundation models are a widely adopted starting point for remote sensing tasks. Most are pretrained on Sentinel-1/2 and Landsat imagery at a ground sampling distance (GSD) of 10m or coarser. Many applications instead rely on very high resolution (VHR) imagery, where individual buildings, roads, and amenities are resolved. We study which pretraining regimes transfer well to this scale. We introduce VHR-Bench, a benchmark suite of ten datasets at 1.5m GSD or finer spanning five task families, eight application domains, and both satellite and aerial platforms. We evaluate twelve encoders in three pretraining tiers with frozen backbones at two training-data fractions. We find that both pretraining on large natural-image collections and VHR-specific pretraining transfer effectively to VHR tasks, with complementary strengths. SkySense leads when labels are scarce; at the full label fraction the web-pretrained DINOv3 ViT-L edges ahead, with the two close together there. Encoders pretrained only on Sentinel-scale imagery transfer least well under RGB input. Guided by these complementary results, we continue the self-supervised pretraining of DINOv3 ViT-L on 1.02 million held-out VHR tiles and evaluate the resulting encoder without changing the downstream protocol. The continued model gives a clear improvement over the released backbone when labels are scarce and a modest one at the full fraction, at no increase in inference parameters or compute. These findings support a generalize-then-specialize strategy: already strong, broad natural-image pretraining can be further improved by a short VHR continuation stage while preserving its general visual features.

Bri3D: Bridging the Object-Scene Gap in Single-Image 3D Generation via Scene-Anchor

Bri3D: Bridging the Object-Scene Gap in Single-Image 3D Generation via Scene-Anchor

Hongye Hou、Shuai Zhang、Hongjie Li、Zhuoxiao Li、Haiping Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generating compositional 3D scenes from a single image requires recovering detailed object geometry and coherent spatial arrangements. Existing object-to-scene methods, including SAM3D and MIDI, extend object generation through image-context reasoning or inter-instance modeling. Coordinating objects remains challenging with limited scene-level supervision. Directly extending object-centric generators to entire scenes can degrade geometry and fail to preserve instance scale, orientation, and pose. We propose Bri3D, a framework bridging scene-level structural reasoning and object-level generative priors through instance-aware voxel scene anchors. Bri3D predicts a color-coded scene scaffold encoding coarse object geometry and spatial arrangements. Each instance is extracted as a scene anchor and refined into a pose-preserving, prior-compatible voxel support, enabling a frozen object generator to synthesize detailed textured geometry in the anchor-defined spatial reference. This coarse-to-fine design separates scene organization from object-scale detail synthesis, focusing scene-level training on spatial organization and anchor refinement while reusing pretrained object priors. Experiments demonstrate improvements in scene layout, object geometry, visual quality, and generalization. Bri3D predicts scene layouts efficiently; full scene generation is faster than SceneGen and SAM3D.

Rotation Is Token-Blind: A Structural Criterion for Post-Training Quantization of Feed-Forward Geometry Transformers

Rotation Is Token-Blind: A Structural Criterion for Post-Training Quantization of Feed-Forward Geometry Transformers

Huanfei He、Bohao Zhang、Jiaxin Zhang、Wei Sui、John See 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward 3D models use Transformers to predict camera poses, depth, and structure in one pass, avoiding iterative multi-view optimization. Long sequences and deep attention incur high compute and memory costs, motivating Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) for deployment. Existing PTQ methods mainly suppress channel-wise outliers by smoothing or orthogonal rotation. Geometry Transformers introduce a different challenge: heterogeneous token types share one activation quantization grid. A few special tokens may exert high leverage over global outputs and be updated by only a subset of blocks. Token-blind transforms, which apply the same transformation to every token, reduce overall quantization error but cannot remove differentiated degradation across token types. We propose QMapAnything, a structure-aware PTQ framework. We cast floating-point-preserving rotation and smoothing as a token-blind reparameterization group, and prove that they yield common-mode gains while leaving relative token SNR unchanged. We introduce a statistic $Γ$ that combines a special token's quantization disadvantage and output leverage to predict whether differentiated bias correction is needed beyond rotation. Experiments on geometric benchmarks show that W4A8 closely matches full-precision performance. Under W8A8, peak memory falls by $2.2$--$3.1\times$ versus FP32, with up to $3.1\times$ speedup on real hardware. These results provide a principled and efficient approach to deploying feed-forward 3D reconstruction models in resource-constrained edges.

WAM-Sim0.1: World Anchored Memory Meets Online RL for Consistent and Realistic Driving Simulation

WAM-Sim0.1: World Anchored Memory Meets Online RL for Consistent and Realistic Driving Simulation

Jiahao Cui、Jiaye Li、Wenhao Zhang、Yaoyi Li、Shan Luan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Autoregressive driving world models systematically suffer from structural drift, identity drift, and quality decay over long rollout horizons due to transient conditioning signals that lack persistent state. We present WAM-Sim, a driving world model anchored by a persistent hierarchical memory and optimized via online flow-matching RL. WAM-Sim structures persistent context across three temporal and semantic scales: pose-aligned structural tokens encoding persistent road topology, read-only scene tokens supplying geolocated background appearance, and write-once instance tokens preserving individual traffic participant identities across occlusions and chunk boundaries. To restore visual sharpness and physical plausibility degraded by multi-scale conditioning, we introduce an online post-training framework that optimizes self-generated rollouts via flow-matching RL guided by hierarchy-aligned visual rewards and a vision-language model physical judge. The invariant memory empirically confines policy optimization within the anchored context, helping suppress semantic collapse and reward hacking, while online RL systematically enforces physical commonsense and fine-grained visual fidelity. On the nuScenes benchmark, WAM-Sim achieves a superior generation fidelity (FID=3.47, FVD=25.72) and strong downstream perception performance (24.16 mAP, 71.23 road mIoU). On minute-scale rollouts of self-collected data, WAM-Sim substantially mitigates structural and identity drift, preserving high scene similarity and subject identity coherence across extended horizons. Project page: https://fudan-generative-vision.github.io/WAM-Sim.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-11-05

Structure from Collision via Hypothesis-Space Reasoning

Structure from Collision via Hypothesis-Space Reasoning

Takuhiro Kaneko

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Knowledge of the hidden interior structure of an object, including the presence and location of cavities, is essential for predictive simulation, robotic manipulation, and nondestructive inspection. However, such cavities leave no direct signature on the static visible surface, making collision-induced deformation one of the few cues used to infer their presence. Structure from Collision (SfC) addresses this problem by recovering the hidden interior structure of an object from multiview observations of a collision event. SfC is challenging for two reasons: hidden cavities are only indirectly observable through surface motion, and voids correspond to the absence of material, making direct optimization using standard density representations difficult. The density-based SfC baseline inherits both difficulties. Surface-matching gradients do not directly indicate the cavity location, while density gradients can be weak or uninformative inside or near void regions. Therefore, we reformulate SfC as hypothesis-space reasoning, representing hidden cavities as explicit structural hypotheses whose variables encode available structural assumptions rather than relying on implicit density updates. A global stage performs gradient-free optimization over this hypothesis space using forward-only material point method (MPM) simulation. A local stage then refines the selected hypothesis as a Lagrangian particle system through gradient-based optimization using differentiable MPM simulation. On the SfC benchmark, our method improves hidden-structure reconstruction and future prediction over the density-based SfC baseline. Interior-topology stress tests and robustness analyses characterize its scope and limitations. The project page is available at https://www.kecl.ntt.co.jp/people/kaneko.takuhiro/projects/sfc-hypothesis/.

PVDepth: Panoramic Video Depth Estimation via Geometry-Aware Spatiotemporal Adaptation

PVDepth: Panoramic Video Depth Estimation via Geometry-Aware Spatiotemporal Adaptation

Chuanxin Song、Peixi Peng

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Panoramic video depth estimation is pivotal for applications such as Virtual Reality and World Models. However, advancements in this field are impeded by two primary obstacles: the scarcity of large-scale training data and the unique spatiotemporal challenges of Equirectangular Projection (ERP), which hinder the direct transfer of perspective models. In this paper, we first present PanoCARLA, a large-scale synthetic RGB-D panoramic video dataset, featuring natural motion trajectories and drone-like roaming perspectives. Building on this foundation, we propose PVDepth, an end-to-end framework adapted from perspective video depth models. To tackle ERP-specific geometric distortions and consequent non-linear temporal dynamics, we introduce two core mechanisms: (1) A Progressive Sphere-aware Noise Initialization strategy that anneals the noise distribution from planar to spherical, guiding the model to adapt to non-uniform information density; and (2) A Cube-rectified Temporal Modeling module that incorporates an auxiliary cubemap temporal branch to rectify non-linear temporal dynamics in the ERP domain. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PVDepth achieves superior performance, generating geometrically accurate and temporally consistent depth sequences. Code and data will be released at https:// github.com/ChuanxinSong/PVDepth.

MV-Sign: Multi-View 3D Motion Integration and Enhanced Text-to-Motion Conditioning for Sign Language Production

MV-Sign: Multi-View 3D Motion Integration and Enhanced Text-to-Motion Conditioning for Sign Language Production

Singyu Muk、Kaustubh Kundu、Kunyuan Xie、Weijie Wang、Kalin Stefanov 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Sign language production (SLP) aims to generate appropriate sign language motion from spoken language text. However, current 3D SLP methods suffer from noisy supervision and weak text conditioning. Specifically, these methods typically rely on pseudo-3D motion data fitted from single-view frontal videos, which introduces severe self-occlusion and loses fine-grained hand details, ultimately degrading downstream production. To overcome these limitations, we propose a comprehensive framework that jointly integrates multi-view 3D motion data and optimizes the text-conditioning pathway. First, we introduce How2Sign-MV3D, a high-fidelity dataset constructed via a novel cross-view curation pipeline that fuses multi-view estimates to resolve occlusions and filter non-signing segments. Second, we design a robust SLP model incorporating a sign-aware spatio-temporal VQ-VAE (ST-VQVAE) for joint body-hand tokenization, a pseudo-gloss adapter, and keyframe-aware retrieval to enhance text-to-motion alignment. Evaluated on the How2Sign benchmark, our framework reduces body and hand DTW-PA-JPE by 32.8% and 20.9% respectively compared to the state-of-the-art. Finally, we release two new high-quality 3D datasets, ASLCitizen-3D and MM-WAuslan-MV3D, alongside SGNify-MV as a multi-view video benchmark to support broader community research.

Rain Beyond Streaks: Cascaded Realistic Rain and Wet Surface Synthesis via Scene Priors

Rain Beyond Streaks: Cascaded Realistic Rain and Wet Surface Synthesis via Scene Priors

Melissa Tijink、Kirill Tolstobrov、Shunxin Wang、Estefania Talavera、Duc V. Le 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Realistic rain synthesis is a persistent challenge for vision systems operating in adverse weather. Existing methods primarily focus on generating rain streaks, often neglecting consequent surface effects such as reflections and puddles, which are critical for perceptual realism and robustness of downstream vision tasks. We introduce CascadeRS, a rain synthesis framework that leverages depth and segmentation priors to render scene-consistent rain and surface effects. In addition to rain streaks, CascadeRS produces reflections and puddles aligned with scene semantics through a physics-inspired zero-shot, controllable generative pipeline. It produces photorealistic rainy urban scenes, with high perceptual realism, which we validate qualitatively with an extensive user study and quantitatively. We produce the Cityscapes-CascadeRS dataset, to study downstream task performance under real rainy conditions. Our results establish CascadeRS as a scalable and realistic rain-generation framework for evaluating and enhancing the robustness of vision in adverse rainy weather. Code and dataset are available via https://github.com/nis-research/CascadeRainSynthesis
