TerraMesh-Masks: Open-Vocabulary Segmentation for Earth Observation

TerraMesh-Masks: Open-Vocabulary Segmentation for Earth Observation

Benedikt Blumenstiel、Hugues Devimeux、Johannes Jakubik、Konrad Schindler

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Open-vocabulary segmentation (OVS) enables models to segment a variety of concepts specified in natural language, removing the need for fixed label sets and fine-tuning. While recent OVS approaches have shown strong performance on natural images, they transfer poorly to Earth observation (EO) data due to differing semantics, scales, sensing modality, and geographic context. To date, large-scale EO-specific datasets and benchmarks for OVS remain scarce. We, therefore, introduce TerraMesh-Masks, a large-scale open-vocabulary segmentation dataset for EO, comprising 16 million binary masks aligned with nine million multimodal TerraMesh samples and annotated using OpenStreetMap and Overture-derived semantics. In addition, we provide an expert-reviewed evaluation set of 523 samples covering over 200 classes to enable robust evaluation. To demonstrate the impact of TerraMesh-Masks, we build an open-vocabulary version of TerraMind. Our results demonstrate significantly improved performance over general-purpose state-of-the-art models reaching 35.18% IoU versus 16.29% for DINOv3.txt and 11.33% for SAM-3. We release the dataset publicly under a permissive license at https://huggingface.co/datasets/ibm-esa-geospatial/TerraMesh-Masks.

Coverage Hides Churn: Auditing and Preserving Conformal Decisions as VLM Label Registries Grow

Coverage Hides Churn: Auditing and Preserving Conformal Decisions as VLM Label Registries Grow

Jai Kumar Sharma、Peeyush Tapadiya

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language classifiers can learn new labels from text alone, so the label lists they serve keep growing. We show that this growth silently changes conformal decisions about old labels. Common softmax-based conformal scores compare each label with an average over all labels; adding labels changes that average, and with it the score of every old label, even when the model, the image, and the old calibration data stay the same. Recalibrating after the update keeps the coverage guarantee, yet across eleven settings it changes at least one old decision on 53-90% of new images. Coverage cannot reveal this, because it checks only the true label: in five controlled settings that keep the old calibration, so that only the normalizer changes, old-class coverage moves by at most 0.01 while 60-67% of images change. We propose a lifecycle audit that measures these changes alongside coverage and set size, and a simple fix, PrefixCP: each class keeps the label registry that existed when it was added. Its old decisions then never change, each class keeps exact class-conditional coverage under exchangeability, and one prefix scan computes all historical normalizers at once. PrefixCP recovers 40.9-58.5% of the set-size gap between never updating the reference and recalibrating, which gives smaller sets but changes old decisions.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-11-05

Degree-Equalized View-Graph Selection under Tight Pair Budgets

Degree-Equalized View-Graph Selection under Tight Pair Budgets

Minhyeok Im、Jongseong Brad Choi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Fully matching all image pairs in Structure-from-Motion is quadratic in the number of images, so practical pipelines pass a budgeted subset to the final matching and reconstruction backend. One-shot selectors score candidates with image retrieval, cheap geometric probes, or a learned prior and finalize B pairs before full matching. Their priorities do not account for the degree already allocated to each image, which can concentrate edges on a few images. Across nine large 1DSfM internet photo collections at B=2N, the coverage top-B selector leaves 54% of covered images with realized degree at most two. Reconstruction can then fail even when the selected graph is nearly connected and almost fully verified. Our training-free greedy rule, Degree-Equalized Allocation (DEAL), exponentially downweights pairs whose endpoints are already well covered. On these collections, DEAL improves the one-shot selector built on the same prior (from 0.776 to 0.929) and matches the mean registered-image ratio of an SfM-supervised edge prior under the shared backend (0.929 vs 0.930), without training. On the largely saturated 38-scene standard benchmark suite, DEAL gives the highest mean among the evaluated training-free selectors (0.875 vs 0.858 one-shot).

Reconstruction can then fail even when the selected graph is nearly connected and almost fully verified. Our training-free greedy rule, Degree-Equalized Allocation (DEAL), exponentially downweights pairs whose endpoints are already well covered. On these collections, DEAL improves the one-shot selector built on the same prior (from 0.776 to 0.929) and matches the mean registered-image ratio of an SfM-supervised edge prior under the shared backend (0.929 vs 0.930), without training. On the largely saturated 38-scene standard benchmark suite, DEAL gives the highest mean among the evaluated training-free selectors (0.875 vs 0.858 one-shot).

目标检测/分割3D视觉
2026-11-05

Compact Motion Embeddings for Feed-forward Unified Human Animation

Compact Motion Embeddings for Feed-forward Unified Human Animation

Seonwook Park、Hyunsoo Cha、Amrita Mazumdar、Shengze Wang、Tianye Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Animating a reference image with a driver's motion requires a motion representation that captures face, hand, and body articulation but excludes the driver's appearance. Most body animation methods take motion from external pose estimators and render it with many denoising steps; compact learned motion representations mostly cover the face. We present FUHA, a real-time-capable feed-forward transformer that learns a compact motion embedding of the whole human and uses it for face, upper-body, and full-body animation. A Q-Former compresses the driver's features from a frozen visual encoder into this embedding. To disentangle motion from appearance, a novel contrastive loss pulls it toward the motion embedding of the driver's rendered keypoint image and away from that of the same person in another pose. At inference time, FUHA needs no external pose estimators, keypoints, or parametric body models. As a retrieval key, the motion embedding matches body pose better than the learned global embeddings of ten vision encoders. Its same-person retrieval rate is 0.1%, against 9.5% for its own DINOv3 backbone. On our upper-body benchmark, FUHA outperforms all baselines on every self-reenactment metric and on cross-reenactment identity similarity. It is competitive on face and full-body and runs at up to 38.9 FPS at 512x512 on a single RTX 4090.

2026-11-05

Multimodal Hierarchical Mixture-of-Experts for 3D Medical Vision-Language Modeling

Multimodal Hierarchical Mixture-of-Experts for 3D Medical Vision-Language Modeling

Gizem Mert、Szymon Płotka、Pedro R. A. S. Bassi、Wenxuan Li、Zongwei Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Medical 3D scans and reports are inherently hierarchical, with diagnostic cues concentrated in specific anatomical regions across multiple levels of organization. Yet, most medical vision-language models ignore this structure and fail to focus on clinically relevant anatomy. We introduce MedHiVE, which combines hierarchical vision-language Soft Mixture-of-Experts (SMoE) with contrastive learning to jointly model fine-grained anatomy and global scan context. To our knowledge, MedHiVE is the first framework to route both vision and text tokens through a two-level SMoE hierarchy organized by anatomical scale, coupling this with report-derived anatomy-level contrastive alignment for 3D medical imaging. This replaces the direct region-text alignment of statically pooled features and vision-only hierarchical routing of prior work with dynamic, slot-based cross-modal information flow across anatomical scales. MedHiVE operates via a two-level, learnable token-routing mechanism, where local image and text features are first routed through a local SMoE and then fused by a global SMoE into unified multimodal representations. Contrastive learning aligns both anatomy-level and global vision-language representations in a unified space. Pre-trained on 25,000+ CT scans with anatomical supervision, MedHiVE consistently surpasses 11 state-of-the-art methods across 6 datasets on 3 tasks: segmentation, classification, and detection, demonstrating the importance of hierarchical modeling. The code for MedHiVE is at github.com/szczurek-lab/MedHiVE.

The code for MedHiVE is at github.com/szczurek-lab/MedHiVE.

ProtoGestalt: Interpretable Prototypes for Facial Phenotyping and Diagnosis of Rare Genetic Disorders

ProtoGestalt: Interpretable Prototypes for Facial Phenotyping and Diagnosis of Rare Genetic Disorders

Ranga Baminiwatte、Alexander Hustinx、Fabio Hellmann、Aaron J Masino

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Rare genetic disorders are difficult to diagnose due to their diversity and overlapping clinical features. Facial morphology can provide diagnostic clues, yet most diagnostic models offer limited interpretability and are not designed to support broad screening for undiagnosed disease. We present ProtoGestalt, an interpretable prototype-based framework for facial phenotyping, rare-disease screening, and syndrome identification. The method extends prototype learning by constraining learned visual features to select facial regions, allowing predictions to be explained through localized and additive evidence. We assess ProtoGestalt's ability to distinguish syndromic from unaffected faces on multiple control datasets and class prevalence values. We further evaluate it's diagnostic performance on syndromes observed during training. The learned prototype-based representations provide significant separation between syndromic and case-matched control images and retain useful performance as the proportion of controls increases. The framework also demonstrates the ability to differentiate among syndromes. Although reasonably accurate specialized models for facial classification of rare disorders already exist, ProtoGestalt offers an interpretable alternative that links predictions to anatomically meaningful facial regions, potentially supporting clinical decision-making and the discovery of phenotype-gene associations.

Towards Practical 20 kbps Generative Video Compression via Adaptive Tokenization and Entropy Modeling

Towards Practical 20 kbps Generative Video Compression via Adaptive Tokenization and Entropy Modeling

Jiancheng Zhao、Jixiang Luo、Haibin Huang、Chi Zhang、Xuelong Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video compression at ultra-low bitrates remains challenging due to the loss of perceptually important details under limited bit budgets. Recent generative video compression methods leverage pretrained video priors to improve perceptual quality and temporal coherence, but many follow a compress-then-generate paradigm, where generative priors are primarily exploited during reconstruction. Moreover, representations learned for generation and reconstruction are not inherently optimized for entropy coding. In this work, we propose \method, which adapts pretrained video representations into compression-oriented representations while preserving their reconstruction capability. Specifically, we adapt the pretrained causal video VAE of MiniMax-H3 into a discrete video tokenizer and develop a small autoregressive entropy model that captures local and global token dependencies, with grouped prediction for parallel decoding. To further improve coding efficiency, we jointly optimize the tokenizer and entropy model using a differentiable rate surrogate, allowing rate gradients to adapt token assignments toward lower coding costs while preserving reconstruction quality. Experiments on UVG, MCL-JCV, and HEVC Class B demonstrate superior perceptual rate--distortion performance, achieving BD-LPIPS savings of 82.2\%--87.5\% and BD-DISTS savings of 78.3\%--85.0\% relative to GLC-Video.

NutriVisRAG: Multimodal Exemplar Grounding for Nutrient Estimation with Vision-Language Models

NutriVisRAG: Multimodal Exemplar Grounding for Nutrient Estimation with Vision-Language Models

Lubnaa Abdur Rahman、Ioannis Papathanail、Maria Panagiotou、Nefeli Dimitropoulou、Margret Harðardóttir 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Diet is a major determinant of health, yet accurate dietary assessment from free-living meal images remains challenging. Existing retrieval-based Vision-Language Model (VLM) approaches to nutrient estimation typically rely on textual food representations, while, to our knowledge, direct grounding on visually similar annotated meals has not been investigated. We present NutriVisRAG, a retrieval-augmented generation framework that uses a vision encoder to retrieve similar meals with known nutritional annotations and provide them as multimodal in-context exemplars for VLM nutrient estimation at inference time, without task-specific fine-tuning. We evaluate NutriVisRAG across four datasets, four VLMs, three retrieval encoders, and two prompting strategies, including free-living meals from adults with insulin-treated diabetes, where carbohydrate estimates are compared against two dietitians' annotations. Across all 96 configurations (4 datasets x 4 VLMs x 3 retrieval encoders x 2 prompting strategies), NutriVisRAG reduced the primary estimation error in 56, with improvements more frequent under whole-meal (35/48) than per-item prompting (21/48). Across the evaluated configurations, the lowest NutriVisRAG error was below the best Vanilla error in 13 of 16 VLM--dataset pairs. For GPT-5.4-mini, under whole-meal prompting, the best-performing retrieval encoder for each dataset reduced the primary error on all four datasets relative to Vanilla, with reductions of 1.7--35.7%. Overall, our results show that grounding in multimodal exemplars comprising images and respective annotations can improve nutrient estimation at inference time without task-specific fine-tuning, although the benefits remain configuration-dependent.

2026-11-05

VFlash: Fast, Accurate Visual Planning via Flash Primitive Decoding

VFlash: Fast, Accurate Visual Planning via Flash Primitive Decoding

Yu-Cheng Chou、Shihao Wang、Jin Huang、Chi-Pin Huang、Chiao-An Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Models for physical AI tasks, such as robotics and autonomous driving, need not reason solely in language. They can use visual plans--points, bounding boxes, and trajectories--to represent spatial goals and anticipate motion. However, autoregressive vision-language models generate these plans one coordinate token at a time, making richer predictions increasingly costly. We introduce VFlash, a method for fast, accurate visual planning through Flash Primitive Decoding. VFlash exploits the known structure of visual primitives to predict their coordinates jointly, using a lightweight drafter that generates an entire primitive in one forward pass. The layout adapts to the primitive's type, dimensionality, and length, supporting compact boxes and long, variable-length trajectories. Crucially, spatial accuracy need not require reproducing an autoregressive model's exact token sequence. VFlash therefore trains on annotated primitives and directly accepts structurally valid predictions without target-token agreement. Across the evaluated 2D and 3D robot trajectory tasks, VFlash consistently reduces prediction errors relative to autoregressive decoding. Preliminary single-rollout measurements indicate approximately 25x faster generation for 3D point motion forecasting. A world action model conditioned on VFlash plans achieves 70.2% average success across four MolmoSpaces task suites, compared with 62.6% for the baseline.

2026-11-05

Edge-Assisted Crowdsensing for Explainable Cyclist Safety Perception

Edge-Assisted Crowdsensing for Explainable Cyclist Safety Perception

Hongsen Cao、Chia-Yen Chiang、Mona Jaber、Meihui Wang、Yasmin Fathy 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Cyclist fatality rates remain high despite policy support for active travel, and autonomous vehicles add complexity to mixed traffic. Neither crash statistics nor multi-model detection pipelines scale to continuous monitoring of perceived safety along a route. We present an edge-assisted crowdsensing system: handlebar cameras supply street-level frames, a vision-language model (VLM) turns each frame into structured traffic attributes through a single prompt, and an explainable boosting machine (EBM) combines them with OpenStreetMap road infrastructure and on-bike kinematics to predict a crowdsourced 1-9 safety score. Extraction belongs at the network edge: the fastest self-hosted model needs 2.49 s per frame and scoring takes 0.4 ms on a CPU. On 1,362 annotated frames from two London routes, the EBM regressor reaches 72.2 percent binary accuracy and a mean absolute error of 1.011 under five-fold cross-validation. It is within one accuracy point of black-box ensembles. Its per-feature shape functions give exact attribution: physical lane separation is worth +0.97 rating points, a painted lane +0.70 and a construction site -0.65. Planners can therefore price an infrastructure change in rating points, and the results enter cost-benefit analysis directly. We also report inter-annotator agreement (alpha = 0.4695), an attribute audit, and a sensitivity analysis linking attribute error to accuracy. With re-encoding, a 20 s sampling interval serves 1,000 riders at 54 Mbit/s at a cost of 1.4 accuracy points, making continuous monitoring practical.

SM4RT: Cascaded Feed-forward Model for 4D Reconstruction

SM4RT: Cascaded Feed-forward Model for 4D Reconstruction

Pang-Chi Lo、Jhao-Syun Lai、Tsung-Wei Ke

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We introduce SM4RT, a multi-task cascade feed-forward model for dynamic scene reconstruction from RGB videos. While existing feed-forward approaches typically address geometry reconstruction and motion estimation separately--and often predict motions in a single coordinate frame that must account for camera movement--SM4RT solves these tasks sequentially by first reconstructing 3D geometry and then bootstrapping motion estimation from the predicted structure. By expressing each pixel's 3D motion in its corresponding camera frame, SM4RT reduces ambiguity arising from camera motion. To address the sparsity of real-world motion labels, SM4RT further incorporates a weakly supervised loss that regularizes unlabeled point motions using labeled point positions at the other time step. We evaluate motion estimation on three dynamic-scene datasets--Kubric, Waymo, and Point Odyssey--and SM4RT achieves the best performance on most metrics compared with prior baselines. We additionally benchmark geometry reconstruction on five dynamic scene datasets and show that SM4RT achieves competitive performance with state-of-the-art methods. Overall, our unified framework demonstrates that combining geometry reconstruction and motion estimation in a cascade not only avoids performance trade-offs but also enhances motion estimation through improved geometric reasoning.

Overall, our unified framework demonstrates that combining geometry reconstruction and motion estimation in a cascade not only avoids performance trade-offs but also enhances motion estimation through improved geometric reasoning.

2026-11-05

Hierarchical Incremental Beta Splatting for Online Reconstruction

Hierarchical Incremental Beta Splatting for Online Reconstruction

Linus Franke

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Online 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstructs a scene during capture: each keyframe spawns primitives once and refines them for only a few dozen iterations, leaving little chance to fix poor initialization or missing detail. Gaussians are also inherently soft, needing many overlaps for solid surfaces. We present Hierarchical Incremental Beta Splatting (HIBS), which brings beta kernels to incremental reconstruction. Their shape parameter interpolates between a soft, Gaussian-like profile and a flat-topped, near-opaque surfel, acting as a confidence dial for geometry. Our confidence-aware spawning uses geometric observability and multi-view depth agreement to initialize both opacity and shape, producing solid primitives where geometry is verifiable and soft, correctable primitives where it is not. As online densification, error-driven relocation recycles stale primitives into photometric-error hotspots. Bounded beta kernel support enables exact tile culling and faster rasterization, and a closed-form, shape-preserving merging formulation provides a level-of-detail (LOD) hierarchy for fast optimization and real-time rendering. On Mip-NeRF 360 in the on-the-fly protocol, HIBS improves PSNR by up to 0.8 dB while reducing optimization time by up to 40%. Project page: https://linus-franke.com/hibs/

2026-11-05

IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing

IMUDINO: Self-distilled Foundation Model for Inertial Sensing

Zhoutong Ye、Chengwen Zhang、Zixuan Wang、Xufeng Jian、Zhe He 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present IMUDINO, the first foundation model (FM) for inertial sensing to achieve competitive performance across diverse sensing tasks including human activity recognition (HAR), health, motion capture, and odometry. Prior inertial FMs often focus on one subfield of inertial sensing and sometimes underperform state-of-the-art supervised methods (e.g., in HAR), making their scope considerably narrower than FMs for vision and natural language. Moreover, they often require fixed input length and sampling rate, and generalize poorly to unseen sensor locations. To build a general-purpose foundation model for inertial sensing, we adapt the DINO self-supervised learning (SSL) paradigm to inertial data through a set of semantics-preserving motion transformations that simulate variations in motion pattern and sensor setup. For model architecture, we use a plain transformer encoder with frequency-aware positional encoding that natively supports varying input length and sampling rate. This simple setup proved remarkably effective for learning global and dense features generalizable across users, sensor setups, tasks, and, in the case of robot odometry, embodiments. We hope IMUDINO can serve as a backbone for inertial sensing and kickstart the development of general-purpose inertial FMs.

PSGAgent: Enhancing MLLMs' Dynamic Visual Perception for Panoptic Scene Graph Generation via Agentic Reasoning

PSGAgent: Enhancing MLLMs' Dynamic Visual Perception for Panoptic Scene Graph Generation via Agentic Reasoning

Qingyun Liu、Yuankun Liu、Jiaye Feng、Jie Huang、Zonghong Dai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Panoptic Scene Graph generation (PSG) requires holistic yet fine-grained reasoning over complex relations among objects. However, current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) applied to PSG suffer from a perception bottleneck: they function as static observers limited by fixed-resolution views. As a result, models often overlook critical relations involving small or peripheral objects and struggle to differentiate ambiguous predicates in complex scenes. To address these limitations, we propose PSGAgent, an agentic framework that enhances visual perception for PSG through a think-act-observe loop. PSGAgent allows MLLMs to dynamically refine their visual perception using two tools: a Region Zoom-In Tool for local exploration of small or boundary relations and a Visual Highlight Tool for relation-focused segmentation that filters out irrelevant contextual noise. To optimize decision-making, we construct a two-stage training pipeline. First, we create a high-quality, error-aware Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (M-CoT) dataset that provides robust supervision for cold-start learning. Then, we design a novel hierarchical reward mechanism integrated with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO) to enable strategic and adaptive tool usage. Extensive experiments on the PSG benchmark show that PSGAgent achieves SOTA performance, demonstrating strong capability in both holistic and fine-grained scene understanding.

FreePath: Neural Mean Free Path for Image Dehazing

FreePath: Neural Mean Free Path for Image Dehazing

Rongrong Gao、Jingxuan Feng、Wenyu Xu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Single image dehazing requires recovering scene radiance from visual responses that are weakened and veiled by participating media. Existing methods either invert explicit scattering variables, which can be unstable under complex scenes, or learn direct restoration mappings, which often discard the physical organization of haze formation. We present \emph{FreePath}, a neural mean free path framework for image dehazing. Instead of estimating atmospheric parameters, FreePath represents dehazing as feature space path state restoration, inspired by the expected distance a visual response can propagate before interacting with haze. Within a residual encoder and decoder, the Mean Free Path Residual Block learns path and collision fields to guide haze constrained correction, while FreePath State Conservation Integration preserves restored path states during deep feature aggregation. Experiments on paired CDD-11 haze images and real RTTS scenes show that FreePath consistently improves full reference fidelity, no reference perceptual quality, and visual faithfulness over representative dehazing baselines.

2026-11-05

Selective Event Evidence and Persistent Temporal Priors for Event-Based Point Tracking

Selective Event Evidence and Persistent Temporal Priors for Event-Based Point Tracking

Xiaokai Song、Zhang Li、Leqi Liu、Yibin Ye、Kun Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Event cameras enable tracking under fast motion and challenging illumination. However, stable long-term point tracking remains challenging: event activity is sparse and varies with scene content and motion, complicating correspondence, while trajectory estimates can drift over long sequences. Together, these challenges motivate a tracker that selectively uses informative event evidence and retains trajectory constraints across temporal windows. We propose Selective Event Evidence and Persistent Temporal Priors (SEPT), an event-only framework for tracking any point (TAP) over long sequences. SEPT introduces two complementary modules: Task-Adaptive Event Evidence Modulation (TEEM) and Persistent Trajectory Prior (PTP). Using only tracking supervision, TEEM learns a bounded reliability field that weights the event tensor across space, time, and channels before feature extraction. PTP carries geometric information across overlapping windows by marginalizing a fixed local quadratic trajectory model with a Schur complement. Its visibility-aware correction refines overlap coordinates, while a clipped terminal velocity initializes coordinates at newly exposed timestamps. SEPT builds on the ETAP architecture. SEPT is trained exclusively on synthetic EventKubric data and evaluated on five datasets across TAP and feature tracking. Results show that SEPT outperforms the retrained ETAP baseline in average Jaccard for TAP and in feature age and expected feature age for feature tracking; it also surpasses several methods combining event and frame inputs on EC and EDS. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/sssxxxkkkk/SEPT.

AffineGS: Unlocking Accurate Geometry in 3DGS via Spatially Varying Affine Rectification

AffineGS: Unlocking Accurate Geometry in 3DGS via Spatially Varying Affine Rectification

Tze Ho Elden Tse、Jizong Peng、Richard Chen、Angela Yao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has become the leading approach for photorealistic novel view synthesis, yet its geometric accuracy lags behind its visual quality. Existing depth-guided methods attempt to resolve this by regularizing the optimization with monocular depth priors, typically using a single global scale-and-shift alignment. Crucially, we discover a fundamental flaw in this assumption: modern monocular depth networks systematically exhibit region-dependent, piecewise-linear distortions. This discovery invalidates standard global alignment strategies, which warp scene geometry by enforcing a single transform. To address this, we introduce a spatially varying affine rectification model that corrects monocular disparity into consistent metric depth using regionally smooth, edge-aware fields. We further derive a normal-aware regularizer that uses spatial gradients to couple the rectified disparity with 3DGS depth and normals. Across diverse experimental settings, our sensor-free method shows strong photometric quality of Gaussian Splatting while substantially improving geometric accuracy and outperforming state-of-the art geometry-focused methods.

Simulation of Refraction Through Voxel Volumes

Simulation of Refraction Through Voxel Volumes

Rainier Mobley、Jean Michel Létang、Max Langer

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Ray tracing in X-ray science has typically been limited to descriptions where refraction occurs only at interfaces, ignoring the refraction occurring due to the continuous variations in index of refraction in the bulk of a material, as are common in biological samples like bone. We propose a method to simulate X-ray images directly on voxel grids using curved rays. We first validate this method compared to a model with an analytical solution, and then investigate the effects of voxel size, interpolation choice, and illumination to grid alignment on a few simulated test objects. From these investigations, we make several recommendations to guide simulations using these techniques.

DEQ-Det: Dual Evidence Queries for Few-Shot Analog IC Device Detection

DEQ-Det: Dual Evidence Queries for Few-Shot Analog IC Device Detection

Zidong Zhao、Rong Huang、Chengxing Fan、Jie Hu、Malik Haris 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Detecting analog integrated circuit (IC) devices in layout images supports circuit analysis and inspection. With only a few annotated examples, variation in device geometry and visual similarity to dummy or routing structures make reliable detection difficult. Foreground similarity alone can highlight plausible targets without distinguishing them from competing layout patterns. We propose Dual Evidence Queries for Detection (DEQ-Det), which constructs complementary foreground and background references from the same support images without additional annotations. Foreground references capture target morphology, while selected background references characterize surrounding layout patterns. Both sources guide query selection and region verification, combining target appearance with layout context. Across five held-out device categories and three random seeds, DEQ-Det achieves macro AP50 of 39.41 and 53.03 at 10 and 30 shots, exceeding the strongest listed baselines by 3.97 and 9.71 points, respectively. A matched 30-shot ablation further shows that RoI background evidence reduces pooled false positives at comparable recall, supporting its role in interference suppression.

Egocentric Action Recognition with Objects and Vision-Language Affordances

Egocentric Action Recognition with Objects and Vision-Language Affordances

Raúl Boix-Aldeguer、Manuel Benavent-Lledo、David Ortiz-Perez、Jose Garcia-Rodriguez

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Action recognition, and more specifically egocentric action recognition, is a core task in computer vision, with applications such as monitoring the daily life activities of elderly people and task assistance. Recent methods rely on visual modalities such as RGB video or depth. However, in egocentric video, an action is largely defined by its objects, which these methods rarely model. We propose a method that combines a frozen SlowFast video expert with an attention-based object expert by late fusion. Each object is represented with its affordances, that is, what can be done with it, generated zero-shot by a vision-language model. Since datasets such as MECCANO annotate only the objects involved in each action, evaluating with these boxes tells the model which objects are active. We therefore train on annotated boxes and evaluate the method with detections from the official active-object detector, and with annotated boxes as an upper bound. Without affordances, our fusion reaches 68.4% Top-1 with annotated boxes but 49.0% with detections. About half of this drop comes from non-active detections. To remove them, the same vision-language model generates an interaction affordance that decides whether a hand is manipulating each detected object. On MECCANO, objects raise the Top-1 of the video expert from 44.9% to 49.0%, and the two affordances raise it to 49.9%, above SlowFast and object-aware RGB baselines. Code will be released upon acceptance.

ArtCrafter: Feed-Forward Generation of Articulated 3D Object with Analytic Joint Derivation

ArtCrafter: Feed-Forward Generation of Articulated 3D Object with Analytic Joint Derivation

Minh Canh Tu、Quang-Binh Nguyen、Khoi Nguyen

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Articulated 3D assets are essential for robotic simulation and training, yet their generation from casual images poses a key challenge: recovering part geometry and kinematics that remain mutually consistent. We present ArtCrafter, a novel feed-forward generative model for controllable generation of articulated 3D objects from a pair of images, one depicting the object in the rest state and the other in the open state. At the core of ArtCrafter is a DiT-based generative model that jointly produces part-level meshes for both articulation states in a single forward pass. Inspired by Slot Attention, its denoising process operates on a $1+2N$ slot layout comprising one shared base slot and $N$ paired rest/open slots, encouraging each pair to bind to the same kinematic part. To ensure geometrically consistent and disjoint part generation, we introduce Triplet Attention to enforce cross-state geometric consistency between paired rest/open slots anchored through the shared base slot, and a repulsion loss $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{rep}}$ on decoded part point clouds to prevent inter-part penetration throughout denoising. Given the resulting paired meshes, URDF joint parameters---type, axis, origin, and range---are derived analytically from the rigid transformation between each rest/open pair, providing an interpretable alternative to learned joint-parameter regression. Extensive experiments on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution datasets demonstrate that ArtCrafter outperforms existing method under the same dual-state setting in geometry quality and articulation precision, while remaining competitive with methods operating under different input settings and paradigms.

Generator-Aligned Reward Modeling with Target-Aware Bounded Optimization for Visual Generation

Generator-Aligned Reward Modeling with Target-Aware Bounded Optimization for Visual Generation

Ziqiao Weng、Yuanzhi Liang、Haibing Huang、Weidong Cai、Xuelong Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Post-training aims to enhance desired attributes in visual generative models while preserving their general generation capabilities. Reward-guided post-training faces two challenges: real training data may mismatch the generator's output distribution, and attribute supervision may be confounded by unrelated content. Unbounded reward maximization can also cause overoptimization and degrade generation quality. We propose a framework combining Generator-Aligned Reward Modeling (GARM) and Target-Aware Bounded Optimization (TABO). GARM mitigates distribution mismatch through generator-support matching and reduces content confounding using content-matched real contrasts, yielding generator-relevant, attribute-focused reward supervision. TABO maps the learned reward to a smooth, upper-bounded function calibrated from real target-positive examples. This transformation is independent of the optimization algorithm and supports both direct reward-gradient and policy-based optimization. We further introduce Target-Aware GRPO (TA-GRPO), an adaptation of Flow-GRPO that combines fixed stage-wise advantage scaling, a generator-to-target curriculum, and timestep-balanced credit assignment for flow-based video optimization. Experiments show that GARM improves target-attribute expression while maintaining competitive generation quality. TABO better balances attribute enhancement and visual quality than unconstrained reward maximization, while TA-GRPO improves target enhancement over vanilla Flow-GRPO and reduces unintended attribute leakage on neutral prompts. These results highlight the importance of generator-aligned reward supervision and controlled, data-calibrated optimization for targeted visual generation.

Georeferenced Cross-View 3D Reconstruction from Ground and Satellite Images

Georeferenced Cross-View 3D Reconstruction from Ground and Satellite Images

David G. Shatwell、Mubarak Shah

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Georeferenced 3D reconstruction from ground images requires recovering not only the local scene geometry, but also localizing every camera center and 3D point in a geographic coordinate frame. Existing cross-view geo-localization methods are able to estimate the GPS coordinate of a single ground camera, but do not reconstruct dense 3D geometry, while recent feed-forward multi-view 3D reconstruction models predict point maps and camera poses only in a local coordinate frame. We introduce X-VGGT, a feed-forward cross-view geometry transformer that unifies these tasks by jointly processing a set of ground-level images and a georeferenced satellite image to predict ground-view camera poses, dense 3D point maps, and a scene-level transform parameterized by gravity, scale, yaw and translation. This transform projects the local reconstruction into the satellite plane, resulting in the geo-registration of all ground images with the georeferenced satellite image. We further propose an evaluation protocol for this joint task and show that, across multiple cross-view datasets, X-VGGT produces accurate georeferenced reconstructions and outperforms prior cross-view geo-localization and multi-view 3D reconstruction models.

Integrating Learned Priors through Prior-Space Reparameterization for Physically Based Inverse Rendering

Integrating Learned Priors through Prior-Space Reparameterization for Physically Based Inverse Rendering

Hun Hwangbo、Minjun Park、Yongsung Kim、Wooseok Song、Jungmin Ko 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Physically based inverse rendering decomposes appearance into materials and illumination, an ambiguous problem for which learned material predictions provide useful prior information. Most existing methods incorporate these cues through auxiliary regularization losses, which alter the optimization objective and require balancing their influence against photometric fitting. We propose a prior-guided material reparameterization that uses the predicted feature relationships to restrict the optimization space while leaving the objective unchanged. We fuse multi-view material predictions onto a mesh and construct a permutohedral lattice in the resulting prior feature space. Surface points with similar predictions share material variables, which are optimized jointly with geometry and illumination using a purely photometric objective. Experiments with two learned predictors on three synthetic benchmarks and Stanford-ORB demonstrate improved material decomposition and relighting. Our method improves relighting PSNR by 1.5-2.5 dB over the strongest evaluated mesh-based baseline on Synthetic4Relight and TensoIR-Synthetic, and outperforms a regularization loss using the same prior in controlled comparisons. We further show that our reparameterization pools noisy photometric gradients, producing updates that are more consistent across sampled views. This consistency enables a shortened joint optimization schedule that takes less than a minute while retaining relighting quality above all evaluated baselines on TensoIR-Synthetic.

RLGPA: A Reinforcement Learning-Guided Granularity Planning Attack for Decision-Based Sparse Adversarial Examples

RLGPA: A Reinforcement Learning-Guided Granularity Planning Attack for Decision-Based Sparse Adversarial Examples

Chao Zhou、Shenghua He、Jiaxin Mo、Qiangguo Yu、Fengchuang Xing 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Decision-based sparse attacks must reduce perturbation support using only the victim's predicted label. The best restoration strategy varies as support contracts: coarse removal works early, while tail states need finer, history-aware tests. We propose RLGPA, an online reinforcement learning method that schedules support-reduction executors and their granularity under a hard query budget. A masked Dueling Double DQN selects among seven stable policy--scale actions; each action produces support-relative restoration groups that are rebuilt after every macro decision. A feasibility-preserving executor commits only adversarial restorations and feeds support gain and query cost back to the planner. Under the matched top-1-label oracle, RLGPA reaches 61\% targeted ASR at sparsity 0.01 on CIFAR-10 (vs. 25\% SparseEvo, 0\% Pointwise), and 75.5\%/73.0\% on ResNet50/ViT at sparsity 0.005 (vs. 29.0\%/15.5\%). A fixed-probe test reduces median \(L_0\) from 165 to 23 after 40 images without any update. State-blind and state-shuffled controls degrade significantly, confirming that the learned policy uses attack state; the main gains come from state-conditioned executor scheduling and dynamic repartition. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/Acui001/RLGPA.git.

目标检测/分割医学图像
2026-11-04

Finite Element Elastographic Analysis of Kidney MRI for Computational Assessment of Chronic Kidney Disease

Finite Element Elastographic Analysis of Kidney MRI for Computational Assessment of Chronic Kidney Disease

A. Bhattacharjee、M. M. Rahman、A. Srivastava、E. H. Bhuiyan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Background and Objective: Bridging kidney MRI with patient-specific computational biomechanics may enable accurate, noninvasive assessment of chronic kidney disease. To develop a finite element method (FEM)-based computational elastography framework for estimating renal biomechanical properties from conventional kidney MRI and to evaluate its ability to differentiate chronic kidney disease (CKD) from healthy controls. Methods: Kidney segmentation masks from healthy control and CKD subjects were converted into triangulated surface meshes, and watertight surfaces were tetrahedralized to create volumetric FEM meshes. Kidney tissue was modeled as a nearly incompressible Kelvin-Voigt viscoelastic material. Harmonic wave propagation simulations were performed using FEM, followed by Helmholtz-based elastographic inversion to estimate complex shear modulus distributions. Derived biomechanical parameters were used for CKD classification using machine learning models. Results: FEM-derived stiffness estimates showed consistency with stiffness ranges previously reported in human renal MRE studies and also showed measurable differences between healthy and CKD groups. Machine learning models trained on FEM-derived features achieved classification performance up to 86.26% cross-validation accuracy with an AUC of 0.92. Conclusion: FEM-derived elastographic parameters showed potential for distinguishing CKD from healthy tissue and may provide an alternative for renal biomechanical assessment without dedicated MRE acquisition.

A Proxy-Based Method for Measuring Racial Disparities in~Text-to-Image~Generators

A Proxy-Based Method for Measuring Racial Disparities in~Text-to-Image~Generators

Nia Mucher、Ngan Pham、Jason Chin、Aaron Brown、David Evans 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present an automated method for auditing text-to-image generators for representational bias against ground-truth demographic benchmarks. Our approach adapts the racial proxy variables used in U.S. antidiscrimination law to provide a fine-grained comparative analysis of variations in racial representation in generated images. We classify each generated image by race and then compare the model's distribution to publicly available demographic benchmark data to identify patterns of over- and under-representation. We also measure skin tone using CIELAB values and calculate cosine similarity amongst groups of images in order to capture biases that don't show up in demographic comparisons such as differences in image quality across races and skin tones. We report on experiments testing three models (Imagen 4 Fast, Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo, and Flux Schnell) using three categories of proxies (geographic, occupational, and surname) and 50 variants within each category. We find notable variation across the models as well as by proxy category. For example, Imagen 4 Fast overrepresents white classified images more than the other models tested and produces images with lighter, more homogeneous skin tones. Imagen 4 Fast also had the largest representational disparity for Black demographics among surname and geographical prompts. Stable Diffusion 3.5 Large Turbo produces results that overall diverge most from census data but yield the most diverse range of skin tones. Our method yields a generalizable and legally-grounded auditing tool that surfaces detailed information about model outputs without relying on implicit normative definitions of fairness.

We present an automated method for auditing text-to-image generators for representational bias against ground-truth demographic benchmarks. Our approach adapts the racial proxy variables used in U.S. antidiscrimination law to provide a fine-grained comparative analysis of variations in racial representation in generated images. We classify each generated image by race and then compare the model's distribution to publicly available demographic benchmark data to identify patterns of over- and under-representation. We also measure skin tone using CIELAB values and calculate cosine similarity amongst groups of images in order to capture biases that don't show up in demographic comparisons such as differences in image quality across races and skin tones. 2026-11-04

Wide View Synthesis from Overlapping Perspective Cameras in Autonomous Driving

Wide View Synthesis from Overlapping Perspective Cameras in Autonomous Driving

Kaixuan Zhang、Zhipeng Xiong、Minxian Li、Mingwu Ren、Xiatian Zhu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-perspective-camera systems are widely adopted since they provide broad visual coverage for autonomous driving, motivating the synthesis of a unified wide view from spatially separated observations. In this work, we formulate wide view synthesis (WVS), which aims to synthesize a geometrically consistent wide view at a specified virtual viewpoint from multiple perspective cameras. Unlike conventional image stitching, WVS requires reasoning about 3D geometry and visibility to handle depth-dependent parallax and occlusions arising from spatially separated camera centers. While vanilla 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables high-fidelity view synthesis, its reliance on per-scene optimization limits its applicability to driving scenes. Furthermore, WVS introduces additional challenges, *e.g.*, peripheral stretching under perspective projection and photometric inconsistencies across cameras. To address these challenges, we propose **Wide-View Gaussian Splatting (WV-GS)**, a feed-forward Gaussian-splatting framework that synthesizes wide views without per-scene optimization. Specifically, we introduce Piecewise Perspective Rendering (PPR) to mitigate peripheral stretching through local perspective projections sharing a common camera center, and Spatial Affine Photometric Modeling (SAPM) to account for camera-dependent photometric variations during training. We further introduce **WideDrive**, a synthetic benchmark providing dense ground-truth wide views and adapt a LiDAR-projected sparse-reference protocol to real-world datasets. Extensive experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of WV-GS for WVS. Dataset and code will be publicly released.

Extensive experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of WV-GS for WVS. Dataset and code will be publicly released.

Deformable Contact-Aware 3D Object Placement

Deformable Contact-Aware 3D Object Placement

Songhe Wang、David Miller、Charles A. Kamhoua

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Current language-guided 3D object placement typically predicts a rigid 6-DoF pose, ignoring the physical reality that indoor placements are dictated by contact. Consequently, predicted poses often float, interpenetrate supports, or fail to deform soft surfaces. We propose Deformable Contact-Aware Placement (DCAP), a framework that reframes placement as a drop-to-equilibrium process. Rather than predicting a static pose, DCAP constructs a semantic release state that specifies the drop location, object scale, and deformable components, allowing a coupled rigid-deformable simulator to settle the object under gravity and friction. This explicitly separates semantic intent from physical realization. Evaluated on a benchmark of 2,063 prompt-asset-scene cases, DCAP significantly outperforms static baselines. On standard placements, it improves VLM Physics & Naturalness by 21.2 points and human preference by 26.5 points over the strongest raw rigid baseline, while reducing mean penetration depth by 72.3%. Compared to simulated rollout baselines, DCAP reduces residual speed by 72.1%. Ultimately, our results prove that physically accurate 3D placement requires modeling semantic intent and physical realization as strictly separate processes.

Leakage- and Label-Aware Benchmarking of Class-Imbalance Strategies for Multimodal Chest X-Ray and EHR Classification

Leakage- and Label-Aware Benchmarking of Class-Imbalance Strategies for Multimodal Chest X-Ray and EHR Classification

Dang-Khoa Bui、Quang-Huy Tran、Minh-Khoi Nguyen-Bui、Duc-Tuan Ngo、Thanh-Trong Tran 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Benchmarks of class-imbalance strategies for multimodal chest X-ray (CXR) and electronic health record (EHR) prediction are often evaluated on record-level splits of cohorts in which one patient contributes several radiographs. We audit such a benchmark on 29,083 MIMIC-CXR radiographs of 10,479 patients with eight diagnosis targets. First, the record-split benchmark was misleading: a lookup table that copies the label of a development-set radiograph with the same EHR vector nearly matched the best model, and on unseen patients every model fell to the level of predicting every case positive. Second, tracing the labels back to MIMIC-IV explained why: each patient's labels are keyword matches on the diagnosis codes of a single admission, copied, with one EHR vector per patient, to all of the patient's radiographs, so about a third of the radiographs carry labels that differ from the codes of their own admission. Third, under patient-separated evaluation with admission-aligned and rule-refined labels, the initial conclusions do not hold: in the evaluated configurations no imbalance strategy raised the observed mean macro F2 over no augmentation by more than 0.01, tuned tree ensembles showed no consistent advantage over tuned logistic regression, and the released EHR variables stayed at the level of the all-positive baseline, whereas the diagnostic history available at imaging time and image embeddings exceeded it, including, with rule-refined labels, a CheXpert-trained encoder that never saw these radiographs; RAD-DINO had seen 96.6% of them during self-supervised pretraining. Adding either encoder to the diagnostic history gave no statistically significant F2 gain; exploratory AUROC gains were significant with RAD-DINO but not with DenseNet. We provide reusable diagnostics, a leakage-aware protocol and a checklist.

EDGAR: Efficient Diffusion-Driven Gaussian Avatar Refinement via Synthetic Failure

EDGAR: Efficient Diffusion-Driven Gaussian Avatar Refinement via Synthetic Failure

Kwan Yun、Minhyeok Bang、Colette Suhjung Koo、Junyong Noh

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent advances in Gaussian splatting have enabled efficient and photorealistic head avatar reconstruction, yet monocular video-based or image-based methods still suffer from under-constrained geometry and texture, leading to artifacts such as blurring, stretching, and temporal instability. We present EDGAR (Efficient Diffusion-Driven Gaussian Avatar Refinement), a conditional diffusion framework designed to enhance the quality of Gaussian avatars by correcting artifacts while preserving identity fidelity. To train EDGAR, we construct EDGAR-SET, a hybrid triplet dataset composed of real-captured and synthetic-rendered samples, which simulates realistic Gaussian avatar failure cases by pairing degraded renderings with ground-truth and reference images. EDGAR refines degraded frames through a fusion block that injects identity features extracted by the self-supervised Sapiens model into a one-step diffusion backbone, enabling effective artifact removal without sacrificing efficiency. Our EDGAR model incorporates a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture to address diverse degradation types and severity levels. The refined multi-view renderings are subsequently used as pseudo-targets to optimize a single underlying Gaussian avatar, so that the final avatar can be rendered without applying EDGAR to every output frame. Experiments on three different Gaussian avatar reconstruction methods, each evaluated under three different settings, show improvements in both perceptual and reconstruction measures over the raw outputs and an SDEdit-style refinement baseline. In addition, cross-view evaluation demonstrates improved calibrated consistency, while tests under large yaw and open-mouth expressions verify robustness under extreme settings. These results collectively indicate a useful operating regime for recoverable appearance artifacts. The code is available at https://kwanyun.github.io/edgar/

XM4D: 4D Reconstruction through Certifiable Geometric Optimization

XM4D: 4D Reconstruction through Certifiable Geometric Optimization

Haoyu Han、Jack Benarroch Jedlicki、Jiahui Lei、Heng Yang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Accurate 4D reconstruction remains challenging: jointly estimating camera motion, scene geometry, and non-rigid deformation entails highly nonconvex optimization, making global optimality difficult to guarantee. We present XM4D, a framework for accurate and efficient 4D reconstruction built upon certifiably globally optimal geometric optimization. By separating the estimation of camera motion from non-rigid deformation, we formulate both stages as structured quadratically constrained quadratic programs (QCQP) that admit semidefinite programming (SDP) relaxations and verifiable global optimality certificates. For camera motion estimation, we lift static point tracks into 3D using foundational depth prediction models and formulate a scaled bundle adjustment problem that jointly estimates camera poses, depth scales, and static landmarks. By analytically eliminating translations and landmarks, we reduce the problem to a QCQP over scaled rotations. For non-rigid reconstruction, we represent dynamic motion using an embedded deformation graph (EDG) and show that its tracking, rigidity, and temporal regularization objectives similarly reduce to a rotation-only QCQP. Both problems admit SDP relaxations that we solve efficiently with a GPU-accelerated Burer-Monteiro solver based on Riemannian optimization. The recovered geometry further initializes dynamic Gaussian splatting for photometric refinement. Extensive experiments across seven benchmarks demonstrate accurate camera motion estimation on both static and dynamic scenes, efficient optimization that scales to thousands of frames, and state-of-the-art novel-view rendering quality.

OcTreeMamba: Topology-Aware Mamba for 3D Medical Segmentation via Octree Sparsification

OcTreeMamba: Topology-Aware Mamba for 3D Medical Segmentation via Octree Sparsification

Kaixiang Mai、Jiaqi Yu、Yuzhu Ji、Yiqun Zhang、Zhijie Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Accurate segmentation of 3D anatomical structures is crucial to medical image analysis. Recently, Mamba-based approaches have achieved promising segmentation performance in capturing long-range dependencies with linear-time computational complexity. However, heuristic selective scanning strategies inherently disrupt the intrinsic spatial hierarchy and increase computational redundancy, ignoring the inherent sparsity of 3D anatomical structures. To this end, we propose OcTreeMamba, a hierarchical framework that incorporates Octree structures into SSM, achieving 3D topology-aware long-range context feature propagation and integration. Specifically, a Structure Learner performs anatomy-adaptive structured sparsification through hierarchical gating and Gumbel-Softmax sampling, enabling differentiable selection of anatomical regions. The selected regions are subsequently organized by a Dynamic Octree Builder into a multi-level topology that preserves their hierarchical connectivity. Based on the constructed Octree, a bidirectional Tree-SSM propagates and integrates contextual features along connectivity-aware leaf--parent--root and root--parent--leaf trajectories. Extensive experiments on four benchmark datasets show that our proposed OcTreeMamba achieves competitive performance \wrt topological continuity and computational efficiency, particularly for recovering the complex structure on vascular datasets. The code is available at https://github.com/MikeMak123/OcTreeMamba.

Learning Robotic World Models with Semantic and Visual Feedback

Learning Robotic World Models with Semantic and Visual Feedback

Hao Wu、Yuqi Li、Yizhou Zhao、Kun Wang、Xiao Luo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot world models predict action-conditioned future visual states to support robotic planning and decision making. However, visual reconstruction does not fully capture task progress, semantic evaluation alone provides limited constraints on fine-grained visual errors, and homogeneous predictions limit comparative feedback. We present RoboImagine, a framework for post-training autoregressive robotic world models through candidate-future comparisons with semantic and visual feedback. Diverse search promotes diversity among candidate trajectories under shared visual observations, action sequences, and task instructions. A frozen multimodal evaluator provides task-relevant semantic preferences through pairwise video comparisons in both presentation orders, complemented by pixel- and perceptual-level visual consistency feedback that constrains local structural and appearance errors. These signals drive policy optimization through group-relative advantages. Fixed initial context and branch-specific rolling histories support long-sequence generation with bounded context and consistent conditioning between candidate generation and policy optimization. Experiments with separately trained models on RT-1, BridgeV2, and RoboCasa cover real-world and simulated manipulation. On RT-1, under matched initialization, update counts, and evaluation protocols, RoboImagine improves PSNR by 1.26 dB and reduces LPIPS by 11.93% relative to continued supervised fine-tuning. Ablations show that diverse search improves prediction quality and that combined semantic and visual feedback yields higher external multimodal evaluation scores than visual feedback alone while maintaining comparable visual fidelity. Rolling histories further improve long-horizon prediction. These results support improving visual fidelity, task relevance, and long-horizon prediction through candidate comparison and complementary feedback.

Adapters as Data: The Latent Space of LoRAs

Adapters as Data: The Latent Space of LoRAs

Moritz Grävinghoff、Konstantin Wehmeyer、Aron Asefaw、Boris Knyazev、Damian Borth 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

LoRA adapters are usually treated as finished artifacts rather than as a data modality. Prior work has shown that LoRA adapters populate a low-dimensional manifold that a linear PCA basis already organizes well enough to support different operations such as sampling, editing, and inversion. We ask what a learned, nonlinear latent adds beyond this. We train a transformer autoencoder on the same population of LoRA identity adapters, mapping each into a latent space and compare every operation against the identical operation applied directly to the raw adapter weights. The latent preserves the capabilities already available in weight space, sampling diverse identities and editing individual attributes. Beyond preservation, we show that the latent is far more robust to displacement than weight space, degrading into distorted but still valid faces rather than texture. Fixed-dimensional weight-space representations cannot natively host adapters of more than one rank, so we extend our sequential autoencoder to hold mixed-rank adapters within one shared latent. This gives a path toward treating adapter weights of varying architecture as a single, general modality rather than one tied to a fixed rank, and points to a learned, nonlinear latent as the mechanism that could carry this generalization further, toward heterogeneous populations of adapters.

多模态/VLM
2026-11-04

Batch-Conditioned Semantic Anchors for Robust Transductive Adaptation of Vision--Language Models

Batch-Conditioned Semantic Anchors for Robust Transductive Adaptation of Vision--Language Models

Mohammed Rahman Sherif Khan Mohammad、Ardhendu Behera、Sandip Pradhan、Swagat Kumar、Amr Ahmed 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Transductive adaptation improves vision-language models at test time by refining predictions on an unlabeled batch, but current methods face a key trade-off. Aggressive approaches exploit batch structure but can drift when batches are sparse, have prior shift, or cover little of the label space. Conservative methods better preserve CLIP text semantics, but fixed semantic references can under-adapt even when the batch provides strong corrective evidence. We propose JPTA (Joint Prior and Transport Adaptation), a prior-aware framework that casts transductive VLM adaptation as batch-conditioned semantic reference estimation. Instead of treating CLIP text prototypes as fixed priors or freely replaceable, JPTA views them as semantic references that shift toward image-side batch structure only under calibrated evidence. It estimates batch priors and soft image-side prototypes to form transported semantic references whose impact is scaled by batch reliability. Across standard benchmarks, low effective-class regimes, all-class evaluation, and online streams, JPTA consistently outperforms TransCLIP and StatA, with the largest gains on sparse and prior-shifted batches. Anchor-drift, active-support, and absent-class-mass diagnostics show that JPTA fixes effective-class mismatch without unconstrained transductive drift, indicating that robust transductive VLM adaptation should recalibrate semantic references using reliable batch evidence rather than choose between unrestricted transduction and fixed prototypes. Code is available at https://github.com/MR-Sherif/JPTA.git.

2026-11-05

VIPA: View-Invariant Projector-Based Adversarial Attack

VIPA: View-Invariant Projector-Based Adversarial Attack

Jiyu Han、Qingyue Deng、Bingyao Huang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Projector-based adversarial attack aims to physically manipulate real-world scenes by projecting adversarial patterns, thereby causing deep image classifiers to produce incorrect predictions. However, existing stealthy projector-based adversarial attack methods model the project-and-capture process in a single and static view, limiting their view-invariant capability and practical applicability in dynamic environments. In this paper, we introduce View-Invariant Projector-Based Adversarial Attack (VIPA), a novel method designed to overcome these limitations by achieving both view-invariant and classifier-agnostic adversarial attacks in the physical world. We first leverage a view-invariant projector-camera system simulation method to model the physical interactions between projected patterns and real-world surfaces. To ensure robustness and stealthiness of the attack across different views, VIPA optimizes adversarial projections by aggregating simulated attack losses from multiple views. This joint optimization across diverse views helps maintain robustness regardless of the viewpoints. Finally, the optimized patterns are physically projected into real-world scenes, where they successfully fool various classifiers from different viewpoints, thereby enabling robust and practical view-invariant adversarial attacks. Our experiments in both targeted and untargeted attacks demonstrate that VIPA consistently achieves higher attack success rates than existing methods, while also enhancing stealthiness and ensuring minimal perceptual degradation across all tested views.

MammoSeek: A Mammography Agent with selective zooming for grounded reports

MammoSeek: A Mammography Agent with selective zooming for grounded reports

Haoyan Ding、Qian Wang、Lixia Wang、Debiao Li

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Radiologists read a screening mammogram by first forming a global impression of the four views and then selectively magnifying suspicious regions before assigning a BI-RADS assessment. MLLMs, in contrast, see the whole image at a fixed, reduced resolution, which makes sub-millimeter calcifications invisible, and they perform close to chance on mammography. Therefore, we present MammoSeek, a mammography agent that reproduces this reading process. After forming an impression of each breast, the agent performs selective zooming: it decides by itself which detector candidates deserve a closer look and inspects only those on full-resolution views. Guided by a BI-RADS assessment skill, it then assigns a category and generates a localized, lexicon-structured report for each breast; its assessment is combined with image-model scores in a decision layer to decide recall. On an internal (EMBED) and an external (VinDr-Mammo) dataset, \ours reaches exam-level AUCs of 0.824 and 0.818, far above other MLLMs, mammography agent and CNN classifiers. Selective zooming also outperforms both no zooming and forced zooming in localization, structured-report F1 and recall decisions at low cost.

IDPrior: Decoupling Identity Generation from Facial Image Synthesis

IDPrior: Decoupling Identity Generation from Facial Image Synthesis

Fadi Boutros、Meriç Demirörs、Darian Tomašević、Vitomir Štruc、Naser Damer 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Large-scale identity-labeled face datasets are essential for training modern face recognition models, yet collecting and annotating such data is costly and raises privacy and ethical concerns. Existing synthetic face generation methods typically derive identities from predefined class labels, unconditional image generation, or predefined latent sampling strategies, without explicitly modeling the underlying distribution of facial identities. In this paper, we propose IDPrior, a two-stage framework that decouples identity generation from image synthesis. We first learn a diffusion prior in the embedding space of a pretrained face recognition model to model the distribution of facial identities and generate novel identity embeddings. These embeddings are then used to condition a pretrained identity-conditioned diffusion model (DM), enabling the synthesis of multiple images of the same identity. This modular design provides a scalable approach to identity generation while remaining compatible with existing and future identity-conditioned DMs. Experiments indicate that IDPrior generates diverse and well-separated identities while maintaining a closer correspondence to the distribution of authentic identities. Face recognition models trained on datasets generated using IDPrior-generated identities achieve competitive or improved performance compared with existing synthetic-data-based methods across multiple face verification benchmarks. Code and generated datasets are publicly available at https://github.com/fdbtrs/IDperturb.

目标检测/分割机器人视觉医学图像
2026-11-05

Towards Scalable Diagnostic-Driven Whole-Slide Image Retrieval for Pathology Archives

Towards Scalable Diagnostic-Driven Whole-Slide Image Retrieval for Pathology Archives

Jingsong Liu、Youssef Wally Han Li、Weiwei Zhou、Jing Dai、Elisabeth Wetzer 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The growing adoption of digital pathology has rapidly expanded collections of hematoxylin-and-eosin whole-slide images, yet efficient navigation through these archives remains challenging, as metadata is often inconsistent or unavailable. Content-based image retrieval addresses this challenge by identifying similar cases directly from visual representations instead of manual annotations or metadata. Existing retrieval systems have demonstrated promising performance but largely rely on handcrafted indexing pipelines and predefined sampling strategies, constraining scalability and generalization. Here, we present CLEAR-WSI (Constant-Length Embedding for Automatic Retrieval of Whole-Slide Images) as a scalable, diagnostic-driven slide-retrieval framework, validated on both public and clinical data. Using a fixed-length slide embedding, CLEAR-WSI refines retrieved candidates with either a predicted diagnostic label or the ground-truth label, depending on the retrieval setting. Newly processed cases are subsequently added to the expanding memory bank, allowing the retrieval archive to grow over time. On a comprehensive benchmark consisting of two public and one private clinical datasets representing four retrieval tasks, CLEAR-WSI improves Precision@5 over the strongest evaluated baseline, with gains on CAMELYON16 (3.10), BRACS-1 (2.75), BRACS-2 (1.95), and X-HER2 (3.75). Additional ablation and latency studies further examine the contribution of each component and highlight its potential for scalable deployment.

InnerLogic-Surg: An Open Surgical Foundation Model via Continual Pretraining

InnerLogic-Surg: An Open Surgical Foundation Model via Continual Pretraining

Kun Yuan、Hao Ding、Yiqing Shen、Gasper Podobnik、Benjamin Killeen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision foundation models are the backbone of modern surgical image analysis, yet most surgical systems still rely on encoders pretrained on natural images or on specialist models trained from scratch on comparatively small datasets. Pretraining encoders on large amounts of surgery-specific data has the potential to generally enhance the performance of surgical image analysis methods simply by providing stronger backbones. Here we present InnerLogic-Surg, an open family of surgical vision encoders (21M--300M parameters) obtained by continually pretraining a transformer-based image encoder on millions of surgical images curated from 11,965 internet-sourced surgical videos spanning ~2,900 hours and more than fifteen procedure types. Our training recipe inherits DINOv3's pretraining stage to preserve representation ability after surgical adaptation, and additionally applies a learned feature-upsampling module for high-resolution dense prediction at low token cost. Across a benchmark covering workflow, instrument and anatomy recognition, segmentation, detection, and monocular depth, under the same architecture, the largest InnerLogic-Surg model achieves the best linear-probe performance in all 20 surgical tasks. We demonstrate that surgical continual pretraining yields transferable representations that generalize across procedures, anatomical regions, and task types, showing our surgical image encoders as a promising and scalable alternative to the procedure-specific representation learning approaches that have traditionally dominated surgical computer vision.

2026-11-05

Enhanced MRI brain tumor detection using deep learning in conjunction with explainable AI SHAP based diverse and multi feature analysis

Enhanced MRI brain tumor detection using deep learning in conjunction with explainable AI SHAP based diverse and multi feature analysis

Asif Rahman、Maqsood Hayat、Nadeem Iqbal、Fawaz Khaled Alarfaj、Salem Alkhalaf 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Recent innovations in medical imaging have markedly improved brain tumor identification, surpassing conventional diagnostic approaches that suffer from low resolution, radiation exposure, and limited contrast. Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) is pivotal in precise and accurate tumor characterization owing to its high-resolution, non-invasive nature. This study investigates the synergy among multiple feature representation schemes such as local Binary Patterns (LBP), Gabor filters, Discrete Wavelet Transform, Fast Fourier Transform, Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN), and Gray-Level Run Length Matrix alongside five learning algorithms namely: k-nearest Neighbor, Random Forest, Support Vector Classifier (SVC), and probabilistic neural network (PNN), and CNN. Empirical findings indicate that LBP in conjunction with SVC and CNN obtained high specificity and accuracy, rendering it a promising method for MRI-based tumor diagnosis. Further to investigate the contribution of LBP, Statistical analysis chi-square and p-value tests are used to confirm the significant impact of LBP feature space for identification of brain Tumor. In addition, The SHAP analysis was used to identify the most important features in classification. In a small dataset, CNN obtained 97.8% accuracy while SVC yielded 98.06% accuracy. In subsequent analysis, a large benchmark dataset is also utilized to evaluate the performance of learning algorithms in order to investigate the generalization power of the proposed model. CNN achieves the highest accuracy of 98.9%, followed by SVC at 96.7%. These results highlight CNN's effectiveness in automated, high-precision tumor diagnosis. Code: https://github.com/asifrahman557/BrainTumorDetection

Code: https://github.com/asifrahman557/BrainTumorDetection

目标检测/分割
2026-11-05

THG-Omni: Traceable Human-Centric Graph Supervision for Video Reasoning

THG-Omni: Traceable Human-Centric Graph Supervision for Video Reasoning

Jisheng Dang、Kai Wei、Ronghao Lin、Bimei Wang、Hong Peng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Omni-modal models can answer questions about video, but their predictions rarely show which participant, event, or interval supports an answer. Moreover, answer-level supervision alone does not teach a model to interpret structured video evidence. To systematically mitigate this issue, we present THG-Omni, a human-centric graph-supervision framework that links Segment Anything Model~3 participant tracks with temporally grounded Qwen3-VL annotations. Candidate semantic claims are checked against the source video and available audio before they are used to construct training questions, while track geometry provides explicit spatial and temporal supervision. The resulting questions teach an omni-modal reasoning model to interpret participant attributes, actions, affect, interactions, and their timing from a structured graph. At inference, THG-Omni uses a predicted graph constructed from the evaluation video. Experiments on IntentBench show consistent improvements over the unadapted backbone across social reasoning, emotion understanding, and deception perception. On NExT-QA, THG-Omni achieves 89.54\% accuracy with the matched graph, compared with 85.10\% without graph input. Further validation experiments show that compact graph input achieves 78.22\% accuracy, compared with 78.02\% using the full graph and 77.58\% without graph input. A complementary diagnostic demonstrates that the predicted graph preserves evidence useful for answering questions even without the source video and audio. Together, these findings show that THG-Omni improves human-centric video reasoning while grounding its evidence in identifiable participants and events. Our codes are available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/review-n94hhfcwp5666/

Our codes are available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/review-n94hhfcwp5666/

FREE-LIVO: Physically Decoupled Yet Tightly Coupled LiDAR-Inertial-Visual Odometry

FREE-LIVO: Physically Decoupled Yet Tightly Coupled LiDAR-Inertial-Visual Odometry

Reon Tabata、Kenji Koide、Shuji Oishi、Taku Okawara、Mizuho Aoki 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

LiDAR-Inertial-Visual Odometry (LIVO) integrates complementary LiDAR, camera, and IMU measurements to achieve robust state estimation. However, most existing methods assume a fixed relative pose and accurate time synchronization between sensors. Toward modular and heterogeneous robotic systems, this paper presents FREE-LIVO, a tightly coupled odometry method that requires neither a rigid LiDAR-camera rig nor LiDAR-camera time synchronization. The proposed method uses a pretrained image feature matcher to establish correspondences between a camera image and a dense LiDAR intensity image generated from a single LiDAR scan using a generative model. From the resulting 3D point-to-pixel correspondences, we construct reprojection factors that directly constrain LiDAR and camera poses at different acquisition times. These factors are jointly optimized in a single factor graph together with constraints from point cloud registration, visual feature tracking, and the IMU measurements associated with each sensor. On six sequences from the public M3DGR dataset, FREE-LIVO achieves an average absolute trajectory error (ATE) RMSE of 0.45 m without provided LiDAR-camera extrinsic parameters and exhibits minimal accuracy degradation under time synchronization errors of up to 0.5 s. Furthermore, experiments with the LiDAR and camera mounted on separate robots demonstrate that the method can jointly estimate the trajectories of independently moving sensors.
