La-JEPA: Compact Robot Policies with Latent World Modeling

La-JEPA: Compact Robot Policies with Latent World Modeling

Mateo Clémente、George Z. Xue、Amin Abyaneh、Liheng Ma、Rui Heng Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World-action models adapt pretrained video generators, and vision-language-action models build on pretrained vision-language backbones; both tie policy architecture and inference cost to large transformers designed for generation rather than control. It remains unclear how much these generation-oriented choices affect control. In this work, we introduce Latent Action JEPA (La-JEPA), a compact world-action model whose policy transformer is trained from scratch in the feature space of a pretrained V-JEPA 2.1 encoder. Following Fast-WAM, La-JEPA learns to predict future features jointly with actions during training and generates only actions at inference. Among policies without embodied pretraining, La-JEPA achieves state-of-the-art performance in clean and randomized RoboTwin 2.0 scenes and on LIBERO-Plus with correct instructions, while using far fewer parameters than the strongest competing policies. It runs substantially faster than Fast-WAM at comparable performance and outperforms the closest JEPA-based methods, LeapBot-WA and JEPA-WAM, on LIBERO-Plus and RoboTwin 2.0 without their pretrained policy backbones. On four real-robot tasks, its average success is comparable to pi0.5 and above non-embodied-pretrained baselines. Through a design study of the choices behind La-JEPA, we show that JEPA features outperform video VAE latents, most clearly out of distribution, and that training-time future prediction choices significantly impact the policy even when future prediction is skipped at inference. We correct the LIBERO-Plus instructions, as the officially released evaluator corrupts them for most variants, and re-evaluate several strong open baselines. Without a generative pretrained policy backbone, La-JEPA is better and faster than far larger policies, suggesting that robot foundation models may benefit from architectures designed for control rather than inherited from generation.

Detecting Weather Fronts from Satellite Observations: What Does Reanalysis Add?

Detecting Weather Fronts from Satellite Observations: What Does Reanalysis Add?

Ioannis Dravilas、Miruna Stoicescu、Roope Tervo、Carlos Horn、Oriol Hinojo Comellas 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Weather fronts are transition regions between air masses with contrasting thermodynamic properties and are associated with mid-latitude precipitation and severe weather. We investigate machine learning (ML) methods for detecting weather fronts and quantify the contribution of reanalysis data to performance. We compare two configurations of the same ML model for pixel-wise frontal classification over the North Atlantic and Europe: (1) using only geostationary infrared satellite observations, and (2) additionally using 28 ERA5 reanalysis fields. The satellite-only model achieves a neighbourhood critical success index at a 150 km tolerance (CSI@150) of $0.402$, which lies within the range of agreement between analyses from different meteorological services. The satellite$+$ERA5 model achieves $0.575$, an improvement of $0.173$. Our analysis shows that low-level winds are the least predictable from the satellite representation ($R^2 = 0.23$, compared with $0.88$ for temperature), while removing winds or moisture causes the largest decreases in detection performance.

Describe Your Facial Expressions by Linking Image Encoders and Large Language Models

Describe Your Facial Expressions by Linking Image Encoders and Large Language Models

Yujian Yuan、Jiabei Zeng、Shiguang Shan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents a novel task of describing human facial expressions of a facial image in natural language, which captures the nuances of facial actions and emotional states beyond traditional emotion categories or facial action units (AUs). To achieve the facial expression captioning model, we propose a three-stage training framework that trains a vision-to-language model using synthesized image-text pairs and the BLIP-2 pre-training techniques. To overcome the challenge of missing training image-text pairs for facial expression captioning, we propose a strategy that involves synthesizing and combining captions using GPT-3.5 and existing annotations on either emotion categories or AUs. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our method in generating captions that describe details of facial actions and emotions, as well as the inferential relationship between them, even when those emotions are not present in the training data. It is also demonstrated that the vision-to-language task enhances the performance of the intermediate visual features on both AU detection and emotion classification tasks.

2026-11-05

FreeOcc: Decoupling Ego-Motion for Efficient 4D Occupancy Forecasting via Continuous Flow Matching

FreeOcc: Decoupling Ego-Motion for Efficient 4D Occupancy Forecasting via Continuous Flow Matching

Zeping Zhang、Zhuoya Zhao、Samy Metari、Robert Laganière

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Current 4D occupancy world models use heavy cross-attention or deep recurrence to disentangle ego-motion from scene dynamics. This requires significant computational capacity. However, this effort is largely redundant since the ego-vehicle's intended future motion is provided by the downstream motion planner. We present FreeOcc, a lightweight continuous-time world model that introduces explicit $SE(2)$ geometric decoupling into a conditional flow-matching framework. By mathematically factoring out self-motion, we create a purely dynamic feature space. This space supports attention-free temporal fusion and allows physics-constrained ODEs to operate strictly on residual agent kinematics. To maximize deployment efficiency, FreeOcc introduces an uncertainty-aware blending mechanism. This routes well-observed voxels to a single-pass decoder, reserving the ODE solver exclusively for ambiguous, high-uncertainty regions. Requiring only 28.3M parameters-roughly 40% of comparable models-our optimal ensemble mode (FreeOcc-E) runs at 27 FPS. On nuScenes, FreeOcc achieves state-of-the-art forecasting performance with a 0.47 m Near Field Chamfer Distance (NFCD) and 32.1% mIoU. Furthermore, the model transfers zero-shot to SemanticKITTI. It also proves highly robust, retaining 92.8% of its ideal mIoU under significant sensor packet loss and timestamp jitter.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM视频理解
2026-11-05

Spatiotemporal Dynamics-Aware Industrial Anomaly Detection and Reasoning

Spatiotemporal Dynamics-Aware Industrial Anomaly Detection and Reasoning

S-Wei Chen、Siangyu Yang、Yi-Chen Jan、Chun-Yi Lee、Shao-Yuan Lo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Industrial Anomaly Detection (IAD) is a long-established problem that aims to detect defects in industrial products during manufacturing for quality control. Most existing studies focus on static visual patterns as the basis for detection. However, in real manufacturing environments, many anomalies are dynamics-based, such as irregular movements that cannot be perceived from static cues alone. We observe that even recent Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM)-based approaches fall short in identifying such dynamic anomalies due to limited temporal modeling capability. To address this, we introduce MUSTARD: MUltimodal SpatioTemporal Anomaly Reasoning and Detection, a framework that equips MLLMs with fine-grained spatiotemporal dynamics awareness for detecting and reasoning about dynamics-based defects. MUSTARD introduces optical-flow-guided keyframe selection and augmented modalities to capture critical motion irregularities through local motion patterns and global dynamic evolution. Its normal pattern-based reasoning pipeline requires only a few normal reference samples as the detection basis, avoiding any model updates. This enables efficient adaptation across various products and provides high decision interpretability. MUSTARD is the first MLLM-based framework for dynamics-based IAD. Experiments demonstrate its superior performance in detecting spatiotemporal anomalies and providing plausible explanations. The code will be made publicly available.

Learning Noise Optimization for Reward Alignment in Few-Step Generative Models

Learning Noise Optimization for Reward Alignment in Few-Step Generative Models

Hongeun Kim、Jeongsol Kim、Jong Chul Ye

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reward alignment for diffusion- and flow-based generative models is often formulated over stochastic trajectories, making it less suitable for few-step deterministic generators. Initial noise optimization provides a trajectory-free alternative, but existing gradient-free methods require costly per-sample search at inference. We introduce LeNO (Learning Noise Optimization), which amortizes reward-guided initial noise optimization into a learned residual transport while keeping the pretrained generator frozen. LeNO learns an anchor-conditioned distribution over noise corrections using Local GRPO and scalar rewards, eliminating iterative test-time optimization. For Gaussian initial noise with norm-preserving projection, we derive a tractable upper bound on the KL divergence between the corrected noise distribution and the original prior, providing a principled regularization objective. We evaluate LeNO on few-step text-to-image and protein structure generation, including expensive black-box designability rewards. Across both domains, LeNO improves target rewards with a single noise correction at inference while largely preserving generation quality and diversity.

EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling

EgoGeneralist: Unifying Egocentric Action-Video Tasks through Video-Native Modeling

Yanlin Jin、Bowen Yang、Cheng Zhang、Yifan Yang、Zhe Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern video models possess strong visual priors, yet do not natively represent human action in metric 3D space. We present EgoGeneralist, which adapts pretrained video generators to unify action-conditioned egocentric video generation, pose estimation, and joint action-video generation. Our role-adaptive action renderer provides video-native representations for multidirectional action-video learning, making metric motion usable as both a spatial control signal and a recoverable generation target. Cross-stream action-video coupling establishes close correspondence between metric motion and visual dynamics through lightweight adaptation while preserving the pretrained visual prior. Together, these designs establish a generalist framework spanning five action-video tasks. Despite adaptation only on controlled laboratory recordings, EgoGeneralist generalizes to diverse unseen scenes and interactions with effective action control and visual quality, bridging pretrained visual knowledge and metric 3D human motion.

VisionSIM: Beyond Idealized Sensors - A Multi-Modal Simulator for Robot Perception

VisionSIM: Beyond Idealized Sensors - A Multi-Modal Simulator for Robot Perception

Sacha Jungerman、Kaustubh Sadekar、Avery Gump、Sriram Narayanan、Shantanu Gupta 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robotic perception increasingly relies on heterogeneous sensor suites combining cameras with emerging modalities (event, SPAD, iToF/dToF, and thermal). Yet existing simulators idealize these sensors, hiding critical modality-specific failure modes that limit real-world deployment. We present VisionSIM, a unified, physically grounded simulation platform that elevates sensor realism to a first-class concern by modeling the raw physical measurement process for diverse conventional and emerging modalities. We expose fine-grained control over physical sensor parameters, reproducing failure modes overlooked by general-purpose simulators: pileup and glare for LiDAR; phase wrapping, saturation, and motion artifacts for iToF; refractory drops for event cameras; and history-dependent emission for thermal cameras. This enables principled cross-modality comparison on identical scenes and trajectories. We release the platform and a large indoor dataset with synchronized raw measurements and rich ground-truth annotations.

OraRefer: Segmentation-Guided Multi-Photograph Fusion for Oral Cancer Screening with Guaranteed Patient-Level Sensitivity

OraRefer: Segmentation-Guided Multi-Photograph Fusion for Oral Cancer Screening with Guaranteed Patient-Level Sensitivity

Rupam Mukherjee、Mehakdeep Singh、Shirin Dasgupta、Subhamoy Mandal

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Oral cancer and the oral potentially malignant disorders (OPMD) that can precede it are visible on the oral mucosa, which makes smartphone photographs a promising tool for screening. Deep learning models for this task classify individual photographs. However, screening refers patients, not photographs: a patient is usually photographed several times, and the referral decision must miss no more than a set share of patients with disease. We propose OraRefer, a patient-level screening model that reads each photograph with a segmentation-guided foundation model that conditions its class decision on the predicted lesion, combines all photographs of a patient with risk factors, and refers through a threshold with a finite-sample sensitivity guarantee. The model localizes the mouth itself, so inference needs no annotation. On 3000 photographs of 714 patients, patient-level fusion reaches an AUROC of 0.919 and outperforms attention-based multiple-instance learning. At a 90% sensitivity target, OraRefer detects 95% of patients with OPMD or cancer and misses 3 of 61 patients with cancer, at a specificity of 0.610. Because the threshold is calibrated without retraining, each clinic can set its own guaranteed operating point on a local calibration sample.

A Theoretical Analysis of a Bilevel Optimization Formulation for Combined Local-Global Optical Flow Models

A Theoretical Analysis of a Bilevel Optimization Formulation for Combined Local-Global Optical Flow Models

El Mostafa Kalmoun

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper analyzes a bilevel formulation for learning the regularization parameter of combined local-global (CLG) optical flow models. The lower-level problem computes the flow from a Lucas--Kanade data term and a quadratic or total variation (TV) regularizer, and the upper-level problem selects the parameter that minimizes the squared distance to a ground-truth flow. The setting is finite-dimensional, and a full-rank assumption makes the lower-level solution unique. For the quadratic model we derive sufficient conditions for the existence of an optimal parameter, for every optimal parameter to be positive, and for strict local optimality. For TV regularization an optimal parameter always exists, because the lower-level solution vanishes for large parameters, and we give a sufficient condition for positivity that accounts for the coupling of the two flow components through the local structure tensors. Synthetic examples illustrate the conditions, including cases in which they fail.

4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering

4D Point Splatting for mmWave Radar Micro-Doppler Differentiable Rendering

Adnan Armouti、Guanyue Qian、Rajalakshmi Nandakumar

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Millimeter-wave radar resolves the micrometer displacements of a moving body as micro-Doppler, enabling gait, gesture, activity, pose and vital-sign sensing. Each task relies on a downstream model trained on real data, yet radar datasets lag orders of magnitude behind vision and language. Brute-force collection will not close that gap, and existing synthesis methods (forward-only simulators, learned generators and static-scene inverse renderers) cannot fit a real capture of a moving body, leaving a sim2real gap. We present 4D Point Splatting (4DPS), a physics-based differentiable renderer that fits an explicit moving scene to a real capture. Using the synchronized camera to initialize the room and body, we represent the scene as oriented points carrying material, normal and velocity, and splat each point into range and Doppler bins. Our output is the raw complex return a real radar records, so we apply the same signal processing on the rendered data to obtain any representation for training any SOTA downstream model. We fit 235 sequences on HuPR and 100 on RT-Pose, two datasets with different radars. Our reconstructions set the state of the art, with median correlations of 0.940 and 0.830 on range-azimuth, 0.829 and 0.695 on range-Doppler, and 0.988 and 0.966 on micro-Doppler. Our renders also set the state of the art in downstream sim2real gap. On RT-Pose, a pose model trained on them reaches 21.9 cm MPJPE on real data, against 16.7 cm for real training and 127.6 cm for the RF-Genesis baseline, where both the rendered- and real-trained models learn nearly the same features. Our custom CUDA kernels fit a sequence in 5.7 minutes on HuPR and 12.9 on RT-Pose on one H100, making dataset-scale inverse rendering of mmWave radar possible for the first time. This work can enable synthetic data generation for any human-centered downstream task and model, and scene editing for data augmentation.

InstantHand: Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting for Animatable Hand Avatar Reconstruction from a Single Image

InstantHand: Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting for Animatable Hand Avatar Reconstruction from a Single Image

Chanho Kim、Muhammad Salman Ali、Kyeonghwan Gwak、Muhammad Shaheryar、Incheol Park 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Hand avatar reconstruction typically relies on multi-view capture or per-subject optimization, limiting its scalability. We introduce InstantHand, a feed-forward framework that constructs an animatable 3D Gaussian hand avatar from a single image using off-the-shelf MANO, camera, and mask estimates, without test-time optimization. Image-averaged losses provide insufficient supervision for alpha leakage, which thickens thin finger contours. To address this issue, we propose a Soft Alpha Band regularizer that concentrates supervision on silhouette boundaries. Furthermore, colors regressed from image features can drift toward a learned prior even in observed regions. Our Reliability-gated Appearance Transfer preserves observed appearance by transferring source colors according to each Gaussian's rendering contribution, while completing unobserved regions using the learned prior conditioned on global source identity. InstantHand constructs an avatar in 2.84 seconds and renders at 161 FPS, matching per-subject-optimized baselines on InterHand2.6M and remaining competitive in zero-shot evaluation on HanCo.

ManimCode: Learning Visual Self-Correction for Educational Animation

ManimCode: Learning Visual Self-Correction for Educational Animation

Zhengyang Liang、Lizi Liao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Manim supports precise, programmatic video generation, but a successfully rendered educational animation can still have visual defects, missing explanations, or incorrect content. Rendering does not directly identify the code changes needed to correct these visual failures, so feedback-driven revision is needed. Existing agentic frameworks use iterative rendering and revision but typically rely on large models, often proprietary. Existing Manim datasets primarily contain final programs and often lack quality verification and the trajectories of feedback and revisions needed to learn this refinement process. We introduce ManimData and ManimCode, a data and training framework for feedback-driven educational animation generation. We construct ManimData through a render-guided pipeline that iteratively generates, executes, reviews, and revises Manim programs with retrieved API documentation. Its 24,034 render-verified, quality-filtered records preserve intermediate programs, execution errors, visual critiques, and corrective revisions. We use role-conditioned supervised fine-tuning on these trajectories to train ManimCode, a single 4B model, to write, review, and revise animation programs. To improve correction of errors that differ from those in the collected trajectories, we then train the Reviser with reinforcement learning on self-generated states, using rendering outcomes and reference-free quality feedback. ManimCode achieves 96.6% and 97.0% render success on ManimData-test and ManimBench, compared with 52.6% and 82.0% for the untrained 4B model under the same inference setup, while also improving video quality scores from 2.48 to 2.94 and from 3.00 to 3.44. Integrating ManimCode into two long-form educational video generation systems increases completed videos from 19/117 to 107/117 and from 53/239 to 239/239, alongside improvements in educational effectiveness and visual quality.

The Geometry of Simulated Perspectives: Dissociating Representation and Behaviour in a Multimodal Language Model

The Geometry of Simulated Perspectives: Dissociating Representation and Behaviour in a Multimodal Language Model

Mathis Immertreu、Fitim Abdullahu、Thomas Kinfe、Claus Metzner、Helmut Grabner 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal language models can simulate different observers, yet it remains unclear how assigned perspectives reshape their internal representations and whether these changes govern their judgements. Here, we investigate the representational geometry of persona-conditioned visual interestingness in Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct. Across 100 images and 53 prompting conditions, we systematically vary persona content and the linguistic framing used to introduce it, while recording behavioural ratings and layer-wise hidden states. We observe a systematic reorganisation of representational geometry across processing depth: early layers primarily encode image identity, whereas persona-specific structures emerge in later layers. Strikingly, prompt framing reshapes internal representations more strongly than persona identity, whereas persona content has a greater impact on behavioural judgements. This dissociation is further illustrated by minimal identity statements that alter representations even without changing responses. These findings demonstrate that representational distance does not necessarily reflect behavioural relevance. More broadly, they reveal how simulated perspectives emerge as distinct structures within the representational space of artificial cognitive systems, highlighting a fundamental distinction between the geometry of internal representations and their functional consequences.

2026-11-05

GEKey: Learning Geodesic Eccentric 3D Keypoints via Self-Supervision

GEKey: Learning Geodesic Eccentric 3D Keypoints via Self-Supervision

Pierre Onghena、Santiago Velasco-Forero、Beatriz Marcotegui

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Point clouds represent 3D object geometry as sets of spatial coordinates. A fundamental task in their analysis is keypoint detection, i.e., identifying a sparse subset of points that captures the most salient features. While supervised methods rely on manual annotations, unsupervised approaches leverage intrinsic geometric properties to localize keypoints without human input. We introduce GEKey, a self-supervised framework that uses the geodesic eccentricity transform, an SE(3)-invariant geometric measure, to identify instance-specific salient points. These points serve as pseudo-labels to train a model that aligns its predictions with the object's intrinsic structure. GEKey is simple, efficient, and robust, preserving intra-class variation by balancing instance-specific and category-level structure. Extensive experiments on ShapeNetCoreV2, KeypointNet, CAPE, ITOP, and DeformingThings4D show state-of-the-art performance in both keypoint detection and alignment, with strong resilience to noise, downsampling, deformations, and real-world conditions. The code and pretrained models are available at https://github.com/Pierreoo/GEKey.

Which Objects Stay? Learning Hierarchical Persistence Beliefs for Lifelong Open-Vocabulary Object Maps

Which Objects Stay? Learning Hierarchical Persistence Beliefs for Lifelong Open-Vocabulary Object Maps

Thanh Nguyen Canh、Haolan Zhang、Nak Young Chong

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Lifelong object maps must estimate whether an object remains at its previously observed location after it leaves view. Existing persistence filters rely on class-based survival priors, making them difficult to apply reliably in open-vocabulary settings. We introduce HiPer, a hierarchical persistence filter that derives persistence beliefs directly from data rather than semantic categories. For a new object, HiPer retrieves visually and geometrically similar training instances and estimates an initial survival rate from their observed histories, without requiring class labels. This estimate initializes a conjugate Beta belief over the object's survival probability, which is updated in closed form using detections and missed observations under Rosen's persistence-filter framework. When an object is re-identified at a new location, its persistence belief is transferred with the object, enabling the map to learn object-specific mobility over time. Experiments on 120 held-out simulated sequences show that HiPer consistently achieves the best performance among competing methods. Retrieval-based priors outperform CLIP-derived label priors, and object-specific online learning provides further gains. Results on 47 held-out 3RScan environments demonstrate that the proposed retrieval-based prior also outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in real-world settings.

Training-Free Predictive Spatio-Temporal Token Pruning for Few-Step Autoregressive Video Diffusion

Training-Free Predictive Spatio-Temporal Token Pruning for Few-Step Autoregressive Video Diffusion

Jia Li、Xurui Peng、Xing Wang、Hayden Kwok-Hay So

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Few-step autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk and have made efficient video generation practical. However, further accelerating these models toward real-time generation remains challenging, as few-step distillation leaves little cross-step redundancy for existing training-free caching methods to exploit. We revisit this regime and identify two token-level redundancy patterns: cross-frame redundancy from static content shared across adjacent frames, and cross-step redundancy from tokens that converge early during denoising. Crucially, we find that early clean estimates along the model's own denoising trajectory provide an effective online signal for locating both patterns, requiring no retraining, auxiliary predictor, or reference video. Based on these findings, we propose PreSTP (Predictive Spatio-Temporal Token Pruning), a training-free framework that constructs a unified token-wise redundancy mask from cross-frame similarity and cross-step convergence. Tokens marked as redundant skip subsequent computation under a consistent last-computed-value restoration rule, and a content-adaptive mask reuse strategy further reduces dense probing cost. On 5-second generation, PreSTP achieves 1.52x-3.20x speedups and a better efficiency-fidelity trade-off than coarse-grained caching baselines such as TeaCache and FlowCache. On 30-second generation, it reaches up to 1.96x speedup while improving VBench-Long and suppressing long-horizon drift. Notably, it continues to reduce inference cost even in the extreme one-step regime, where cross-step redundancy vanishes entirely and coarse step-wise caching is no longer applicable.

Few-step autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk and have made efficient video generation practical. However, further accelerating these models toward real-time generation remains challenging, as few-step distillation leaves little cross-step redundancy for existing training-free caching methods to exploit. We revisit this regime and identify two token-level redundancy patterns: cross-frame redundancy from static content shared across adjacent frames, and cross-step redundancy from tokens that converge early during denoising. Crucially, we find that early clean estimates along the model's own denoising trajectory provide an effective online signal for locating both patterns, requiring no retraining, auxiliary predictor, or reference video. Based on these findings, we propose PreSTP (Predictive Spatio-Temporal Token Pruning), a training-free framework that constructs a unified token-wise redundancy mask from cross-frame similarity and cross-step convergence. Tokens marked as redundant skip subsequent computation under a consistent last-computed-value restoration rule, and a content-adaptive mask reuse strategy further reduces dense probing cost. On 5-second generation, PreSTP achieves 1.52x-3.20x speedups and a better efficiency-fidelity trade-off than coarse-grained caching baselines such as TeaCache and FlowCache. On 30-second generation, it reaches up to 1.96x speedup while improving VBench-Long and suppressing long-horizon drift. Notably, it continues to reduce inference cost even in the extreme one-step regime, where cross-step redundancy vanishes entirely and coarse step-wise caching is no longer applicable.

MessyNav: Zero-Shot Navigation via Manipulation in Messy Environments

MessyNav: Zero-Shot Navigation via Manipulation in Messy Environments

Kenneth Llontop、Cherie Ho、Dylan Zhou、Anuva Banwasi、Priya Sundaresan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Real-world environments are messy, with paths often blocked by everyday objects like chairs and toys, requiring robots to perform interactive navigation by manipulating obstacles to clear a path. Existing methods rely on extensive simulation-based learning or per-scene instructions specifying which objects to avoid or manipulate. They struggle to generalize across novel objects, diverse environments, and varied contexts such as a cart of fragile glassware that should be avoided while an empty one can be pushed. We formalize this problem as open-world interactive navigation and present MessyNav, a zero-shot framework based on vision-language models (VLMs). At its core, MessyNav performs explicit VLM-based affordance reasoning before task planning to decide whether to swipe, pick up, or avoid an object and produce a high-level plan. Evaluated on a mobile manipulator across real-world indoor and outdoor environments, MessyNav improves success rate by 27 percentage points (pp) over the strongest baseline in real-robot trials and achieves statistically significant gains in a larger-scale offline evaluation, without task-specific training.

FFMT: Frequency-Factorized Multi-Rate Tokenization for Music-to-Dance Generation

FFMT: Frequency-Factorized Multi-Rate Tokenization for Music-to-Dance Generation

Qirong Guo、Ruijie Li、Zeyan Li、Sicheng Shu、Guanzhi Ding 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Dance motion combines slowly varying body configurations with rapid local movements. We investigate how explicit temporal-frequency decomposition can guide the temporal resolution and codebook capacity of discrete motion representations. We introduce frequency-factorized motion tokenization (FFMT), which separates root-relative motion into a low-frequency choreographic scaffold and a complementary high-frequency residual. FFMT uses a lower token rate and a smaller codebook for the low-frequency component, and a higher token rate and a larger codebook for the high-frequency residual. This representation further induces a plan-then-articulate autoregressive factorization: low-frequency tokens establish coarse choreography, while high-frequency tokens realize local motion details conditioned on the generated plan and music. Experiments on AIST++ and FineDance show strong distributional fidelity while preserving realistic diversity and music--motion synchronization. Ablations consistently support frequency factorization, asymmetric temporal allocation, and low-to-high conditioning.

Distributed Geometric Optimization and Control of Rigid Differential-Drive Formations from Relative-Position Measurements

Distributed Geometric Optimization and Control of Rigid Differential-Drive Formations from Relative-Position Measurements

Thanacha Choopojcharoen、Erman Selim、Ramisha Anjum、Baris Fidan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Distance based formation control fixes the shape of a robot team but leaves its position and orientation unconstrained. We regulate all three for differential drive robots that have only one absolute position anchor, absolute headings, body frame relative position measurements, and communication with their neighbors. We derive a two layer estimation control scheme directly from optimization. The first half of the result is a set of graph-proximal estimators that reconstruct the centroid, the anchor vector, and the centered coordinates. Exact proximal updates reach consensus in finitely many steps, and a finite number of primal dual iterations leaves a bounded reconstruction error. The controller combines proximal centroid and orientation steps with distance gradient shape regulation, and a finite rotation removes the orientation drift caused by the shape step. A discrete Lyapunov analysis gives local exponential convergence once the estimators have converged. We validate the method in ROS 2 Gazebo and in experiments with five physical robots.

PhySA: Decoupling Aesthetic and Physical Rewards for Humanoid Locomotion

PhySA: Decoupling Aesthetic and Physical Rewards for Humanoid Locomotion

Yunwan Fu、Sicheng He、Jiageng Mao、Yue Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humanoid locomotion policies trained without an explicit smoothness objective tend to produce jerky, high-energy gaits that stress actuators. Vision-language models (VLMs) offer an appealing reward source: given a rendered rollout, a VLM returns an aesthetic quality score on [0,10] without task-specific feature engineering. We find, however, that aesthetic preference and physical smoothness point in opposite directions: across four production VLMs, clips with higher joint acceleration receive higher aesthetic scores (mean Pearson r=+0.66). We study PhySA (Physical Smoothness and Aesthetics), which keeps the two on separate, independently removable reward channels: a sparse, asynchronous VLM score and a phase-triggered correction channel that adjusts physical penalty weights from simulator state. Ablations attribute the smoothness gain to the correction channel. On MuJoCo Humanoid-v5 at 2.6M steps, PhySA lowers joint acceleration by 26 to 32% relative to vanilla PPO, Text2Reward, and Eureka (three seeds), at the cost of shorter episodes. In a single extended run it lowers joint acceleration by 59.5% relative to Prompt2Policy, while a three-seed replication shows a smaller, non-significant reduction (7.2%). The gain does not transfer to IsaacLab or the Unitree G1, even after rescaling the smoothness term.

Real-Time Event-Based LED Tracking and Pose Estimation with PWM-Encoded Markers

Real-Time Event-Based LED Tracking and Pose Estimation with PWM-Encoded Markers

Junkai Niu、Sheng Zhong、Delei Kong、Xin Wang、Kaizhen Sun 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Event cameras are neuromorphic vision sensors with pixels that independently and asynchronously respond to brightness changes at microsecond resolution, supporting state estimation under rapid motion, motion blur, and high dynamic range (HDR) illumination. This also enables capture of high-frequency blinking patterns from light-emitting diodes (LEDs). Based on this, we propose a real-time event-based framework for LED tracking and six-degree-of-freedom (6-DoF) relative pose estimation using pulse-width modulation (PWM)-encoded LED markers. Multiple LEDs share a common blinking frequency and encode identities through distinct PWM duty cycles, reducing frequency-resource requirements and computational cost. Our dual-rate asynchronous tracking framework combines low-rate LED detection with high-rate raw-event updates, maintaining robust LED identity association and accurate pixel-level tracking under rapid motion. LED identities and positions establish reliable 2D-3D correspondences for real-time metric 6-DoF relative pose estimation. Extensive evaluations on a self-collected dataset demonstrate robustness under large motion flow and transient image-plane LED overlap. Furthermore, we develop a coordinated robotic platform comprising a quadrotor and a Mecanum-wheeled mobile robot, demonstrating the proposed framework's potential for closed-loop drone guidance through hovering and target-following experiments under time-varying HDR illumination, with onboard deployment on an NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX. The dataset and implementation will be released to facilitate further research.

Unified Overview of Safety Certificate Functions: Techniques from Hamilton--Jacobi Reachability and Control Barrier Functions

Unified Overview of Safety Certificate Functions: Techniques from Hamilton--Jacobi Reachability and Control Barrier Functions

Hao Wang、Max Cohen、Zeyuan Feng、Donggeon David Oh、Oswin So 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Safety is a central concern in modern autonomous systems. Safety certificates---functions whose existence guarantees that a system meets its safety specification---have long been used as a principled tool for rigorously reasoning about safety. Methods for synthesizing such certificates are scattered across research areas, with little guidance on when to use a particular approach. This tutorial provides a holistic treatment of safety certificate synthesis centered on two widely used families: Hamilton--Jacobi (HJ) reachability analysis and control barrier functions (CBFs). For each family of methods, we first introduce its general formulation, along with classical and learning-based tools for certificate synthesis. Next, we introduce two benchmark problems consisting of vehicle systems of differing complexity to assess the certificate quality of different synthesis methods and their effectiveness in safety-critical control. We use these benchmarks to compare various methods and provide practical guidance for selecting an approach.

Semantics-to-Contact: A Stagewise Framework for Robust Contact-Rich Manipulation

Semantics-to-Contact: A Stagewise Framework for Robust Contact-Rich Manipulation

Guanren Qiao、Ruixiang Ouyang、Sheng Xu、Yueci Deng、Yunxin Tai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Contact-rich manipulation requires precise control under complex contact dynamics while remaining robust to diverse visual conditions. However, real-world reinforcement learning often overfits to the visual appearance of training scenes. We propose Semantics-to-Contact (S2C), a stagewise framework for visually robust contact-rich manipulation. The insight is to decompose the problem into two stages: semantic focusing and contact refinement. In the first stage, a vision-language model localizes task-relevant regions across visually diverse scenes, providing coarse spatial guidance and reducing the burden of exploration. In the second stage, a real-world human-in-the-loop residual reinforcement learning policy learns fine-grained contact behaviors within the localized region using force feedback. To further improve visual robustness, we introduce an object-centric visual augmentation strategy that randomizes background appearance while preserving the robot and task-relevant objects. Experiments on six contact-rich real-world manipulation tasks exhibit that S2C consistently improves success rates over strong baselines and maintains reliable performance under significant visual and positional variations. These results demonstrate that stagewise semantic focusing and contact refinement provide a practical path toward visually robust real-world contact-rich manipulation. Our demos can be seen in https://edem-ai.github.io/S2C/.

LMGN: A Unified Policy Learning Model for Last-Mile Grasp-Navigation

LMGN: A Unified Policy Learning Model for Last-Mile Grasp-Navigation

Xuenmin Hu、Qingxue Qian、Wei Luo、Benyi Ma、Bo Tang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Legged robots equipped with manipulators have shown significant potential in the fields of daily life and industry in complex environments. However, existing methods mainly focus on global navigation or manipulation independently, while overlooking the last-mile navigation problem, where the robot must jointly determine the base placement, approach direction, and grasp execution to achieve reliable object grasping, and different manipulation stages require distinct control behaviors. To address these challenges, we propose Last-Mile Grasp-Navigation (LMGN), a unified policy learning model for Quadruped robot loco-manipulation. An Explicit Distance and Directional Reachability (ED-DR) representation is first introduced to explicitly represent the features of grasp-compatible base placement and approach direction. We then develop a semantically aligned Hierarchical Mixture-of-Experts (HMoE) policy that models stage-aware behaviors across the stages of base adjustment, pre-grasp alignment and grasp execution, and object lift. Finally, a new optimization method that jointly aligns navigation and manipulation, stage-dependent expert routing, and action-level consistency is proposed to optimize the HMoE policy. Extensive experiments on the Habitat and Isaac Gym simulators demonstrate that LMGN consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods, denoting that integrating grasp-aware reachability with stage-aware policy learning significantly improves the success rates of last-mile navigation and target grasp for robots.

H-TRIP: Exact Many-to-Many Distance Index for Multi-Robot Exploration

H-TRIP: Exact Many-to-Many Distance Index for Multi-Robot Exploration

Elif A. Özaslan、Tolga Özaslan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Frontier-based exploration systems repeatedly evaluate geodesic travel costs between robot poses and candidate viewpoints, and tour-based planners consume the full pairwise distance matrix built from them. Recomputing these matrices with one graph search per source scales with the explored map size, whereas distance caches that refresh only locally affected or newly created frontiers can retain stale costs between surviving pairs when newly discovered free space creates a global shortcut. We present H-TRIP (Hierarchical Tropical Rank-one Incremental Propagation), an exact distance index grounded in Kleene algebra over the min-plus semiring for monotonically growing occupancy grids. H-TRIP partitions the known map into a spatial hierarchy with fixed boundary ports. Each unknown-to-free revelation is decomposed into efficient rank-one min-plus edge insertions at the affected leaf, and only decreased port-to-port summaries are propagated along its ancestor chain. A shared hierarchical lift evaluates the dense many-to-many matrix of shortest distances for all active query points on the occupancy map. We prove that sequential insertions preserve exact shortest-path distances on the grid. Benchmark experiments confirm exact agreement with reference searches on fully built maps and show that dense many-to-many queries at a few dozen viewpoints are up to two orders of magnitude faster than sequential k x BFS. On exploration runs with sixteen robots, in-loop queries on the growing occupancy graph are 2.21x-8.59x faster than co-scheduled k x BFS on maps with side 256, 512, and 1024 cells.

H-BRICK: Hierarchical Boundary Reachability Index with Compressed Kleene Closure for Directed Grid Graphs

H-BRICK: Hierarchical Boundary Reachability Index with Compressed Kleene Closure for Directed Grid Graphs

Tolga Özaslan、Elif A. Özaslan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Repeated directed reachability queries arise in online path planning, task-agent allocation, and swarm control on static grid maps, where reachability is asymmetric and full transitive closure is storage-prohibitive. We introduce H-BRICK, a Hierarchical Boundary Reachability Index with Compressed Kleene closure for exact cell-to-cell queries on directed grid graphs. H-BRICK partitions the graph into tiles, computes a local Boolean Kleene closure within each tile, and projects it onto the tile's boundary ports. The resulting directed port-to-port summaries are then composed recursively across shared interfaces up to a single root covering the graph. We prove algebraically that the construction is exact: in the Boolean Kleene algebra of binary relations, every composed summary equals the boundary projection of the regional reflexive transitive closure, and every cell-to-cell query agrees with reachability in the original graph. Experiments on directed grid graphs across diverse map topologies compare H-BRICK against directed search and component-based baselines, and compare its storage requirements against full transitive closure. A batched reachability evaluation further demonstrates that sharing endpoint lifts across many-source/many-target queries amortizes hierarchical traversal and, at k=64, yields a 5.0x-22.9x throughput improvement over the scalar query loop. The results show that hierarchical boundary compression is most advantageous in query-intensive spatial graphs where online search traverses large reachable regions and the hierarchy maintains compact boundary interfaces.

HITCH: Teaching a Vision-Language Model to Operate a Humanoid through a Whole-Body Harness

HITCH: Teaching a Vision-Language Model to Operate a Humanoid through a Whole-Body Harness

Qingzhou Lu、Chenxin Liu、Xinming Wang、Liu Cao、Yanjiang Guo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Enabling vision-language models (VLMs) to control humanoid robots requires both reliable whole-body interfaces and knowledge of how to use the body that carries over across tasks. We present HITCH (Harness Interfaces To Command Humanoids), a harness in which perception returns the 3D position of any point the VLM selects in its image, and execution turns commands for the pelvis, torso, hands, walking, and gaze into whole-body motion with a learned tracker. We find that both measuring the scene and executing commands accurately raise task success. We then fine-tune a VLM on trajectories in which teacher policies solve five base tasks through HITCH, so that it learns how to operate the humanoid. Without task-specific trajectories, the fine-tuned model completes 51% of SIMPLE episodes and 42.5% of HumanoidArena episodes, and adding five trajectories per SIMPLE task raises its SIMPLE success to 95%. On a real G1, it carries a box and walks to a goal without real training data.

Learning the Task or Learning the Verifier? Rethinking Criteria Exposure in Embodied Self-Improvement

Learning the Task or Learning the Verifier? Rethinking Criteria Exposure in Embodied Self-Improvement

Wenbo Huang、Jianxin Bi、Shunyu Yang、Ruiyang Si、Rui Ni 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Self-improving embodied agents are increasingly being developed and compared on robot simulation benchmarks. Several recent works expose the benchmark's success criteria to the learning agent, a practice we call Criteria Exposure (CE). These criteria, originally written to verify policies trained by imitation learning, often demand more than the corresponding language instructions, so a score from such a verifier may not faithfully reflect whether the instructed task was completed, especially for robot-control programs written by an agent. As embodied coding agents report ever higher benchmark scores, a natural question arises: when a coding agent is exposed to the benchmark's criteria, does it generate code that better performs the instructed task, or code that simply overfits to the verification criteria? We conduct controlled experiments with a self-improving agent with and without CE, and find that with CE the agent quickly produces code with a higher success rate under the given criteria, but this advantage disappears when the criteria are changed slightly without departing from the original instructions. More importantly, the skills the agent develops under CE transfer poorly across tasks. We further propose instruction-faithful criteria and show that they better reflect the performance of agent-generated programs.

Several recent works expose the benchmark's success criteria to the learning agent, a practice we call Criteria Exposure (CE). These criteria, originally written to verify policies trained by imitation learning, often demand more than the corresponding language instructions, so a score from such a verifier may not faithfully reflect whether the instructed task was completed, especially for robot-control programs written by an agent. As embodied coding agents report ever higher benchmark scores, a natural question arises: when a coding agent is exposed to the benchmark's criteria, does it generate code that better performs the instructed task, or code that simply overfits to the verification criteria? We conduct controlled experiments with a self-improving agent with and without CE, and find that with CE the agent quickly produces code with a higher success rate under the given criteria, but this advantage disappears when the criteria are changed slightly without departing from the original instructions. More importantly, the skills the agent develops under CE transfer poorly across tasks. We further propose instruction-faithful criteria and show that they better reflect the performance of agent-generated programs.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像遥感
2026-11-05

A Sequential Framework for Urban Drone Delivery: Network Design, Vehicle Sizing, and Energy and Cost Assessment

A Sequential Framework for Urban Drone Delivery: Network Design, Vehicle Sizing, and Energy and Cost Assessment

Hong-Su Nam、Seokhwan Lee、Jungwoo Cho、Hak-Tae Lee

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A sequential engineering framework is presented for urban drone delivery, linking hub location, route planning, mission-specific vehicle sizing, and energy and operating cost assessment. Aggregate origin-destination demand is assumed known a priori. For each hub configuration, the maximum required link distance is minimized, and feasible routes that return to their departure hubs are generated. Route frequencies are determined using mixed-integer linear programming to satisfy delivery demand. The resulting range requirement guides motor-propeller selection and battery sizing to minimize vehicle takeoff mass. Flight distances and frequencies, cruise performance, and vehicle dimensions are combined to estimate monthly energy consumption, fleet size, hub area, staffing, and operating cost. The framework is applied to district-level Seoul delivery data. Increasing the number of hubs from one to four reduces the required range from 30.6 to 21.9 km and vehicle takeoff mass from 8.12 to 6.12 kg. Despite a 21.5% increase in total flight distance, monthly flight energy consumption decreases by 13.1%, from 15.2 to 13.2 MWh. Battery replacement is the largest vehicle-related operating expense, while operating cost per delivery increases from USD 1.18 to USD 2.20 because of higher rent and labor costs at the hubs. Among the designs evaluated, range-matched vehicles achieve the lowest operating cost in the two- and four-hub networks; a longer-range vehicle is least costly in the three-hub network. Sensitivity analyses further examine the effects of delivery demand and operating assumptions. The results demonstrate that reductions in vehicle mass and energy consumption do not necessarily lead to lower system operating cost.

2026-11-05

Flexible Piezoelectric Tactile Sensing System for Intelligent Object Recognition

Flexible Piezoelectric Tactile Sensing System for Intelligent Object Recognition

Razan Khalifeh、Mohamad Yaacoub、Christian Gianoglio、Moustafa Saleh、Maurizio Valle 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Tactile sensing provides information about object properties that can improve robotic grasping and manipulation. However, accurate object recognition during the early stages of grasping remains challenging, particularly under strict real-time constraints. This study presents a flexible, distributed piezoelectric tactile sensing system for early-stage object recognition. The system was experimentally evaluated using six everyday objects and three recognition approaches: feature-based classification using support vector machines (SVMs), raw-signal classification using 1D convolutional neural networks (1D-CNNs) and convolutional recurrent neural networks (C-RNNs), and spatiotemporal heatmap classification using a 2D-CNN. The effects of sensor count and temporal window size on recognition accuracy and real-time feasibility were systematically investigated. Increasing the number of sensing elements improved recognition performance across all approaches. The best-performing method, based on spatiotemporal heatmaps and a 2D-CNN, achieved F1-scores of 0.9796, 0.9846, and 0.9903 using 16, 24, and 32 sensors, respectively. The optimal configuration, comprising 32 sensors and a 100-ms temporal window (50 samples), was deployed on an embedded edge platform, achieving an end-to-end recognition latency of approximately 101 ms from contact onset and an energy consumption of 63.8 microjoules per inference. These results demonstrate the potential of distributed piezoelectric tactile sensing for accurate, energy-efficient, and real-time object recognition during robotic grasping.

2026-11-05

TactileLab: Sim-to-Real Dexterous Robot Manipulation with Efficient Shear-Sensitive Tactile Simulation

TactileLab: Sim-to-Real Dexterous Robot Manipulation with Efficient Shear-Sensitive Tactile Simulation

Yijiong Lin、Wenhao He、Toby Jack、Max Yang、Haoran Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robotic simulators that represent shear within tactile sensing are becoming increasingly important for contact-rich manipulation. However, simulating contact shear with high-fidelity deformable or soft-body simulation is computationally demanding, limiting scalability to large-scale reinforcement learning. Here we introduce \textit{TactileLab}, a scalable multimodal tactile simulation framework built on IsaacLab, with a particular focus on a new optical-flow-based tactile representation termed \textit{simulated tactile flow}. Instead of relying on soft-body physics to model shear deformation, TactileLab uses rigid-body simulation to efficiently estimate dense tangential motion at the contact interface, producing tactile flow fields that encode shear-relevant contact dynamics. This flow field complements geometric features from a contact depth field. We further introduce a novel policy architecture for jointly processing long-horizon proprioceptive signals and rich tactile observations. Across multiple contact-rich tasks, policies trained using simulated tactile flow learn efficiently at scale and exhibit emergent contact-aware behaviours. To facilitate sim-to-real policy deployment, we then propose a novel real-to-sim tactile translation method that maps real tactile image streams to simulated tactile flow and depth fields, together with a new tactile data collection method, and validate it across diverse tactile sensor designs. Using this pipeline, we demonstrate real-world deployment on tasks encompassing dual-arm and dexterous in-hand manipulation. Overall, TactileLab provides an end-to-end sim-to-real framework for scalable tactile policy learning, enabling diverse shear-sensitive manipulation across heterogeneous tactile robotic systems.

Dual-Arm Tabletop Rearrangement with Movable Stacking and Gap-Aware Push Transfers

Dual-Arm Tabletop Rearrangement with Movable Stacking and Gap-Aware Push Transfers

Mohammadreza Firoozalizadeh、Zakiye Rostamirad、Arman Barghi、Ahmad Kalhor、Mohammadreza Nayeri 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Object rearrangement in cluttered tabletop scenes is challenging, especially in wide workspaces where dual-arm systems improve coverage and efficiency. Limited free space often necessitates buffering, while objects near or across the shared reachability region of the manipulators must be transferred under careful coordination to avoid inter-arm collisions. Conventional inter-arm transfer typically relies on a fixed handoff configuration, which constrains where and how objects are exchanged, enforces spatiotemporal synchronization of the arms, and reduces flexibility in dense layouts. This paper presents a dual-arm rearrangement framework that combines movable stacking with Gap-Aware Push Transfer for asynchronous cross-boundary exchange. The method scans the shared boundary to identify collision-free transfer gaps, places an object near a selected crossing point, and then pushes it into the receiving workspace without requiring simultaneous interaction between the two arms. A joint Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS)-based planner coordinates both manipulators while reasoning over buffering and transfer decisions, and execution cost is used after planning to assess the resulting plan. Experiments show that the proposed method shortens joint execution sequences and becomes increasingly advantageous over a fixed handoff baseline as scene density increases, achieving lower execution cost in denser scenes while remaining competitive in lighter ones.

2026-11-05

AffectiveVLA: Safe and Intent-Aware VLA Control for Human-Humanoid Social Interaction

AffectiveVLA: Safe and Intent-Aware VLA Control for Human-Humanoid Social Interaction

Nikita Kuzmin、Georgii Demianchuk、Mariya Lezina、Yara Mahmoud、Iana Zhura 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Social touch interactions, such as handshakes, fist bumps, and hugs, require humanoid robots to infer human intent, generate contact-rich motions, and maintain safe physical contact. We present AffectiveVLA, an intent-gated Vision-Language-Action (VLA) pipeline deployed on a Unitree G1 humanoid with dexterous hands. A lightweight RGB-based model recognizes interaction intent from two onboard cameras and converts it into a language prompt for the VLA policy. Low-confidence predictions suppress motion execution, reducing unintended activations. During physical contact, interaction-specific compliance profiles adjust arm impedance, while an independent voice-triggered mechanism initiates a latched safe-stop procedure. Using VR teleoperation, we collected approximately 1,300 multimodal interaction episodes and fine-tuned four VLA models: GR00T, RLDX-1, Being-H0.5, and UnifoLM-VLA-0. On-robot evaluation identifies GR00T as the best-performing standalone model. Integrating intent recognition increases its macro-F1 score from 0.75 to 0.90, while the intent model achieves a p95 inference latency of 6.27 ms. These results demonstrate that explicit intent gating improves the reliability of VLA-based social interaction without compromising real-time operation.

Certified folding-free deformable image registration: A whole-cell Jacobian certificate and a sequential variational solver

Certified folding-free deformable image registration: A whole-cell Jacobian certificate and a sequential variational solver

Baihan Zhao、Zicong Zhou、Guojun Liao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Registration maps propagate labels, measure local volume change, and map results back through their inverses; requiring invertibility. Voxelwise checks can miss folds between grid points. We study the canonical continuous extension of a nodal displacement field by trilinear interpolation and give a test that proves its Jacobian determinant positive in every grid cell or rejects the map. The test is rigorous under IEEE 754 binary64 arithmetic (correct rounding, no contraction). With a fixed boundary, certification implies a bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism of the image domain. We introduce SeqVP, built around this certificate. A proximal Jacobian-determinant-curl formulation yields inner problems whose penalties on changes in the map, Jacobian determinant, and curl vanish to first order at the current iterate. The method decreases a fixed outer energy up to a floating-point resolution guard and certifies every committed iterate. After certified initialization, stopping at any time returns a folding-free map. A Poisson-preconditioned gradient method with Jacobian-aware step prediction and affine-scaling damping, initialized by certified handoff from an uncertified binary32 pre-optimizer, handles a 1 mm brain MRI with about 2e7 unknowns in 90 s on one GPU. On 49 held-out OASIS pairs, SeqVP achieves mean Dice 0.821 over 35 structures and 0.889 over 4 tissue classes, with certified zero folding. Gains are 0.022 and 0.025 over ANTs SyN, and 0.022 and 0.023 over uniGradICON with instance optimization at equal time (paired, Holm-adjusted p < 0.001), but maps are rougher. SeqVP is level with ANTs on LPBA40, behind it on IBSR18, and ahead of all label-free baselines on 20 large-deformation pairs. On fields from nine methods, the certificate finds non-positive-Jacobian cells in every ANTs SyN map (median 10, missed by voxelwise checks) and in maps from NiftyReg, ConvexAdam, uniGradICON, and TransMorph.

A Multimodal Haptic Interface for Simultaneous Softness and Thermal Modulation

A Multimodal Haptic Interface for Simultaneous Softness and Thermal Modulation

Joshua Brown、Fernando Bello、Lynette Jones

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Haptic interactions, such as touching an object in virtual reality, involve detecting a plethora of sensations through the skin. Whilst tactile displays exist to simulate and manipulate all of these cues individually, little progress has been made constructing devices that can control multiple cues simultaneously. This paper presents a tactile display technology that offers both softness and temperature change. A prototype display, based on particle jamming and the thermal transfer of heated and cooled water, is evaluated in terms of its ability to change both stiffness and temperature. Mechanical evaluations demonstrate controllable stiffness increase of 300%, whilst thermal characterisation showed strong surface uniformity and a range of achievable temperatures from 10°C up to 50°C, with closed-loop control maintaining a 0.2°C error from a setpoint. This work advances the design of multimodal tactile displays with applications to perceptual research, medical simulation, and affective haptics.

2026-11-05

Radio-to-Pose Information for Gauge-Drift Correction in Cooperative Localization

Radio-to-Pose Information for Gauge-Drift Correction in Cooperative Localization

Aditya Iyer、Andy Ye、Akshay Narayan、Nora Ayanian

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Cooperative localization uses odometry and inter-robot measurements to estimate the motion of a robot team. These measurements describe only relative geometry, but the team's estimated position and orientation can drift together over time, i.e., gauge drift. In this work, we study correction of this gauge drift using a radio attenuation map registered in the same world coordinate frame used for robot poses. Specifically, we propose Radio-to-Pose Information (RPI), an interface that converts power residuals and their pose derivatives into a Gaussian information matrix and vector pair. RPI builds on the insight that received power is sensitive to shared displacement in the world map. The pose estimator uses the interface together with odometry and inter-robot measurements to update poses and minimize uncertainty. We characterize the shared motions detectable from the map and observed links. For an uncertain shared map, RPI keeps correlations across robots and time and reproduces joint Gaussian pose estimates and covariances. Across $1000$ simulated Gaussian trials, RPI matches the results of joint estimation. Across 32 paired trials with a registered map, RPI reduces common-position RMSE from $0.542$m to $0.140$m in a $50\times50$m arena compared with a baseline that uses radio only to weight relative measurements.

2026-11-05

Transformer-Based Trajectory Prescription for sEMG-Optimizing Robot-Mediated Rehabilitation

Transformer-Based Trajectory Prescription for sEMG-Optimizing Robot-Mediated Rehabilitation

Gabriel Parra、Kimia Khoshnami、Daniel S. Brown、Laura A. Hallock

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot-mediated rehabilitation has the potential to deliver high-intensity, individualized therapy, but existing systems (which have not consistently improved outcomes) typically prescribe kinematic trajectories rather than explicitly targeting the neuromotor activations thought to support recovery. To address this gap, and ultimately enable activation-optimizing rehabilitation robot behavior, we present a personalizable transformer-based model mapping individuals' muscle activations (as measured via surface electromyography) to prescribed contact force trajectories when interacting with an end-effector rehabilitation robot. The model uses a transformer encoder-decoder leveraging rotary positional embeddings (RoPE) to generate a latent representation of trajectory-relevant and user-specific neuromotor factors, and augments trajectory prediction with flow matching to produce a distribution over plausible prescriptions biased toward the training distribution. Using data from 16 healthy participants completing 15 isometric trajectory tracking tasks, we evaluate generalization across held-out tasks and users, as well as the feasibility of user-specific fine-tuning within a therapy-session timescale. The model achieves mean trajectory prediction errors of <8% on held out tasks and users, with effectively instantaneous deterministic inference and sub-second sampling of trajectory distributions. An initial test using the model during live rehabilitation tasks demonstrates that, on average, participants reach the target neuromotor activations with just 15% error accumulated throughout the task. These results provide a proof of concept for data-driven, activation-targeted trajectory prescription in robot-mediated rehabilitation and establish a foundation for future validation in clinical populations. Data and code have been open-sourced as part of the OpenRobotRehab project on SimTK.

Detecting Silent Faults at the Environment-Trainer Boundary in Reinforcement Learning

Detecting Silent Faults at the Environment-Trainer Boundary in Reinforcement Learning

Zuo Gou、Chenhao Lu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot reinforcement learning increasingly runs environments outside the trainer: in another process, on another machine, in another language. Many faults at this boundary raise no error. We make the boundary a wire protocol and ask what notices a fault in it. On Pendulum, HalfCheetah, Hopper and a 7-joint arm (Pusher), we injected up to 23 faults into what crosses the wire and 2 into the trainer's log, from one value to every value. Unless every value was wrong, a learning-curve threshold caught at most one run in ten, and a comparison with ten fault-free seeds at most 21%. A clean evaluation of all 1,060 final policies agreed with the curves: of the 728 boundary-fault runs both curve checks passed, 723 scored no more than three standard deviations below fault-free seeds, though nearly all ended on other weights and, for reward faults, logged other returns. Checks of the run itself, not of its outcome, saw every fault. A step ledger kept on both sides and compared step by step caught and located every fault that crossed, on Pendulum, Hopper and Pusher on one machine and on Pendulum across two, with no fault-free run and, on Pendulum, a median 3% more training time. An episode record kept at the environment caught the faults in the trainer's log. We list such faults found in practice, in Gymnasium 1.3 and our own code and, reported by others, in five multi-agent libraries, and recommend a step ledger and episode counts kept at the environment.

Educational robotics beyond ROS: a custom library for direct transfer of Webots controllers in the Turtlebot3

Educational robotics beyond ROS: a custom library for direct transfer of Webots controllers in the Turtlebot3

Jaime P. Pérez-Moro、Alejandro Romero、Hector Quintian、Francisco Bellas

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot Operating System (ROS) is becoming a standard in robotics courses at higher education, but its installation, configuration and distributed programming model can introduce a significant entry barrier in the new landscape of AI-based master and degree studies, where many students do not have a strong informatics background. This paper presents an educational experience that enables students to program a TurtleBot3 Burger directly in Python, while preserving a direct transfer path from simulation to the physical robot. The approach is based on a custom library that mirrors the Webots API, allowing the same controller code to be executed in the simulator and on the real TurtleBot3 with minimal modification. The library provides access to the robot motors, wheel encoders, inertial sensors and LiDAR, and is complemented by scripts that automate deployment and execution on the physical platform. It was used in a master-level course on introductory autonomous vehicles, and initial student feedback suggests that the approach reduces the perceived complexity of ROS-related infrastructure and helps learners focus on algorithmic logic and real-world behaviour.

An Integrated Open-Source Stack for Vision-Based Autonomous Navigation of Wheeled Rovers

An Integrated Open-Source Stack for Vision-Based Autonomous Navigation of Wheeled Rovers

Iaroslav Kolomiets、Iaroslav Sheipak、Mikhail Reppo、Vsevolod Egorov、Viktor Chertopolokhov 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-based rover autonomy requires integrating perception, localization, planning and platform-specific control under limited sensing and computing resources. We present an open-source ROS 2 stack for following directional arrows, reaching a terminal cone and returning autonomously to the start. It connects YOLOv8n detection and geometric direction, range and bearing estimation with RGB-D SLAM, wheel-inertial state estimation, kinematics-aware planning, behavior-tree mission management and STM32 actuation. Standard interfaces separate reusable autonomy from the hardware layer; a proportional path-following plugin supports low-speed rover motion. Public resources include software, configuration, a labeled dataset, trained weights and evaluation logs. On 478 held-out images, the detector achieves mAP@0.5 of 0.720 under the Ultralytics evaluator and outperforms a classical OpenCV baseline under a shared evaluation protocol. The reported perception latency is 25 ms on a Jetson Orin NX. Three missions, one indoor and two outdoor, demonstrate marker-guided navigation and autonomous return without operator motion commands or reported collisions. Field results document integration failures and corrective choices; repeated instrumented trials remain necessary to quantify physical navigation errors and reliability.

2026-11-05

Fast and Reliable Open-Vocabulary Object Search with Absence Detection from Robot Memory

Fast and Reliable Open-Vocabulary Object Search with Absence Detection from Robot Memory

Nicholas Paul、Devson Butani、Eric Martinson

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Mobile manipulators deployed in everyday human environments, such as homes for assistive care or flexible automation, often need to locate specific objects on demand from accumulated observations. Equally important, they must reliably determine when a target is absent before committing to navigation or interaction. Existing approaches demand either slow exhaustive re-processing or expensive 3D representations, and are evaluated on recall and accuracy rather than specificity, leaving their ability to abstain on missing targets unmeasured. We find existing systems that search using text or visual embedding almost never abstain on absent objects achieving at most 9\% specificity. To support accurate search at speed, we present a two-stage architecture that decouples fast candidate retrieval from verified selection, enabling both high recall and reliable absence detection from sequential RGB observations with pose. We evaluate eight retrieval-selection configurations plus an exhaustive search baseline across ScanNet apartment scenes, including significant numbers of absent objects. Adding a visual verification step to caption-based retrieval matches or exceeds exhaustive search reliability while requiring an order of magnitude fewer detector calls. We reproduce ScanNet scores on a physical robot operating entirely on-board with a single consumer GPU running in real time.

Wedge-Jamming Mechanism for String Configuration

Wedge-Jamming Mechanism for String Configuration

Taro Tako、Takanori Jin、Hiroshi Kaminaga、Taisuke Kobayashi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Variable stiffness mechanisms that achieve both flexibility and rigidity are important technologies for robotic systems. Recently, wire-based jamming mechanisms have attracted significant attention. In the wire-based jamming mechanisms, modules are chained by a wire, and the overall stiffness is controlled by changing the friction between the modules through wire tension. These mechanisms have been widely studied due to their simple structure and controllability. However, conventional wire-based jamming mechanisms mainly focus on holding torque along the joint axis, and the resistance against disturbances in other directions has not been sufficiently explored. In this study, we propose a wedge-jamming mechanism that introduces wedge-shaped contact surfaces into the modules of wire-based jamming structures. By changing the wedge angle, the holding characteristics against disturbances in different directions can be adjusted. Experimental results show that an out-wedge configuration improves resistance to vertical disturbances, while an in-wedge configuration enhances resistance to torsional disturbances. These results demonstrate that the anisotropic holding performance of wire-based jamming mechanisms depend on wedge geometry.

Energy-Aware Open-Vocabulary Detection Scheduling for Low-Power Robotic Autonomy

Energy-Aware Open-Vocabulary Detection Scheduling for Low-Power Robotic Autonomy

Abdul Basit、Muhammad Shafique

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Open-vocabulary object detection (OVD) lets robots search for run-time object queries, but fixed-rate inference spends embedded compute on low-value frames during idle motion, map revisits, or stable viewpoints. This paper presents an energy-aware OVD scheduler for low-power mobile robots. The scheduler uses odometry, map change, and detector confidence to switch among low-, mid-, and high-power YOLO-World-S modes on a Jetson Orin Nano. We evaluate the energy-quality trade-off on an AgileX LIMO using 40 real navigation trials: two target objects, five placements per target-policy pair, and four perception policies. Across bottle and cup trials, the scheduler records 8/10 mission success compared with 7/10 for each fixed-rate baseline, while reducing mean Jetson input power (a Jetson-side power-draw metric) by 5.2% relative to an 8 Hz high-rate baseline and lowering achieved OVD call rate by 34.0%. It does not minimize mission-window Jetson input energy because longer missions can erase instantaneous power savings. A separate 60 s stationary microbenchmark isolates the perception workload: the scheduler lowers mean power from 8.045 W to 7.301 W and energy from 484.7 J to 438.1 J relative to the 8 Hz baseline. These results support robot-state-aware OVD scheduling as a promising power-management mechanism with competitive task reliability, while showing that mission-window Jetson input energy must be read together with task progress and runtime.

Data-Driven Configuration of Protective Separation Zones in Collaborative Robot Cells from Operator Occupancy

Data-Driven Configuration of Protective Separation Zones in Collaborative Robot Cells from Operator Occupancy

Aulon Bajrami、Theo Jacobs、Werner Kraus

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The protective separation zone of a collaborative robot cell is commonly dimensioned for a worst case that disregards where the operator is actually present during the task. This paper presents a camera-based, data-driven method that configures this zone from observed operator occupancy. Operator positions accumulated over several production shifts are aggregated into an occupancy heatmap, from which a protective field is derived that trades off the achievable robot velocity against the required safety distance, following the speed and separation monitoring principle of ISO/TS 15066. The resulting configuration is advisory, that is, reviewed within the risk assessment rather than applied autonomously to the robot controller. The method, which builds on a pose-based perception pipeline with activity recognition, was evaluated in two operational cells at two industrial partners, where the protective field could be reduced by approximately 17% and a productivity increase of 9% was measured. However, adoption proved to depend on more than the demonstrated gain alone.

DC-SQEM: Medial Mesh Extraction by Dual Contouring with Spherical Quadric Error Metric

DC-SQEM: Medial Mesh Extraction by Dual Contouring with Spherical Quadric Error Metric

Bingchuan Li、Ruilong Liu、Tianhao Hu、Congyi Zhang、Ningna Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The medial axis, as a compact representation, describes not only the boundary but also the interior of the shape, and has been widely used in analyzing and simplifying shapes. However, approximating feature-preserving medial axis from unstructured data, such as oriented point clouds, remains challenging due to the lack of explicit connectivity and the presence of noise in the source. We propose DC-SQEM, a dual-contouring-based framework that computes medial meshes (an explicit representation of the medial axis) directly from oriented point clouds without relying on watertight manifold surfaces or volumetric structures. With the help of an adaptive octree, our method fits local medial spheres for each cell based on the Spherical Quadric Error Metric (SQEM). We address several long-standing robustness issues in dual-contouring and mesh extraction, including out-of-cell solutions, unstable feature classification, and redundant topological connections. To this end, we introduce virtual nodes, a feature-downgrading strategy, and a geometry-driven topology-aware cleanup procedure, enabling robust SQEM-based medial dual contouring with clean mesh outputs. Unlike prior point-cloud-based methods that produce either only 1D skeletal structures or incomplete medial features, our method preserves sharp geometric features from the input and reconstructs a complete medial structure, including sheets, curves, seams, and junctions. Compared to restricted-power-diagram-based approaches, our algorithm can handle point clouds as input, without having a strong requirement on water-tightness or manifoldness.
