2026-11-05

Leave It Better Than Found: Value-Guided Cooperation in Sequential Tasks

Leave It Better Than Found: Value-Guided Cooperation in Sequential Tasks

Mario Gomez Andreu、Michael Amir、Antonio Marino、Tianxu An、Marco Hutter 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We consider the problem of sequential task continuation, where one robot acts in a workspace that has been affected by a previous robot. Current approaches in multi-robot cooperation rely heavily on co-training or co-optimization, assumingthat the robots have access to collective interaction data during the policy training or planning process. However, as increasingly varied robotic embodiments and policies are deployed and operate in the same real-world environment, learning policies for cooperation becomes infeasible due to the combinatorial explosion of possible robot combinations that need to be accounted for. To address this challenge, this paper considers the problem of ad hoc sequential cooperation between robots that have never before operated together in a shared environment. We demonstrate that robots can explicitly communicate their environmental preferences through sharing their value functions, enabling coordination without the need for prior joint training. To ground this exchange and prevent overconfidence in out-of-distribution states, we further introduce a k-nearest neighbors (KNN)- based uncertainty filter. Our method yields an increase of up to 48.5 pp compared with naive or even selfish agents, in task success rates across simulated and real-world experiments, demonstrating that value function transmission between agents is useful not merely as an internal learning tool, but as an effective basis for ad hoc coordination. Website: https://proroklab.github.io/PAVE/

Terrain-Indexed Motion Priors and Geometric Support for Extreme Humanoid Parkour

Terrain-Indexed Motion Priors and Geometric Support for Extreme Humanoid Parkour

Chenghao Sun、Youhao Lyu、Zizheng Li、Shuting Zhang、Ziyang Dong 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present a progressive imitation framework that learns terrain-appropriate humanoid parkour skills and subsequently adapts their hand--knee support. Human demonstrations provide traversal behaviors, but their contact patterns need not suit a robot's proportions or the available support surfaces. Our framework changes the role of demonstrations across three stages: privileged imitation produces physically executable robot references; terrain-indexed adversarial motion priors guide skill acquisition; and geometric support guidance with a reduced motion-prior weight enables contact adaptation. Candidate-based precontact attraction and actual-contact evaluation guide support without prescribing a contact sequence. The deployed policy selects behaviors from onboard depth, proprioception, and velocity commands without an external skill label. On the Noetix E1, the policy achieves 96.34\% hybrid success in simulation and transfers without real-world policy fine-tuning, completing 29 of 30 individual obstacle trials and four of five hybrid courses. Support-adaptation ablations show higher traversal success for the complete adaptation stage on the tested terrains, while qualitative observations show changes in contact placement. These results support acquiring skills from demonstrations before relaxing their influence to accommodate robot-specific support configurations.

These results support acquiring skills from demonstrations before relaxing their influence to accommodate robot-specific support configurations.

A Unified Fractal Impedance Control Framework

A Unified Fractal Impedance Control Framework

Adnan Takriti、Mahmoud Sadeq、Quentin Rouxel、Daniele Cafolla、Michael Mistry 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Physical human--robot interaction requires controllers capable of maintaining intrinsically bounded interactions with the environment. The Fractal Impedance Controller (FIC) has been proven to guarantee these safety characteristics by design. This paper introduces the robotic implementation and experimental validation of its most advanced architecture: the Harmonic-Passive Motion Paradigm (H-PMP), an evolution of the Passive Motion Paradigm designed to mimic human-like interaction. Furthermore, we present a unified framework for FIC deployment, supported by an open-source software suite featuring ROS2, MuJoCo, and physical hardware integrations. Through three experiments conducted on a Kinova Gen3 arm (trajectory tracking, a wall press with sudden loss of contact, and learning-by-demonstration) we compare the H-PMP against earlier FIC architectures. The results demonstrate that the H-PMP achieves a seamless trade-off between admittance and impedance behaviours. This makes it ideal for deployment in unstructured environments, enabling the robot to transition between interaction modes with no computational overhead.

OopsieRecover: Learning Transferable VLA Corrections from Semantic Interventions

OopsieRecover: Learning Transferable VLA Corrections from Semantic Interventions

Arpit Bahety、Skand Peri、Sriniket Ambatipudi、Chen Xu、Haruki Nishimura 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models can perform a wide range of robotic manipulation tasks, but often fail during deployment. Teaching them to recover from these failures requires substantial corrective data: existing approaches learn to recover from failures by directly associating corrective actions with the observations in which they were demonstrated. For instance, DAgger-like fine-tuning can require tens to hundreds of interactions, yet the resulting policy may still fail to generalize the learned corrections beyond the demonstrated scenarios. We hypothesize that semantics can capture the underlying reasons for failures and their corrections, enabling policies to learn from only a handful of corrective demonstrations and generalize those corrections to novel objects, and scene variations. We propose OopsieRecover, a framework that uses abduction to infer the intent behind a small set of corrective demonstrations and encodes the inferred knowledge as dense spatial representations and conditional costs. These costs capture when a behavior is undesirable and steer the policy toward more desirable action regions. We experiment across 6 tasks and variations in object poses, scenes, and related tasks, and find that only a handful of corrective demonstrations can enable a VLA to overcome failures across a broad range of novel situations, without requiring new corrective data for each variation. Please refer to https://robin-lab.cs.utexas.edu/oopsierecover for videos.

2026-11-05

Latent RL Priors for Data-Efficient Dexterous Manipulation

Latent RL Priors for Data-Efficient Dexterous Manipulation

Yixiao Wang、Peiyi Chen、Mustafa Mukadam、Shreekant Gayaka、Masayoshi Tomizuka 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Learning dexterous manipulation from limited visual demonstrations is challenging because policies must acquire both perceptual grounding and precise control, while reinforcement learning (RL) in simulation can acquire rich control behaviors from large-scale interaction with privileged information. We introduce \textbf{Latent RL Prior}, a two-stage framework that reuses this control knowledge for data-efficient visual policy learning. During privileged pretraining, we distill large-scale RL rollouts into a flow-matching action decoder conditioned on proprioception and latent task context. During visual adaptation, we freeze the decoder and learn a visual interface that maps images to its latent context using action supervision alone, without privileged labels or latent targets. The same decoder can further be shared across arms by learning coordinated visual contexts for bimanual manipulation. Across three simulated tasks, our method outperforms full-parameter fine-tuning of $π_{0.5}$ and GR00T N1.7 in the low-data regime; on door lifting and bimanual assembly, it surpasses full-data baselines using $10\times$ and $5\times$ fewer demonstrations, respectively. Further analysis shows that direct visual-to-latent conditioning improves adaptation performance and training stability over routing visual predictions through the pretrained state encoder. Robustness to unseen mass variation and greater linear accessibility of contact-force information in decoder representations further provide evidence of retained RL-acquired control knowledge. Project website: https://yixiaowang7.github.io/latent-rl-prior-page/.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-11-05

Failure Prediction and Classification in Vision-Language-Action Models Via Calibrated Latent Probing

Failure Prediction and Classification in Vision-Language-Action Models Via Calibrated Latent Probing

Michal P. Podolinsky、Yunhao Yang、Daniel Little、Hong Lu、Rohan Siva 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable general-purpose embodied intelligence, but their performance is brittle under distribution shift, where unanticipated failures can damage equipment or endanger people. Deploying VLAs in real-world systems therefore requires a runtime monitor that is timely, calibrated, and informative while being efficient enough to run on the edge. Existing methods fail to fulfill these requirements. We present VLA-Tug, a lightweight probe over the latent layers of a VLA backbone that improves early failure detection and bounds the missed-detection and false-alarm rates, with minimal inference overhead. We show that supervising on the pre-failure segment of a trajectory increases early failure detection at matched false-positive rates by up to 30% over the strongest learned baseline. Under shift to unseen tasks, the bounds on missed-detection and false-alarm rates remain valid in up to 86% of runs, versus at most 21% for baselines. Unlike existing methods, VLA-Tug predicts the failure mode to enable corrective actions, raising top-3 accuracy on unseen tasks by up to 44% over a majority baseline. VLA-Tug enables real-time monitoring where the well-timed and informative probe supports lightweight steering that improves task success. We release a dataset of 3,180 annotated manipulation episodes to support future work on latent failure detection in VLAs at https://vla-tug.github.io.

Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable general-purpose embodied intelligence, but their performance is brittle under distribution shift, where unanticipated failures can damage equipment or endanger people. Deploying VLAs in real-world systems therefore requires a runtime monitor that is timely, calibrated, and informative while being efficient enough to run on the edge. Existing methods fail to fulfill these requirements. We present VLA-Tug, a lightweight probe over the latent layers of a VLA backbone that improves early failure detection and bounds the missed-detection and false-alarm rates, with minimal inference overhead. We show that supervising on the pre-failure segment of a trajectory increases early failure detection at matched false-positive rates by up to 30% over the strongest learned baseline. Under shift to unseen tasks, the bounds on missed-detection and false-alarm rates remain valid in up to 86% of runs, versus at most 21% for baselines. Unlike existing methods, VLA-Tug predicts the failure mode to enable corrective actions, raising top-3 accuracy on unseen tasks by up to 44% over a majority baseline. VLA-Tug enables real-time monitoring where the well-timed and informative probe supports lightweight steering that improves task success. We release a dataset of 3,180 annotated manipulation episodes to support future work on latent failure detection in VLAs at https://vla-tug.github.io.

目标检测/分割机器人视觉
2026-11-05

SHORE: Stability-Hybrid On-Policy Reinforcement Learning for Online Bin Packing

SHORE: Stability-Hybrid On-Policy Reinforcement Learning for Online Bin Packing

Chang Shi、Ann Majewicz Fey

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Online 3D bin packing with a processing buffer requires choosing which box to pack, its orientation, and its placement while keeping the stack stable under unknown future arrivals. A collapse ends the episode and forfeits the remaining volume. Existing methods use geometric rules or hard stability masks, limiting how risk can influence the joint choice of box, orientation, and placement. We propose Stability-Hybrid On-Policy Reinforcement Learning (SHORE), which combines a learned stability probability of a placement with an on-policy estimate of its return conditional on stability. A 3D U-Net learns stability from executed outcomes and simulated alternatives, revealing outcomes of actions underrepresented in early rollouts. A discrete on-policy actor--critic uses stability predictions in both its action values and policy prior, with gates that limit the effect of an untrained or changing model. In deployment, SHORE does not require additional search or simulation overhead. In Isaac Gym, SHORE reaches 73\% space utilization by 0.4M environment steps, while the same learner without stability modeling reaches that level at about 1M. On the expanded 60-box set, SHORE achieves 0.734 space utilization versus 0.588 for the GOPT state-of-the-art baseline, with a higher placement-failure frequency (1.03\% vs. 0.63\%), trading more risk for greater packed volume. The results show faster learning and higher utilization on the more demanding realistic box arrival set. Ablations suggest that simulated alternatives provide useful early supervision, while executed outcomes alone sustain later performance. We also demonstrate the method on a real robot to verify the simplicity of integrating the method to real bin packing pipelines.

RoboARCade: Benchmarking Agentic Robot Control

RoboARCade: Benchmarking Agentic Robot Control

Satvik Sharma、Hengyuan Hu、Mihika Kamat、Dorsa Sadigh、Jeannette Bohg 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Improvements in multi-modal capabilities have enabled agents to act directly as robot policies, a setting we refer to as agentic robot control (ARC). We introduce RoboARCade, a benchmark of long-horizon manipulation tasks across three embodiments designed to test in-context adaptation, memory, and reasoning for precise control. The tasks build on existing open-source benchmarks, which we make harder by widening the range of initial conditions. RoboARCade is easy to use and extend: all tasks run on a single simulation backend, MuJoCo, each task is a self-contained data pack, and agents connect through interchangeable interfaces, so new tasks, agents, and interfaces can be added independently. The benchmark separates leading agents: GPT-6 Astra achieves 49.8% compared with 43.6% for Claude Fable 5.1, leaving substantial room for improvement. Comparing two interfaces, we find that a richer visual interface adds only 1.5 to 6.8 points at 1.7 to 2.3 times the cost. Failures stem mostly from choosing a poor manipulation strategy or executing a good one imprecisely, rarely from misunderstanding the scene, and agents incorrectly claim success on 24% to 39% of attempted subtasks. Agents can also improve without human input: one successful demonstration in context raises Astra's score from 53.5% to 73.0% on a five-task subset, and a prompt the agent writes and revises itself reaches 72.0%. Code, videos and full traces are available at roboarcade.org.

Code, videos and full traces are available at roboarcade.org.

GPWM: Modeling the World as 3D Particle Motion

GPWM: Modeling the World as 3D Particle Motion

Robert Ren、Matthew Bronars、He Zhu、Yikai Tang、Jianing Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We introduce GPWM, a Generative Particle World Model that predicts how 3D scenes evolve by generating the motion of particles and rigid bodies over time. Unlike video world models that generate scene appearance, GPWM operates directly in 3D physical space. A single representation and architecture cover rigid, deformable, cloth, and granular materials, in both passive physics and robotic interaction. GPWM is an object-aware multi-resolution transformer that combines flow matching with diffusion forcing to generate coherent physical trajectories far beyond its training horizon. We train and evaluate GPWM on ParticleWorlds, a 25.5-million-frame corpus spanning diverse geometries, materials, interactions, and robot embodiments. GPWM outperforms the best prior learned-dynamics baseline in each of six physical domains, reducing prediction error by 31% on average. Breaking from the domain-specific setting of prior methods, a single Unified GPWM models these heterogeneous regimes with one set of weights, largely outperforming per-domain baselines and even surpassing our own specialist models in two domains. GPWM further generalizes to unseen geometries, physical properties, object counts, and rollout horizons. Beyond prediction, GPWM enables contact-rich planning by learning a generative prior over joint actuator-object motion. Instead of searching over unconstrained actions, planning searches the model's latent noise for interactions that achieve a desired object-space goal. With the same world model, noise-space planning substantially outperforms action-space search on pushing and picking, where complex contact sequences are hard to discover. Executed open-loop in simulation, the resulting plans achieve goal-directed manipulation without reference action trajectories or goal-specific policies.

Perception Over Geometry: Rethinking Gaussian Allocation in Mesh-based Splatting

Perception Over Geometry: Rethinking Gaussian Allocation in Mesh-based Splatting

Yuting Wang、Yang Zhao、Shanding Diao、Penglin Wang、Yuan Chen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables photorealistic novel-view synthesis but lacks explicit topology for geometric editing. Existing mesh-based methods enable geometric manipulation by associating Gaussians with triangular mesh faces, typically assigning a fixed number of Gaussians to each face. However, the representation capacity required for appearance reconstruction depends on local appearance complexity rather than merely on mesh tessellation density. To address these limitations, we propose P-MGS, a framework that separates appearance modeling from geometric control to achieve high quality rendering, compact representation, and flexible mesh editing. P-MGS introduces a perceptual complexity guided allocation strategy that distributes Gaussians according to surface appearance demand estimated from multiview observations. To preserve reconstruction flexibility, Gaussians are optimized without mesh constraints and subsequently associated with the original mesh through post hoc binding, retaining adaptive refinement while preserving mesh topology. During mesh editing, local affine deformation transfer updates Gaussian centers and covariances under mesh deformation, reducing misalignment and floating artifacts. Experiments demonstrate superior average novel view synthesis quality with fewer Gaussians and coherent mesh editing.

2026-11-05

GuidedWAM: Training-Free Energy Guidance for World Action Models in Autonomous Driving

GuidedWAM: Training-Free Energy Guidance for World Action Models in Autonomous Driving

Xiyan Su、Finn Rasmus Schäfer、Hojun Lim、Frank Diermeyer、Johannes Betz 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World action models (WAMs) for autonomous driving couple the driving policy and the visual world model in a single generative network whose objectives are fixed post-training. Conditioning on a new driving objective requires retraining. We study energy guidance as a general mechanism and present GuidedWAM, a model-agnostic framework for injecting driving objectives into frozen WAMs at sampling time. Our framework constructs each objective as differentiable energies on the sampler's intermediate clean-action estimate, which needs only forward access to the frozen model, and is stabilized by sampling-time guidance scheduling, per-energy normalization, and velocity-bounded correction. On the NAVSIM v2 hard-split, guidance improves all three frozen, publicly released WAMs by 1.8 to 3.6 EPDMS, averaged over three sampler seeds, and adds less than 10% to the sampling time per step. These results position energy guidance as a practical post-training control channel for WAMs.

Generate-then-Rank: Learning Symbolic Goal Interpretation for Embodied Agents

Generate-then-Rank: Learning Symbolic Goal Interpretation for Embodied Agents

Jisen Lei、Huizhi Liang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Goal interpretation is to translate a natural language instruction into a formal symbolic goal that a planner can execute. It is critical for embodied agents because any error here propagates to every downstream planning and action step. Doing it well requires both generating suitable candidates and reliably selecting among them. We present a two-stage generate-then-rank approach to Goal Interpretation in the Embodied Agent Interface (EAI). First, supervised fine-tuning adapts a Large Language Model (LLM) to EAI's output convention. Second, Candidate-Output Ranking from Errors (CORE) learns preferences over complete outputs from the model's own generated alternatives. We conducted experiments on the household scenes of the Embodied Agent Interface Benchmark. Multiple open source LLMs including DeepSeek-V3 chat, DeepSeek-Reasoner, and Qwen2.5-7B and Qwen2.5-72B were evaluated. The proposed method outperformed the baseline models including LLMs with simple prompting, chain-of-thought, and knowledge graph based approaches.

Kinematic analysis of a double-closed-loop four-bar chain-based leg for a multimodal quadruped-pentarotor robot

Kinematic analysis of a double-closed-loop four-bar chain-based leg for a multimodal quadruped-pentarotor robot

Prashant Raturi、Santhakumar Mohan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents the kinematic modeling and singularity analysis of a three-degree-of-freedom, double four-bar closed-loop quadruped leg mechanism. This leg is essential for transitioning between the two operational modes; therefore, it is examined in both joint and task spaces, leading to the development of its Jacobian matrix, which is vital for designing the gaits required for terrestrial mode and mid-air aerial reconfiguration (for aerial mode). Various gaits are devised for the system's motion in the MATLAB simulation, and their practical implementation on the single-leg test rig is demonstrated.

Patch-Based Generalized Neural Texture Upscaling

Patch-Based Generalized Neural Texture Upscaling

Shin Fujieda、Takahiro Harada

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We propose a lightweight neural texture upscaling method. A single generalized MLP is trained on a diverse collection of texture patches and integrated into the texture sampling stage, upscaling low-resolution textures on-the-fly at rendering time. Because the model is generalized rather than overfit to specific content, no per-asset training is required - the same model applies to any existing scene without modifying texture data or the asset pipeline. Storing lower-resolution base textures reduces disk footprint, upscaling at sampling time eliminates the need to store high-resolution textures in VRAM, and sharing a single set of model weights across all textures ensures non-divergent GPU execution. We evaluate our method across multiple texture classes, demonstrating consistent improvement over analytic upscaling methods in perceptual quality.

When Does Test-Time Search Help World Action Models? An Empirical Audit of Candidate Selection and Simulator Integrity

When Does Test-Time Search Help World Action Models? An Empirical Audit of Candidate Selection and Simulator Integrity

Jun Liu、Dajie Sun、Zhenglun Kong、Zhenjiang Fan、Peiyan Dong 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World-action models increasingly apply test-time scaling, spending extra inference computation on candidate selection and adaptive allocation to improve control. Such gains are credible only when candidates carry exploitable signal, nominal compute savings become real speedup, and evaluation stays reliable. We audit all three on Cosmos Policy and RoboCasa with a premise-first, pre-registered methodology. On clean data, a learned value selector is 1.66 pp worse than random; a hindsight oracle gains only 1.40 pp, with an episode-level interval spanning zero; and 82% of states show identical candidate outcomes, leaving little selection headroom. Token pruning cuts per-forward FLOPs by up to 47% but yields at most a measured 1.15$\times$ end-to-end speedup on the deployed path (1.25$\times$ on a decode-excluded path), short of a 1.5$\times$ target, and our adaptive-allocation experiment is inconclusive under a power gate. We further identify and fix a silent rendering corruption whose rate rises sharply along compute-heavy planning paths while leaving the official direct-policy result unaffected, and release a detector, fix, and scripts. These findings show why test-time scaling should be audited as a chain of premises (decision signal, realized efficiency, evaluationintegrity) rather than by success rate alone. Conclusions are scoped to the tested configuration.

Closed-Loop Physics-Based Manipulation Evaluation at a Commodity Price: What 4.4 L4 GPU hours at $3.50 Buys You

Closed-Loop Physics-Based Manipulation Evaluation at a Commodity Price: What 4.4 L4 GPU hours at $3.50 Buys You

Rohun Kshirsagar、Diana Rypkema

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Running evaluations of contact-rich robotic manipulation at statistical scale has largely been possible only at well-resourced labs, highlighting the need for affordable and efficient options for broader audiences. We present a reproducible evaluation harness wrapper based on a previous study at 200-rollout scale on serverless commodity GPUs, at a cost of approximately $3.50 USD and 38 minutes of wall-clock per replication (4.4 L4-GPU-hours across eight parallel shards). Using the harness, we report a partially controlled negative result on action chunking with sparse data: three independently trained chunked checkpoints produce zero genuine successes across 780 rollouts, while three single-step checkpoints range from 2.6% to 11.8% success rate. A per-step-replanning control partially isolates chunk prediction, rather than open-loop chunk execution as the mechanism, with the failure localized to the training formulation and multi-step prediction. This work further democratizes physics-based policy development and evaluation through our reusable harness.

Reversible Dynamics, Asymmetric Actions: Parking as a Trajectory-Level Affordance

Reversible Dynamics, Asymmetric Actions: Parking as a Trajectory-Level Affordance

Adam Y. Shavit

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Drivers are often told to back into a tight parking space, not drive in nose first. The advice looks paradoxical, because a slow car is dynamically reversible: reverse its speed and replay the steering backward, and it retraces a feasible path. Played backward, though, reverse-in shows a car driving forward out of the space. Reverse-in and forward-in are different tasks: they share a start position but end facing opposite ways. We separate three clearances between car and obstacles. Static fit $F$ is the clearance the parked car keeps at its best acceptable destination pose. Eventual access $A_\infty$ is the clearance it can keep all the way there with unlimited corrections. Resource-bounded access $A_K$ is the clearance that survives a budget of $K$ reversals, switches between forward and reverse. They satisfy $F\ge A_\infty\ge A_K$. In one fully specified scene (one car, bay, aisle and start), a certified argument proves $A_\infty=F=0.5000$ m for both tasks. In this scene, all certified forward/reverse asymmetry therefore arises at finite $K$: it lies in how many corrections each task needs, not in the clearance either can eventually keep. With one reversal, an explicit path gives reverse-in at least $0.4410$ m clearance. A certified reachability argument caps forward-in's one-reversal clearance at $d_0<0.1720002$ m. With two reversals, forward-in gets at least $0.2833$ m. To keep a clearance strictly above any $d$ with $d_0\le d<0.2833$ m, reverse-in thus needs exactly one reversal and forward-in exactly two when both tasks move forward first. If both move in reverse first, the counts are two and three. The contrast depends on the start position (Section 4.15). Proposed experiments test whether observers distinguish fit, eventual access and budgeted access. They compare these with simpler cues, such as how the turning body sweeps, and learned manoeuvre labels.

Time-Indexed Skill Success Models for Latency-Aware Skill Selection and Timing in Dynamic Manipulation

Time-Indexed Skill Success Models for Latency-Aware Skill Selection and Timing in Dynamic Manipulation

Mohsen Omidi、Maria Chiara Leva、Brian Vaughan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robots that manipulate moving objects act on observations that are outdated when the chosen skill starts, and the best skill can change within a few hundred milliseconds. Skill competence models, however, predict success for a launch at the moment of observation. We propose time-indexed skill success models, which predict whole-task success when a skill is launched after a given delay. Trained on rollouts branched at a grid of launch delays, they let a receding-horizon planner select both the skill and its launch time under fixed or random latency. In a MuJoCo study in which a UR5e captures a sliding puck with three skills, the planner exceeded a static competence model by 2.8 to 6.2 percentage points at 100 to 300 ms of latency, including when that model was evaluated on point or sampled forecasts of the launch state. Across five complete runs (three training seeds and two distribution shifts), all 80 paired 95% intervals against four baselines excluded zero. On delayed launches, the time-indexed model was calibrated (expected calibration error 0.009 against 0.239), and pausing the world during latency overstated success by up to 23 points. On a real UR5e that captures a rolling ball after 0.2 s of injected latency, a static model trained in a hardware-matched simulator predicted 99% success on a prespecified subset of 35 delayed launches that it rated at least 0.8. Of these, 63% succeeded (95% interval 46 to 77%), and the time-indexed model predicted 56%. In this single-skill, predictable regime, a matched physics-based forecast was also well calibrated.

Robots that manipulate moving objects act on observations that are outdated when the chosen skill starts, and the best skill can change within a few hundred milliseconds. Skill competence models, however, predict success for a launch at the moment of observation. We propose time-indexed skill success models, which predict whole-task success when a skill is launched after a given delay. Trained on rollouts branched at a grid of launch delays, they let a receding-horizon planner select both the skill and its launch time under fixed or random latency. In a MuJoCo study in which a UR5e captures a sliding puck with three skills, the planner exceeded a static competence model by 2.8 to 6.2 percentage points at 100 to 300 ms of latency, including when that model was evaluated on point or sampled forecasts of the launch state. Across five complete runs (three training seeds and two distribution shifts), all 80 paired 95% intervals against four baselines excluded zero. On delayed launches, the time-indexed model was calibrated (expected calibration error 0.009 against 0.239), and pausing the world during latency overstated success by up to 23 points. On a real UR5e that captures a rolling ball after 0.2 s of injected latency, a static model trained in a hardware-matched simulator predicted 99% success on a prespecified subset of 35 delayed launches that it rated at least 0.8. Of these, 63% succeeded (95% interval 46 to 77%), and the time-indexed model predicted 56%. In this single-skill, predictable regime, a matched physics-based forecast was also well calibrated.

Symmetry-Aware Surrogate-Assisted Testing of UAVs

Symmetry-Aware Surrogate-Assisted Testing of UAVs

Anas Shrinah、Chris Bennett、Kerstin Eder

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We investigate a symmetry-aware surrogate-assisted testing approach for UAV autopilots. The proposed approach exploits geometric symmetries between flight missions by trans- forming obstacle configurations into a shared canonical coordi- nate frame. This enables a neural-network surrogate trained on a single reference mission to be reused across arbitrary missions, reducing the amount of high-fidelity simulation data required for training. The surrogate model is trained to predict the minimum clearance distance between a UAV and surrounding obstacles and is used to guide test generation towards safety-critical scenarios. We evaluate this approach using the Aerialist UAV test bench and benchmark its performance against techniques from the 2024 to 2026 UAV Testing Competitions. Results show that the approach achieves an average critical-test-case hit rate of 42% across the evaluated missions, compared with approximately 10% for the constrained-random baseline used to generate the training data. This approach also achieves high input diversity and competitive benchmark performance. However, our findings reveal limitations in transferring surrogate predictions across missions with differing flight-path characteristics, highlighting the boundaries of geometric canonicalization. To support reproducibility and future research, we provide the trained surrogate model and a dataset comprising more than 2,500 high-fidelity simulations.

2026-11-05

SCALE: Real-Time Latent Communication for Cyber-Physical Agents

SCALE: Real-Time Latent Communication for Cyber-Physical Agents

Shanghao Shi、Jian Xiao、Ching-Hsiang Chan、Sanjoy Baruah、Sastry Kompella 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Latent communication enables agents to collaborate efficiently by directly exchanging intermediate model representations rather than raw sensor data or text messages. Existing approaches, however, are primarily designed for LLM agents and do not consider the bandwidth constraints and heterogeneous task deadlines faced by cyber-physical agents. In this work, we propose SCALE, a real-time latent communication method tailored for cyber-physical agents. Motivated by the spatio-temporal continuity of the physical world, SCALE matches tokens across consecutive latent messages using similarity scores to identify reusable information and avoid redundant transmission. It then profiles the semantic gain and communication cost of transmitting different amounts of latent information. Based on these profiles, SCALE employs a real-time scheduler that jointly determines which pending messages to transmit and how many latent tokens to send, subject to available bandwidth and task deadlines. We evaluate SCALE across three cyber-physical benchmarks and a physical spider robot case study under diverse network conditions and heterogeneous task deadlines. Our results show that SCALE significantly improves both task performance and on-time delivery in bandwidth-constrained networks. Our code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/SCALE-4E80/.

REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals

REACT: Towards Real-Time Completion of Skeletal Motion Signals

P. de Clermont Gallerande、Q. Avril、P. Gosselin、F. Argelaguet、L. Hoyet 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Tracking user motion in interactive and immersive applications is increasingly common (e.g., video games, XR systems, smartphones). Yet, processing and transmitting human motion data remain critical challenges, as current approaches strongly rely on the quality of the tracked motion signals. In general, affordable motion-capture systems may produce unnatural animations in the presence of calibration errors, occlusions, sensor drift, high-frequency noise, or other artifacts. More specifically, we simulate occlusions and high-frequency noise and propose to mitigate their effects with REACT, an interactive online motion-completion framework operating causally, frame by frame, without look-ahead. REACT is an S4-based autoregressive model that coordinates complementary motion estimates under specific reliability-dependent constraints and exposes several interpretable internal signals. We present a system-level contribution consisting of a structured formulation of interactive motion completion together with a coherent instantiation of this formulation. We evaluate REACT against several causal baselines and architectural variants under varying levels of occlusion severity, and report their respective inference latencies. Our results show that REACT achieves a relevant compromise between reconstruction accuracy, update smoothness, and interactive inference. Finally, we characterize how its fusion behaviour changes under missing input observations.

Fault-Geometry-Aware Control Allocation for Emergency Quadrotor Landing under Single-Motor Loss of Effectiveness

Fault-Geometry-Aware Control Allocation for Emergency Quadrotor Landing under Single-Motor Loss of Effectiveness

Suhas S P、Samridhi Salwan、Arshan Ali Khan、Ujwal Pai Jakarbet、Savithramma R M 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Partial single-motor loss of effectiveness (LoE) changes the remaining control-authority geometry of a quadrotor and can change which control allocator performs best during emergency landing[cite: 11]. We compare three allocators in Crazyflie software-in-the-loop simulation: bounded weighted least squares (reported as PINV), a finite-candidate residual allocator (QP-lite), and the same residual allocator with scoring parameters tuned offline by the Cross-Entropy Method (CEM)[cite: 11]. For this study, fault geometry is represented by the known failed-motor index[cite: 11]. At $η=0.496$ remaining effectiveness, matched development trials select CEM for M1, M3, and M4 and PINV for M2; the M3 selection is a one-trial margin (29/30 versus 28/30)[cite: 11]. After freezing this routing map, the policy achieves 117/120 safe first-contact touchdowns on fresh seeds and 115/120 in a separate randomized-order integrated-supervisor evaluation[cite: 11]. For the critical M2/PINV branch, Firth regression gives a nominal $η_{50}\approx0.4956$ with a within-model 95% bootstrap interval of approximately [0.4955, 0.4956][cite: 11]. One-factor-at-a-time $\pm10\%$ plant perturbations shift $η_{50}$ by up to 0.05495 for thrust coefficient and 0.04974 for mass; the most frequent violated first-contact criterion also changes from vertical speed in many conditions to angular rate for mass -10% and thrust-coefficient +10%[cite: 11]. At fixed $η=0.496$, PINV has a higher M2 safe count than frozen CEM in 9/11 tested plant states and ties in 2/11; the two ties are both 0/30[cite: 11]. The experiments therefore distinguish motor-conditioned allocator selection from sensitivity to plant-model mismatch[cite: 11].

Not Losing Your Way: Mitigating Visual-Inertial Odometry Drift through On-Demand Visual Feature Creation

Not Losing Your Way: Mitigating Visual-Inertial Odometry Drift through On-Demand Visual Feature Creation

Simone D'Angelo、Gianmarco Corrado、Federico Esposito、Vincenzo Scognamiglio、Fabio Ruggiero 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual-inertial odometry (VIO) can degrade during aerial inspection in repetitive or poorly textured environments. We propose an active environment-modification framework for unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) relying on cameras and an inertial measurement unit only, without prior maps or additional localization sensors. Inspired by the fairy tale of Tom Thumb, the UAV creates visual features on demand by releasing ground markers or marking walls through physical contact. An information-based indicator triggers interventions using temporal confirmation and hysteresis. Evaluation includes three simulated environments and a 900-run factorial campaign. Lower cruise speed and active marking are associated with reduced divergence, while accuracy among non-divergent runs depends on speed-strategy interactions. Corridor experiments, with five runs per configuration, use OptiTrack for flight control while VIO-based marking decisions remain active online. The combined framework reduces the reported position root mean square error by approximately 35% relative to baseline VIO, supporting active feature creation for drift mitigation.

GraspBench: Execution-Aware Cross-Robot Evaluation of 6-DoF Grasp Generation in MuJoCo

GraspBench: Execution-Aware Cross-Robot Evaluation of 6-DoF Grasp Generation in MuJoCo

Abdul Basit、Bibek Poudel、Muhammad Shafique

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern 6-DoF grasp generators are often evaluated using grasp scores, dataset-level metrics, or single-robot demonstrations, yet proposal quality alone does not determine whether a grasp is executable by a particular robot. We present GraspBench, a controlled MuJoCo protocol for evaluating complete grasp-generation pipelines after robot-specific inverse-kinematics filtering, motion planning, and fixed approach-close-lift execution. GraspBench evaluates six configured pipelines across four robot embodiments, five diagnostic objects, and a structured tabletop pose grid, yielding 540 scenarios per method and 3,240 method-scenario evaluations in total. Missing outputs are conservatively counted as failures, and success follows the binary lift criterion produced by each method's execution runner. GraspGen achieves the highest overall success rate (73.1%), followed by EconomicGrasp (63.5%), while robot- and object-conditioned analyses reveal substantial variation across embodiments and object geometries. GPG produces the largest native candidate sets but achieves 44.3% success, indicating that proposal volume alone does not guarantee robot-executable grasps. Cluster-aware paired intervals support the principal method differences while not estimating repeated-execution uncertainty. The results characterize complete configured pipelines on fixed scenarios rather than intrinsic model quality or stochastic repeatability, showing that grasping performance depends jointly on the generator, robot embodiment, scene, and execution protocol.

Whole-Body Elasto-Geometrical Calibration of Wheeled Humanoid Robots Using a Shared-Waist Dual-Chain Model

Whole-Body Elasto-Geometrical Calibration of Wheeled Humanoid Robots Using a Shared-Waist Dual-Chain Model

Pengxin Zha、Yi Lu、Haojun Ma、Jun Zhu、Minghao Qiu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Wheeled humanoid robots carry both arms on one actuated waist, usually without torque sensors, so the accuracy of both end-effectors depends on a single waist model. After a first stage that estimates the tracker and target extrinsics, we identify the waist and both arms in one problem from the target positions on both flanges, measured by an optical tracker. The kinematic model is complete, with waist-base, parallel-axis, and branch parameters; arm compliances are driven by measured torques and waist compliances by gravity torques of the robot's mass model; and Gaussian priors with evidence-maximizing scales stabilize the estimate. Independent and sequential single-chain calibration are restrictions of this problem, and its waist information is the sum of the single-chain Schur complements. On two robots with different waists, each without and with a payload, calibration reduces the mean errors on held-out poses from 11.3-31.1 mm to 0.50-1.11 mm. For every training-set size from 20 poses to all, the method has 17-63% lower mean errors than adaptations of two published whole-body calibration methods, mainly through the waist compliance. Independent calibration yields two waist models; using either for both arms raises the other arm's error to up to 3.21 mm.

2026-11-05

SLA: Scaling Spatial-Language-Action Model via Continual Pre-training

SLA: Scaling Spatial-Language-Action Model via Continual Pre-training

Fuhao Li、Wenxuan Song、Qizhou Huang、Ziyang Zhou、Jingbo Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reliable robotic manipulation requires both semantic understanding and precise control in three-dimensional space. However, vision-language-action (VLA) foundations largely inherit visual representations from 2D vision-language pre-training, while spatial supervision is often introduced only during task-specific adaptation. Developing spatial understanding as a transferable foundation-model capability therefore calls for learning from broader robot experience. We present SLA, a Spatial-Language-Action Model that scales implicit spatial alignment to continual pre-training and maintains it during downstream post-training. Initialized from a pretrained pi05 policy, SLA jointly optimizes action prediction and alignment between intermediate visual embeddings and spatial representations from a frozen 3D perception model. We curate and unify heterogeneous robot datasets to support spatial learning across diverse embodiments, tasks, and scenes. To accommodate this diversity, we introduce feature-dependent gates that adaptively aggregate visual embeddings from multiple VLA layers for spatial supervision. The teacher further exploits partially available depth and camera calibration alongside RGB observations, enabling effective use of heterogeneous geometric annotations. Probabilistic state dropout encourages reliance on visual cues and mitigates overfitting to demonstration trajectories. All auxiliary components are used only during training, allowing direct reuse of pretrained VLA weights and preserving the original inference architecture.

2026-11-05

AffordJEV: Affordance Grounding by Confirming Candidate Regions

AffordJEV: Affordance Grounding by Confirming Candidate Regions

Jincheng Li、Yutian Zhang、Jianyu Zhang、Xinshun Wang、Mengyuan Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Affordance grounding asks which visible part of an object performs a requested operation, a basic capability for embodied manipulation and human--robot interaction. Most existing methods follow a System Two paradigm: a multimodal language model first writes a chain of thought and then turns its conclusion into a box or points for a segmenter. This incurs considerable inference cost, gives the semantic decision little direct control over the extent of the final region, and makes mistakes hard to correct. Yet locating an affordance is essentially a perceptual judgment that need not be argued out in words. We therefore propose, in which frozen segmenters propose candidate regions and a System One model decides directly which of them perform the operation, generating no text and reading each decision from a single forward pass. Our contributions are twofold. (1) An inference framework that confirms candidate regions level by level, from object to part to sub-part, with evidence passed between the levels in both directions so that the levels correct one another. (2) A System One model trained for this framework: existing System One models are built for general-purpose decisions, judge one candidate per request and are ill-suited to the framework's region questions, so ours learns directly from the requests the framework itself produces and judges all candidates of a level at once. Trained only on InstructPart and evaluated with definition-aligned operation words, outperforms chain-of-thought methods in domain and in zero-shot transfer to UMD and IIT-AFF, is better overall than existing System One models run in the same framework, and needs fewer judge requests. To our knowledge, is the first approach to bring System One models to affordance grounding.

2026-11-05

Embodied RAG++: Retrieval-Augmented Generation From What Exists to What Works in Embodied Scenes

Embodied RAG++: Retrieval-Augmented Generation From What Exists to What Works in Embodied Scenes

Fan Yang、Tatsuya Kikuzuki、Jun Liang、Jun Liu、Wenkai Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

To serve a request in a human environment, a robot system must find an object that satisfies a functional requirement and a robot that can feasibly reach it. Scene graphs record which objects exist and where, but they often omit capabilities documented only in product manuals. Retrieval-augmented generation methods can read such manuals, yet they rarely reason jointly over object capabilities, physical instances, robot states, and dynamic connectivity. We introduce E2W-Bench, the Exists-to-Works Benchmark, which links dynamic hybrid scene graphs with real product manuals to evaluate capability-grounded robot--object assignment under availability, mobility, vertical-reach, and connector constraints. We also present Embodied RAG++, a spatially grounded retrieval framework that scopes retrieval to the manuals linked to candidate objects, caches extracted capabilities per product model, and resolves robot--object feasibility through deterministic spatial reasoning. On E2W-Bench, Embodied RAG++ reaches a pair accuracy of 0.90 with Qwen2.5-32B and 1.00 with Llama3.3-70B, compared with at most 0.30 and 0.55 for the baselines, while requiring the shortest preprocessing time among the retrieval-based methods and tying for the lowest total token count.

目标检测/分割机器人视觉
2026-11-05

Training-Independent Validation of a Learning-Based Controller for Stunt Maneuvers

Training-Independent Validation of a Learning-Based Controller for Stunt Maneuvers

Kiruthiga Chandra Shekar、Aliasghar Arab、Jingang Yi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Learned controllers for vehicle maneuvers are usually validated under the conditions and objective used to train them, where they appear highly reliable, yet the parameters that change on real roads are often not those the model was trained on. We argue that validation must be independent of training: it should vary physical parameters that training kept fixed and use acceptance criteria absent from the training objective. A learning-based control policy, G-Maneuver, with 90.11% success is tested for stunt-level maneuvers. A looser terminal-heading score ranked the worst setting as the best. Because real stunt tests are safety-critical, a simulation-to-reality-to-simulation (Sim2Real2Sim) loop uses the simulation to select safe zones for real-vehicle trials with 16.9% acceptance. Automotive safety analysis frameworks are used to identify parameters for validation using coverage tests. We demonstrated that the kinematic stunt-driving indicators, namely heading change, sideslip, and speed-normalized yaw rate, match between simulation and the real world, while force and roll indicators do not, which identifies the indicators that can support safe deployment. A video of the experiments is available at https://youtu.be/dN1Oes8pNIo.

We argue that validation must be independent of training: it should vary physical parameters that training kept fixed and use acceptance criteria absent from the training objective. A learning-based control policy, G-Maneuver, with 90.11% success is tested for stunt-level maneuvers. A looser terminal-heading score ranked the worst setting as the best. Because real stunt tests are safety-critical, a simulation-to-reality-to-simulation (Sim2Real2Sim) loop uses the simulation to select safe zones for real-vehicle trials with 16.9% acceptance. Automotive safety analysis frameworks are used to identify parameters for validation using coverage tests. We demonstrated that the kinematic stunt-driving indicators, namely heading change, sideslip, and speed-normalized yaw rate, match between simulation and the real world, while force and roll indicators do not, which identifies the indicators that can support safe deployment. A video of the experiments is available at https://youtu.be/dN1Oes8pNIo.

目标检测/分割视频理解机器人视觉
2026-11-05

STEP: Spatiotemporal Training with Explicit Prediction for Perceptive Humanoid Locomotion

STEP: Spatiotemporal Training with Explicit Prediction for Perceptive Humanoid Locomotion

Youhao Lyu、Chenghao Sun、Zhenxuan Ding、Shuting Zhang、Ziyang Dong 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

For perceptive humanoid locomotion, the relevance of terrain geometry depends on the robot's body state and how both evolve under its actions. Existing work has advanced terrain-aware sensory fusion and cross-modal prediction, improving how visual and proprioceptive information is combined and related. However, feature aggregation alone does not specify which cross-modal relationships should be learned, while prediction between simultaneous observations does not explicitly constrain their action-dependent evolution. We present Spatiotemporal Training with Explicit Prediction (STEP), a one-stage framework that jointly supervises cross-modal relationships and action-dependent state evolution within a shared control representation. Multiple learned queries aggregate depth and proprioceptive tokens into a compact policy state. Bidirectional masked prediction uses each visible modality to predict latent features of the omitted modality, explicitly supervising terrain-body relationships. An action-conditioned predictor maps the current policy state and executed action to the next fused state, with an exponential-moving-average target defined directly in the policy representation space. Simulation comparisons show overall improvements in terrain traversal over evaluated fusion alternatives and ablations, while depth-dropout and delay experiments demonstrate the benefits of both predictive objectives under degraded visual input. Deployed on a 23-DoF Noetix E1 humanoid with onboard sensing, STEP succeeds in 98 of 100 hardware trials across stairs, a 40\,cm platform, a 70\,cm gap, stepping stones, and backward stair traversal without rearward terrain visibility.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉机器人视觉
2026-11-05

Development of Anthropomorphic Arm for collaborative and Home Service Robot CHARMIE

Development of Anthropomorphic Arm for collaborative and Home Service Robot CHARMIE

Hafiz Adil、Tiago Ribeiro、Antonio Ribeiro、Carolina Lopes、Joana Coelho 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Service Robots are becoming a reality and are progressing by leaps and bounds. Alongside this avenue of robotics is also the field of prosthetics, which is evolving rapidly. These topics are hot cakes in the industry and are widely developed. Some have one quality, and some have others, but are making a shoreline towards success. The development of arm and hand as individual or as a package is becoming common as more and more advanced collaborative robots come to the surface and join their human counterparts in the real world. This dissertation presents an open-source 3D-printed arm which was assembled and programmed on two fronts. The first is the control of the hand by utilising teleoperation with a camera and ML-based hand pose estimation. Secondly, it is the control via kinematics utilising the Denavit-Hartenberg method to define the motion and to find the position of the end effector in 3D space.
